# Playtest Platform: how the app works, page by page

Oct 30, 2026 · @Mhanna112

## What Playtest is

Playtest is a marketplace where developers pay testers to try their games, websites, apps and other products. A developer posts a test: what to do, the parts to send (written answers, a recording, live calls), the price of each part, Open until (the last day to join) and who can join. Every tester gets the same number of days to work, counted from the day they join. Testers join or apply, send their work and are paid for each part they send: the developer pays any amount from $0 up, and the tester can dispute the amount once. At the end both sides rate each other.

- **Developers** post and run tests, invite testers, pay for work, and rate testers.
- **Testers** find and join tests, send work, take calls, get paid, and rate developers.
- **Admins** suspend and reinstate either side of an account, pause or stop tests, decide disputes, and review reports and payment holds.

One account can be a developer, a tester or both; a switch in the header changes sides (Shared pages). With both, the two sides look like two separate accounts: only the switch button (the header's, or the Account page's "Switch to your … side") changes sides. Opening a page of the other side (a link, a bell line, an email link, a typed address, another tab) never does: that page shows only "This page is on your tester side." (or developer) with the button "Switch to your tester side", which switches and then opens it; the header stays on the side in use. Coming back from Stripe leaves the side as it is. Old /app/switch links only open the page asked for. Payments, Account, Notifications, Messages and the bell show only the side in use's content (batch 34 §5). An admin account is only an admin, with no developer or tester side (Administrator console).

**The main promise:** every press that touches money, joining, permissions or agreed terms is checked on the server against what is true right now. Nobody is charged twice, loses pay they earned because an account changed, accepts terms they didn't see, or sees a success that didn't happen.

## How to read this guide

- One tab per page, in the order people meet them: Developer dashboard, Post a test, Developer test page, Edit test, Tester dashboard, Find tests and the join page, Tester session page, Shared pages and Administrator console.
- Each page uses the same headings, skipping empty ones: what it's for and who sees it; what's on it; each button, and what it changes elsewhere; what can stop a press; how it looks in each state; the bell lines that open it.
- Every page explains once, with examples, what holds everywhere: notices, how state stays consistent (with a state map of tests, accounts, parts and calls), long lists, pop-ups, suspensions and holds, the bell and emails.
- Rules holds every number, cap and timer: test lifecycle, money, ratings, holds and suspensions, limits.
- Security and launch says, in plain words, what the pre-launch security fixes mean for people.
- QA and open questions lists the edge cases to test, the decisions still open and post-launch ideas.
- Testers never see the words hold, suspended, failed or chargeback about a developer. They see "paused" and "delayed".

## Every page

These rules hold on every signed-in page. Each page tab says which of them apply there and links back here.

### Notices

- **Blue:** something changed, or a press didn't go through. It sits right under the button that was pressed, and the page scrolls just enough to show it. It always names the press first: "Your Pay didn't go through."
- **Red:** a mistake in a field (checked when you leave the field and when you submit), a deleted account, and the banner on a paused or stopped test page.
- **Mistakes in a form** follow one rule on every form with fields (Post, Edit, posting a draft, profile and settings, sign up, log in, reset password, Contact us, the dispute and rating boxes, a call request):
  - Each mistake shows at its field: the box red, with a red line under it saying what's wrong. The page's own check and the server use the same words.
  - By the button (under it; on Post and Edit just above it): "Fix the mistake above, then press <button> again." (or "Fix the 2 mistakes above, …") and one bullet per field that is red right now, with the same words as the field's line. Each bullet is a link that goes to its field and puts the cursor in it.
  - On the press, the page scrolls to the first red field and puts the cursor in it.
  - A field stays red until its own value is fixed (once red, it's checked again as you type; editing another field never clears it), and its bullet goes at the same moment. When the last one is fixed, the list goes.
  - A server mistake that belongs to no field stays in the list without a link. A server line the form already shows red at its own field (the calls cap under Request a time) is shown and counted once. Refusals that aren't mistakes stay blue.

  [video: Post with two mistakes (Mature "idk" and an empty title): the press goes to the title, both are red and listed; fixing the title removes its bullet, the Mature bullet goes to its box, and the list goes once it's fixed.]
- **Yellow:** a list that needs attention, or a warning that never blocks anything (a reused access key, money left for calls).
- **Green:** a confirmation, e.g. "Saved."
- A failed press never shows success. It says why, keeps what was typed, and updates the page to what is true now.
- **No connection:** a press made offline shows "You seem to be offline. Check your connection and try again." under the button, and the typed text stays.

  [image: No connection: "You seem to be offline. Check your connection and try again." under the button; the typed answers stay.]

### How state stays consistent

- **The server checks every press.** Before doing anything it re-checks what is true now: the tester's place in the test (working, left, ended, rated), the test (open, paused, stopped), holds, suspensions, blocks, spots, Open until, deadlines and limits. More in Why an old tab can't act on stale information.
- **Live box.** Changes made by other people appear in a blue box at the top of the page as they happen: "Updated · 3:10 PM: Ana sent their written answers", newest first, with Dismiss. Your own changes made in another tab or device show there too, as "You … in another tab" lines ("You paid Ana Silva $10.00 for the written answers in another tab"), and raise the pill like any update (Why a press stops: Your own changes in another tab).
  - Nothing moves on its own. Every update that arrives while the box is out of view raises a small floating pill at the bottom of the screen, "1 new update ↑", whether or not anything is being typed, and it counts each new one ("3 new updates ↑"), e.g. a call request that arrives while the tester is typing in the Dispute box. Its arrow points where the box is: "↑" when it's above the screen, "↓" when it's below (on Edit the box sits under Save, so it can be below), and it follows the box as the page scrolls. Pressing the pill scrolls to the box and the pill goes; it also goes once the top of the box comes into view. Nothing is raised when the box is already in view. The new line flashes. With reduced motion turned on, the pill's scroll is a jump without the animation. This holds on every page with the live box: the developer's test page, Edit, the tester's session page, the join page and the dashboards.
  - Every page's poll starts from what the server drew the page with, so a change that lands between the drawing and the first poll (a new invite, a payment) still shows on that poll: the dashboards, the join page, Payments, the test page, the session page and chat.

  [video: The developer is at the bottom of the test page when Ben sends their answers: the page doesn't move; the pill "1 new update ↑" shows, and pressing it scrolls up to the live box, where the new line flashes.]

  [image: Ana is typing when the developer pays them: the page doesn't move; a small pill "1 new update ↑" shows instead.]
- **Stop-once.** The first press after a change you haven't seen stops once. A blue box under the button reads, top to bottom: "Your <button> didn't go through." (e.g. "Your Pay on Ana Silva's written answers didn't go through."), "Since you opened this page:" and the changes with their times, the Alerts lines, and last, in bold, "Press <button> again to go ahead." The next press goes ahead unless something newer happened. Rate is narrower: it stops once only for changes about that person and your shared work (Why a press stops: Rate). The same rules hold on a stopped, closed or paused test, after a tester's work ended, and while the developer is held or suspended; the stop, close or pause itself is a change like any other, so a page opened before it stops once naming it (Stopped tests). Every kind of change, for developers and testers, and the kinds that never stop: Why a press stops.
  - Example: the developer presses Pay on Ana's answers just as Ana disputes their recording. Pay stops once with "Ana disputed the pay for their recording".
- **Always refused.** A change that makes the press impossible refuses it every time, not once: the test stopped, the work ended, the request was already taken, the offer was replaced. A refusal names the press too: "Your Pay didn't go through: <reason>."
  - Example: "Ben stopped the test on Oct 12. Work you sent is still reviewed." When the team stopped it, testers never read who: "This test was stopped at 3:10 PM. Work you sent is still reviewed."
  - **A refused press redraws the page at once,** on every page and both sides: when the server refuses a press because the state changed (a block, a stop, a pause, an ended session or call, a closed request, already paid…), the page redraws right away, the same as its poll, without a full reload and keeping typed text and open boxes, so the buttons that no longer work go and the new state shows. Example: a Join call pressed on a page opened before a block is refused ("Your Join call didn't go through: this call isn't available.") and Join call, "Call in progress / measuring" and the minutes box go at once. A stop-once box and the new-terms block don't redraw (nothing was refused for good), and nothing redraws while the bank's check is open.
  - **The refusal line stays where the button was.** When the redraw takes the pressed button away (Join call after a block, Pay after another tab paid, Charge after the developer paid, Withdraw on a request that closed…), its line stays in that spot as a small blue line, "Your Join call didn't go through: this call isn't available.", until the next press anywhere on the page. While the button, or its own line under it, is still on the page, only that one shows.
  - A block, either way, also changes what the session page's own poll watches, so Join call, Request a time and the call requests go on that poll too, without any press.
- **Your other tab.** A press you already made in another tab on the same thing is refused plainly, with no stop box: "You already rated Ana Silva.", "You already allowed this excerpt.". Your other changes in another tab or device stop every press on the page once, as "You … in another tab" lines (for the developer on the test page and Edit; for the tester on the session page and the join page); Rate and the excerpt presses only race with the same press. The full lists are in Why a press stops (Your own changes in another tab).
- **Red wins.** Nothing saves while a field is red.
- **Nothing happens twice.** If a press reached the server but its answer was lost (the connection dropped), pressing again never does it twice: Pay, Pay more, Pay what you owe, Charge, Let it go, Send work, Join, Apply, Accept, Book a call, Rate and Allow or Deny either answer "already done" or act once. Each payment carries a one-time key, so a repeated press returns the first result instead of charging again. A chat message carries one too: a resend of the same unconfirmed message posts once, while "Hello" sent twice on purpose posts twice.
- **Payments that never answered.** If the payment service doesn't answer, the press says so: "We didn't hear back from the payment service, so this payment is being checked. Within 15 minutes it shows as paid, or it's back here to pay again. Don't pay it twice: it's never charged twice." After 15 minutes the app checks it: if it went through, it's finished as normal; if it didn't, the piece is back in Needs you with Pay, and nobody gets a bell. A new press on that piece within 24 hours reuses the same one-time key.
- **Old tabs and Back.** Back and Forward show the saved page, then refresh it from the server; nothing on a restored page is trusted. More in Why going Back is safe.
- **Never reloaded under someone's typing.** A page follows its poll by redrawing in place. A full reload is only a last resort (a refresh that didn't take), and it never happens while a field has focus or holds text typed since the page loaded, or while a pop-up or inline box is open (Invite anyway, a stop box, End early, Stop, a dispute, a rating, a charge…). It waits until nothing would be lost; meanwhile a quiet button at the bottom right reads "This page has updates · Refresh". The forms that matter also keep what was typed across any reload (Drafts, numbers and ranges, below).
- **Cross-tab sign-in.** Signing in as someone else switches every other tab of that browser to the new account, on the page the sign-in landed on. Signing out in one tab signs out every tab and sends them to the sign-in page. A press racing the switch is refused ("You're signed in as someone else in another tab. This page is switching to that account.") and that tab switches.
- **Renames** never stop a press, and nothing is added under it. Every change of the product name or the test name is recorded in each working tester's contract changes (Developer test page, Tester session page).

### State map

Each object's states, and where people see them.

**Test**

| State | Meaning | Where it shows |
|---|---|---|
| Draft | A next round not opened yet | Developer dashboard card "Draft". It isn't a test page: it opens in Post a test, filled in, where Post the test opens it for testers. Not in Find tests; for testers its link is not found. |
| Open | Takes testers until Open until passes or it's full | Developer dashboard card: "Accepting testers". Find tests (unless it's invite only) and the join page (Join, Apply); testers' session pages work normally. |
| Full | No spots left | Developer dashboard card: "Full". Not in Find tests. Join page: "This test is full." Invited testers: "<test> is full right now. Your invite stays open; we'll tell you when a spot opens." |
| Joining closed | Open until passed; testers who joined keep working until their own deadlines | Developer card and test page: "Joining closed · testers working until <latest deadline>". Not in Find tests; the join page says "This test closed to new testers on <date>."; waiting applications and open invites close. It's done when the last working tester's deadline passes or everyone has finished. It no longer counts toward the open tests (DEFAULT). |
| Paused | An admin paused it, or its developer is on a payment or chargeback hold, or their developer side is suspended. It closes after 7 days. | Developer dashboard card: "Paused: account suspended / payment hold / chargeback hold / paused by the team". Whatever the cause, the test page opens in the paused view: the red banner, the overview with Stop the test and End early, Booked calls with Cancel, excerpt requests and disputes (Developer test page). Testers: "This test is paused right now." and a "Paused" tag on their dashboard; their session page hides what doesn't work right now (Send, the call-request tools and Join call) under a blue banner that lists what still works; applications wait, and open invites close (Tester dashboard). When it lifts, deadlines and Open until move later by the length of the pause. Not in Find tests; others opening the link see "This test isn't available right now." |
| Stopped | Stopped by the developer (with a reason testers read) or an admin, or its developer was deleted | "Stopped" label on both sides; the developer's red banner and the tester's still-allowed line (below). Testers get a live-box line and a bell line. Pay, Pay more, Charge, Let it go and Rate end at the test's close-out (Rules: Close-out). |
| Closed | Closed after 7 days paused, or an admin pressed Close now | Like Stopped. Card: "Closed after 7 days paused". Testers read "This test ended on <date>." with no reason; the developer gets the reason by bell and a banner. |

**Account**

| State | Where it shows |
|---|---|
| Active | Everything works. For 3 days after reinstatement the dashboard shows "Your account is active again" (on a mixed account, "Your developer account…" or "Your tester account…"). |
| Email not confirmed | A strip: "Confirm your email: we sent a link to <email>". Post a test, Join, Apply and accepting an invite are refused (below). |
| Payment hold (developer) | Every signed-in developer page except the dashboard, Payments, Settings, the bell list and the paused view of their test pages moves to the dashboard top, with the blue box. All their tests are paused. |
| Chargeback hold (developer) | The same, plus the three chargeback steps on Payments. |
| Developer side suspended | The same, for the developer side only. Their tester side works as normal. |
| Tester side suspended | Their tester pages close and move them to the dashboard top; removed from every test; developers see "Left this test". Each test they were removed from opens in a limited view where Dispute is the only press (Tester session page). Their developer side works as normal. |
| Deleted | Every open tab logs out with a red "This account no longer exists". Others see "Deleted account". |

**Part (a piece of work in a tester's contract)**

| State | Where it shows |
|---|---|
| Not sent | Session page "Your parts"; tester dashboard To do. |
| Sent, waiting for review | Developer: the piece in Needs you (one green Pay) and the dashboard's Needs review. Tester: "Waiting for the developer". "Sent late (deadline was <date>)" if sent after that tester's own deadline: late work never opens Charge, so only the developer's Pay settles it. |
| Paid | Tester: "Paid" and "Paid so far $Y"; at $0: "Paid $0: "<reason>"". Developer: a receipt. The tester can send an updated version: below the piece's cap it comes back to the developer's Needs you with Pay more; at the cap it's only a quiet line (Rules: Money). |
| Closed by the team | The team closed it at $0 with a reason (abuse cases: Administrator console). Tester: "The team closed this for $0: “<reason>”". Final: no dispute, no Pay more, no update. |
| Payment delayed | The developer's card was declined (a hold starts). Tester: "Payment may be delayed". |
| Waiting for the bank | The developer's bank wants them to confirm the payment. On their own press the bank's check opens inline, under the button; a charge made while they weren't there waits 3 days for "Confirm with your bank" under that piece on the test page (listed at the top of the developer dashboard meanwhile), then counts as declined. While a press waits for the bank the piece is locked: a Charge or Let it go is refused with "A payment for this is being confirmed right now." Tester: "Payment may be delayed". |
| Rejected (older work only) | There is no Reject any more. Work rejected before it was removed still reads "Rejected" with the reason, and can still be disputed once. |
| Charge open | At the review date (the later of that tester's own deadline and 7 days after sending), for work sent by that deadline. Tester: Charge or Let it go; the dashboard's "Charge or cancel". Developer: can still Pay until the tester chooses. Only written answers and recordings: never for an unlisted part, and never for a call (a listed call is charged automatically instead: Call, below). It lasts until the piece closes (at least 14 days: Rules, Close-out). |
| Closed unpaid | Nothing was paid, charged or let go by the piece's closing date (Rules, Close-out). It stays unpaid. Developer: the row leaves Needs you, and the piece reads "Closed on <date>: not paid.". Tester: "Not paid (closed on <date>)". |
| Charged | The tester charged up to the listing. During a payment or chargeback hold it's added to what the developer owes and paid when they pay; the tester sees "Charged $X. Payment may be delayed until the test resumes." Developer sees "<name> charged $X for this." |
| Let go | Closed with nothing more paid. |
| Disputed | Any part the developer paid (any amount, $0 included; unlisted parts too; an older rejection too) can be disputed once. Developer: the dispute in Needs you; admin: Open disputes. Decided: both are told. |
| Can't be paid | The developer deleted their account while the work was still under review. Tester: "Can't be paid: <dev> deleted their account". |

**Call**

| State | Where it shows |
|---|---|
| Requested | The Call requests panel in both sides' Live session (Accept, Decline, Withdraw); the developer's Needs you rows open it. Two requests for the same time book at once ("Booked: you both picked <time>"). |
| Booked | Both Live session panels and both dashboards' Calls boxes: timer, Add to calendar, Cancel ▾, and "Check your call setup" (Call page). A reminder comes 15 minutes before (The bell). |
| Joinable | From 10 minutes before the start to 30 minutes after the booked end: Join call appears wherever the call is listed and opens the call room (Call page). |
| In progress | Once it has started and until its length is final: "Call in progress / measuring". In the developer's paused view and on the row of a tester who is no longer working, the badge reads "In progress" once the two browsers have connected, and "Waiting to connect" until then. The app measures the time both browsers were connected. The server ends it for both at 150% of the booked length, at the $1,000 for that tester's calls, or when a pause, a block, a suspension of either side, a leave, End early or a Stop happens; then nobody can rejoin (Call page). |
| Cancelled | By either side; by a pause, stop, end early, leave or block before it starts; by an outage; by nobody joining by halfway; or because the two browsers never connected (Call page: Strict networks). Join disappears; a late press reads "Your Join call didn't go through: <name> cancelled this call at <time>." and the row leaves the upcoming lists. |
| Took place | The length is final once the server ended it, once both have left, or 30 minutes after the latest allowed end (Call page): "Length: 29 min (both in the call 2:21–2:50 PM)". The developer pays; nobody changes the length. A listed call the developer hasn't paid 7 days after it is charged automatically for its measured length × the contract rate (Rules: Money). Either side can report a problem. |
| Over | Every call that's over (took place, cancelled, nobody joined, missed because of a pause or outage, skipped) moves out of Live session: to the tester's row ("Calls (N)") for the developer, and to "Call history" for the tester, each with its outcome badge. |

### Sign-in and accounts

- Sign up with email and password (or with Google, when it's switched on), confirm being 18 or older, and agree to the Terms of Use (and to each later version: Terms of Use, Shared pages), then log in. Forgot password sends a link that works once and signs out every device. Changing the password signs out other devices. "Sign out of all devices" in Settings signs the account out everywhere, this device included.
- **18 or older.** Sign-up has a required tick next to the Terms agreement, "I'm 18 or older." Unticked, Sign up is refused, red at the tick: "You must be 18 or older to use Playtest." The server refuses the same. Every missing tick is red at once: with both the Terms agreement and the age tick left empty, both are red ("Agree to the Terms of Use to sign up" and "You must be 18 or older to use Playtest."), each red line under its whole tick row, never between the tick and its words. When the age was confirmed is kept with the account. Accounts made before this rule aren't asked (nothing has launched).
- **Signing up with Google:** after Google answers for a new account, one short step, "Finish signing up", comes before the account exists: "Signing up with Google as <email>.", the Terms agreement and the same "I'm 18 or older." tick, then Continue. Nothing is created until both are ticked; every missing one is red at once, its line under the whole tick row ("Agree to the Terms of Use to sign up", "You must be 18 or older to use Playtest."). The step waits 30 minutes; after that it reads "This Google sign-up expired. Continue with Google again." with "Back to sign up". An existing account signs in with Google straight away.
- **Signing in** says only "Wrong email or password" when anything is wrong: no account, a deleted account, an account made with Google (no password), or a wrong password. It never says which. When Google sign-in is on, the form adds "Signed up with Google? Use Continue with Google."

  [image: One message for every wrong sign-in: "Wrong email or password".]
- **Too many tries.** 5 failed tries for one email in 15 minutes, or 20 from one network, pause sign-in for that email or network: "Too many tries. Wait 15 minutes or reset your password." This applies even when the password is right. At most 10 new accounts can be made from one network in an hour ("Too many new accounts from here. Try again in an hour.").

  [image: After several wrong passwords: "Too many tries. Wait 15 minutes or reset your password."]
- **Forgot your password?** always shows the same answer, whatever the email, so it never reveals who has an account. An account with no password (made with Google) gets "Your account has no password yet. Choose one here:" with the same link. At most 10 reset requests from one network an hour ("Too many reset requests from here. Try again in an hour."). Sign-up keeps "An account with that email already exists".
- Email confirmation is needed for Post a test, Join, Apply, accepting an invite and payout setup. A blocked press shows, under the button: "Confirm your email first. We sent a link to <email>." with "Resend the link". Payments says "Confirm your email first: payouts go only to a confirmed account. We sent you a link." The server checks too.
- **Signing in with Google to an account whose email isn't confirmed yet** makes it safe first: the account's password is cleared, every device is signed out, and any payout setup is dropped. Then the email counts as confirmed. So nobody can make an account with someone else's email and keep it once the real owner signs in.
- On a local setup with confirmation switched off (SKIP_EMAIL_CONFIRM=1), a strip under the header says "Email confirmation is off for testing." It never shows in production.
- Names need at least 1 character, nothing else.
- Deleted account: every open tab logs out with a red "This account no longer exists".

  [image: Kai deleted their account in another tab: this tab is signed out with the red notice.]

### Long lists

- Every blue box (the live box, stop boxes, notices in pop-ups, notices under buttons) shows 3 lines, then "Show N more", which opens a scroll box about 8 lines tall.
  - Example: 12 testers send work while the page is open. The live box shows the 3 newest, then "Show 9 more".
- A list inside a row, such as the unmet needs on an invite row or the devices and languages in a missing-needs line, shows 3 items, then "+N more", which opens the rest in place ("fewer" folds it again).
- **Past calls** (the tester's Call history, the developer's "Calls (N)" fold in each tester row): more than 3 sit in a plain scroll box about 3 calls tall, newest first, every call inside with its own Pay, Pay more or Dispute; 3 or fewer show without a box. A bell link to a call scrolls the box to it and marks it.
- **A part's payment lines** on the tester's session page (the Pay line, then each "Extra $X: you get $Y" line, newest last) sit in a plain scroll box about 4 lines tall once there are more than 4, everything inside; the lines under them (whether it can be paid more, the team's decision, Dispute) stay outside.
- **Searching by name** (Search by name in Invite testers, Find tests' search, the tester dashboard's Search by developer, the admin's Accounts search) works from 1 character. With 1 or 2 characters it matches a name equal to them, or with a word starting with them (any case), so results stay short; from 3 characters, any part of the name. An exact match always comes first. Example: "an" finds "Ana Silva" and "Dan" but not "Joanna"; "ann" finds "Joanna"; a tester named "m" is found by "m".
- **Dashboard and profile lists are plain scroll boxes:** every list panel on both dashboards (Your tests, Needs you, Needs review, Calls, Ratings to give, Tests visited, disputes, excerpts, invites, applications, tests left and the rest), the "Open tests" on a developer's profile and "Your tests with <name>" on a tester's profile. Everything is in the list, newest or most urgent first; no "Show N more", no pages, no cut-off. A row list is about 4 rows tall; the developer's "Your tests" cards scroll in a box about 2 card rows tall (one column of about 3 cards on phones). A short list shows without a scroll bar. Headings, filters, tick boxes and search boxes stay above the box and never scroll away. The restricted dashboard's "Disputes: add your side" and "Excerpt requests" boxes scroll once they hold more than 3 items (about 3 items tall), and so does the developer dashboard's "Your bank wants you to confirm" (soonest deadline first).
- Long names and titles are cut with "…" in rows (full text on hover) and shown in full where they are the main content.
- **Long text** is handled the same way everywhere:
  - The NDA, wherever it's shown (the join page, the session page's terms, the Switch / Keep offer and Terms history), sits in a scroll box about 12 lines tall; the offer and Terms history show "Before" and "Offered" as two such boxes, never in full.
  - The steps ("What should testers do", up to 20,000 characters) sit in a scroll box about 6 lines tall on the listing, the join page and the session page (short steps show without a scroll bar); on cards and rows, 3 lines, then "Show more"; in the offer box and Terms history, "Before" and "Offered" are two such scroll boxes.
  - Each question (up to 20,000 characters) sits in a scroll box about 6 lines tall wherever it's shown in full: the answer form, previous question sets, the listing and join page, Post and Edit, the developer's piece view and By question, and the admin's "See the work".
  - **More than 3 questions sit in a scroll box about 3 questions tall**, with every question inside (no "+N more"); 3 or fewer show without a box; on phones the box is full width. This holds wherever questions are read: the listing and join page, the tester's "Your agreed scope", the Switch / Keep offer's before and after lists, "Questions then and now" in Terms history, and the developer's Contract changes. Inside the box each question is cut to 3 lines with "Show more". The tester's answer form does the same (Tester session page). Post and Edit, where questions are typed, are unchanged, and Edit's notes from another tab still show 3, then "+N more".
  - A piece's written answers sit in their own scroll box about 16 lines tall when they're longer, with the question labels inside; the Pay box stays outside it, always visible.
  - **Reasons and notes** (the team's reasons, Stop's and End early's reasons, Pay notes, the reason for paying $0 or less than the listing) show under their heading in italics, as a quote: The team stopped this test. / *"Media pass check: build link is broken"*. A long one sits in a scroll box about 4 lines tall. This holds on banners, tester rows, Needs you, the session page and the admin lists and History. Messages in rows show 3 lines, then "Show more".
  - Limited fields have live counters and are refused in red at the field when too long, never cut without telling: "N / 20,000" (the steps, each question), "N / 500" (why a test is Mature), "N / 1,000" (the NDA, every reason and note), "N / 10,000" (Stop's and End early's reasons), "N / 2,000" (dispute text and the developer's side) and "N / 50,000" (a written part, Contact us).

### Pop-ups

Let it go uses its inline checkbox rather than a pop-up.

- **Confirm pop-ups** (Cancel is focused; the confirm button is red): Stop and End early on Developer test page; Leave and Rate and finish on Tester session page. While open they show live blue notices ("Just updated (3:10 PM): …", the 3 newest, then "Show N more"). If the confirm fails (an unseen change or any refusal), the pop-up closes and the blue notice, naming the press, plus the changes shows under the button that opened it. Leave's pop-up is built from the current state when it opens, and Leave itself never stops.
- **Inline "anyway" boxes:** Join anyway on the join page and Invite anyway on Developer test page. They list the needs still unmet, update live, and disappear when all are met. A press after a change shows the updated list with "Your press didn't go through"; any other failure closes the box, with the notice under the original button. A press that goes through closes it too, unless the page moves on (Join anyway opens the session page).
- Block and Delete account also confirm, without live notices.

### Drafts, numbers and ranges

- Unsent text (written answers, chat, dispute text, rating comments, Pay notes, Post and Edit, the times and length typed in Request a time on both sides, and End early's and Stop's reasons) is kept per person and form, up to 50 forms per person (a new one drops the oldest), and forgotten once it's sent. When they come back to the page, or the page reloads, it returns, labelled "Not sent yet" (on Post a test "Not posted yet", on Edit "Not saved yet"). Never on log in or sign up.
- Numbers use thousands separators. Money being paid or charged is always exact.
- **Money boxes round to the cent.** Every money box (Pay, Pay more, a call's rate and amount, Charge, the prices on Post and Edit, Pay what you owe if typed) rounds half up to the cent when you leave it, and shows the rounded value, so you see exactly what will be charged (1.4545 → 1.45; 2.005 → 2.01). The server rounds the same way; a press that arrives unrounded is charged at the rounded amount. Minimums and caps apply to the rounded value. Letters, negative numbers and an empty box where one is needed are still refused. Big stat tiles shorten: in full under 10,000, then k, M, B, T, then × 10^N, with no trailing ".0" (12,400 shows as 12.4k).
- **Number boxes take only what they can hold.** Whole-number boxes (tester spots, a call's Length, the admin's Extend days) take only digits, typed or pasted: a minus, "e", "+", "." and letters are ignored, and a value of only zeros is ignored too (the box stays empty or keeps what it had), so nothing below 1 can be entered; the spots box's arrows stop at 1. Money boxes (Pay, Pay more, Charge, prices and rates) never take a minus sign; $0 stays allowed where it is (Pay at $0 with a reason, the Free tick). Above the maximum still shows its red line, and the server still refuses anything out of range.
- Every box with a minimum or maximum shows the range next to it ("10–180 min", "at least $1.00").

### Suspended or on hold

A payment hold, a chargeback hold and a suspension of the developer side do the same thing to a developer's pages; a suspension of the tester side does the same to a tester's. Suspensions are per side: an admin suspends the developer side or the tester side of an account, and the other side keeps working. A hold limits only the developer side. Causes, timers and lifting are in Rules.

- **Routing.** Every signed-in page of the restricted side except the open ones below moves them to the dashboard top, live and on load; on the dashboard it scrolls up. Every press is checked on the server by the side it belongs to, and a refused press sends them there too.

  [video: A payment hold starts while the test page (left) and Edit (right) are open: both move to the dashboard top, where the blue box explains.]
- **Blue box at the dashboard top** (never red). It says:
  - if they just pressed something, "Your <press> on <test> didn't go through";
  - their account (on a mixed account, "your developer account" or "your tester account") is suspended or paused, or on hold (payment or chargeback);
  - all their tests are paused, and testers stay in them;
  - how to resume: "Pay what you owe" (opens Payments in a new tab), the chargeback steps on Payments, or an appeal through Contact us in Settings;
  - after more than 3 holds: the team will review the account after they pay;
  - when it lifts, their tests resume as normal.
- **Open while it lasts:** the dashboard; each test page, opened from its dashboard card, in the paused view only (below); Payments (receipts, Pay what you owe, Change card, payout setup); Settings (Contact us, the email setting, Sign out of all devices, Delete account; the profile editor is hidden); the bell list, where a line whose page is closed opens the dashboard top. Change card works during a payment hold, a chargeback hold and a suspension, and a suspended tester can still set up payouts. The open pages other than the dashboard show one blue line under the header, e.g. "Your tests are paused until you pay what you owe. Pay what you owe." **Closed:** tester and developer profiles, session pages, Find tests and test listings, Messages and chat, Edit, the full test page, Post a test. On their dashboard, names are plain text with no hover card, so there's no Chat with, Report, Block or profile link.
- **What works on their tests.** A test page opens in the paused view: the red banner, the overview with only **Stop the test** and **End early**, a compact **Booked calls** list with **Cancel** on each, the test's excerpt requests (Allow, Deny), the disputes, and "Your bank wants you to confirm" with **Confirm with your bank** for any charge made while they were away (Payments). The dashboard lists every such charge above the blue box, each linking to its piece here (Developer dashboard). Rate is hidden while they are held or suspended (Rules: Ratings).
  - Every press here follows the same stop-once rules as on a running test (Stopped tests): a change the page hasn't shown stops it once, the hold, suspension or pause itself included when the page was opened before it ("Your tests were paused at 3:10 PM."), and the second press goes ahead. Stop the test and End early still need their reasons. After Stop the test or End early the developer lands on the dashboard top, which says "Done: you stopped <test>." or "Done: you ended <N> testers' work on <test>."
  - After Cancel call they stay on the paused view. Calls can also be cancelled from the dashboard's Calls box.
  - Excerpt requests are never stopped, routed or refused for the pause.
  - The developer's side of a dispute can always be added, from the dashboard's "Disputes: add your side" box under the blue box: each open dispute with the tester's text and the Add your side box.
  - Pay waits until it lifts. Any other press from an older full page is refused and routed to the dashboard top with the failed-press line.

  [video: From the card to the paused view; a call cancelled (it stops once if something changed since the page loaded); Stop goes through and the dashboard says it's done.]

  [image: A suspended developer opens the test from its card: the red banner (title, reason, what's allowed), the overview with Stop and End early, Booked calls with Cancel.]

  [image: Stop went through silently, and the dashboard top says "Done: you stopped Harbor Run: first 3 levels."]

  [image: While suspended, the dashboard lists each open dispute with the tester's text and the Add your side box; more than 3 scroll.]
- **A mixed account** keeps its other side. The side switch still works: switching to the restricted side lands on the dashboard top with that side's blue box, and the other side's pages stay open. With the developer side suspended, they can still find and join tests, send work, Charge, get payouts and chat as a tester.

  [image: The developer side is paused ("Your developer account is paused"); the switch to the tester side still works.]
- **Chat** closes only for a restricted person, never because a test is paused. The restricted person's chat pages are closed, and a Send from a page loaded before the restriction is refused and routes them to the dashboard top, with the typed text kept. The other person can still open the chat and read everything; the message box is replaced by "Messages are paused for now. You can still read this chat." A Send from a page loaded before then shows, under Send, "Messages are paused for now, so this wasn't sent." and keeps the text. When the restriction lifts, the message box comes back live. Only chats where the person is on the restricted side are paused: a developer-side suspension pauses the chats on their own tests, not the chats on tests they're testing.

  [image: The developer is restricted: the tester reads "Messages are paused for now. You can still read this chat."]
- **Testers in a paused test** aren't restricted: they keep their session page, where the paused rules apply: they can't send work (drafts can be saved), request calls or join calls, and they can still cancel calls, dispute, Charge or Let it go, rate and leave, with the usual stop-once (Leave never stops; Tester session page). Chat follows the rule above: open on a test the team paused, read-only while the developer is restricted.
- **A suspended tester side** follows the same rules: the open pages above stay open, and every other tester page, their session pages included, moves them to the dashboard top. The blue box there says their account is paused, they were removed from their tests, how to appeal (Contact us in Settings), and that pay for work already accepted still reaches their Stripe account. Each test they were removed from opens in a limited view where Dispute is the only press (Tester session page), and their excerpts, with Remove, sit on the dashboard under the blue box. Charge and Let it go wait for the reinstatement: nothing is lost, because a tester's Charge never happens on its own, and the suspended time is added to their Charge windows (Rules: Close-out). An old page's Charge or Let it go reads "You can do this once your account is active again."; its Rate, "You can rate once your account is active again." Any other press is refused with "Your account is paused. Contact support if you think this is a mistake." Their dashboard is in Tester dashboard, Find tests and the join page.
- Testers never see hold, suspended, failed or chargeback about a developer. They see "paused" and "delayed".

### Paused and stopped test banners

Any pause is treated the same, whatever its cause: the team paused the test, a payment hold, a chargeback hold or a developer-side suspension. The only difference by cause is the banner's title and reason. The developer's test page shows one red banner for paused and stopped tests, top to bottom:

1. The title, by cause: "The team paused this test." / "The team stopped this test." / "Your tests are paused until you pay what you owe." / "Your account is suspended: your tests are paused." / "This test is stopped."
2. The reason, in italics in a scroll box, when there is one.
3. The updates: the failed-press lines ("Your Save on <test> didn't go through: the team paused this test.") and any other live lines for it.
4. At the bottom, what's still allowed, built from the buttons actually shown, so it never lists something hidden:
   - paused (any cause): "For now you can stop this test, end testers' work early, cancel calls, or rate testers.", each part only when it's there: "rate testers" only while a Rate box is open on the test ("Rate your testers", Developer test page), so on a team pause and never while the developer's own account is paused.
   - stopped: "You can still pay for work testers already sent, chat with them, rate testers, and read their answers", ending ", and start a Next round" only when Next round is really there (never after a team stop).

[image: The team paused this test: the title, a 1,000-character reason in italics in its scroll box, and at the bottom "For now you can stop this test, end testers' work early, cancel calls, or rate testers."]

[image: A payment hold: "Your tests are paused until you pay what you owe." on the same banner.]

[image: Stopped by the developer: "This test is stopped." and what still works, with Next round.]

[image: The team stopped this test: the same banner; Next round isn't offered after a team stop, so the allowed line doesn't list it.]

### Stopped tests

- A stopped or closed test shows a "Stopped" label at the top and:
  - developer: the red banner above, "This test is stopped." (or "The team stopped this test.") with what still works;
  - tester: the stopped line, built from what is actually still available (Tester session page).
- Every other button and panel is hidden, so the page shows only what still works (Developer test page, Tester session page). An old page's press is refused in blue, naming the stop.
- **The same stop-once rules as a running test.** On a stopped, closed or paused test (any cause: the developer's Stop, the team, the developer's payment or chargeback hold or suspension), after a tester's work ended (ended early, left, removed, blocked), and while the developer is held or suspended, every press still allowed follows the same rules it has on a running test: the first press after a change the page hasn't shown stops once in the blue box (with the Alerts lines as usual), and the second goes ahead. That covers the developer's Pay, Pay more, Confirm with your bank, Stop the test, End early, Cancel call, Next round and Go to the next round, and the tester's Charge, Let it go, Dispute and Cancel call. What still works in each state is listed on Developer test page and Tester session page. Refusals stay plain, and a refused press shows its reason while the page updates: "Pay closed on <date>.", "<dev> paid $X for this meanwhile.", "You already rated Ana Silva.", "Rating opens once the team decides your dispute.", an NDA now on the test, a cap reached.
  - Example: on a stopped test, a tester disputes after the developer's page loaded: the developer's Pay stops once with "Ana disputed the pay for their recording"; the second press pays.
- **Exceptions, unchanged:** Rate keeps its own short list (Why a press stops: Rate); excerpt requests, Allow, Deny and Remove, and the developer's "Send your side" never stop; Leave and Block never stop. **Pay what you owe** (DEFAULT) stops once when the amount owed changed since the page loaded (a tester charged meanwhile), showing the new total; it never charges an amount the developer didn't see (Payments).

**When a test stops or pauses while a page is open.** "Stops" here means the developer's Stop, the team's Stop, the test closing (after 7 days paused, or Close now) and End early for that tester, all treated alike; "pauses" means the team's pause and the developer's payment hold, chargeback hold or developer-side suspension. On a page opened before it:

- **Stops once, with the stop or pause as the change:** every press still allowed (the developer's Pay, Pay more, Confirm with your bank, Stop the test, End early, Next round, the call buttons; the tester's Charge, Let it go, Dispute, Cancel call), and Rate on both sides for a stop (a pause never stops Rate). The second press goes ahead. The line, to testers: "Nora Lane stopped this test at 3:10 PM." (the developer's Stop), "This test was stopped at 3:10 PM." (the team's Stop: never who or why), "This test closed at 3:10 PM.", "Nora Lane ended your work early at 3:10 PM.", "This test was paused at 3:10 PM." (any pause: never why, never hold or suspended); to the developer: "You stopped this test at …", "The team stopped this test at …" (the reason is on the banner), "This test closed at …", "You ended Ana's work early at …", "The team paused this test at …", "Your tests were paused at …" (a payment or chargeback hold), "Your developer account was suspended at …". The time is in the reader's own saved time zone and shows as "3:10 PM" within 12 hours, with the date after that ("Tue, Nov 3, 3:10 PM"). A stop line says its time once: the box never adds a second time after it, in Rate's box as in every other. A Stop the developer made in another tab is said once, as "You stopped the test in another tab".
- **Refused:** the tester's Cancel call after a stop: "This call was cancelled when the test stopped."
- **Never stopped by it:** the developer's side of a dispute, and excerpt requests, Allow, Deny and Remove; Leave and Block.
- Other presses that are gone after a stop are refused as on Tester session page and Developer test page.

A page opened after the stop or pause doesn't stop for the state itself; every other change since it loaded stops a press once as usual. In the suspended tester's limited view, Dispute (its one press) follows the same rules.

### Deleted accounts

- The deleted person: every open tab logs out with a red "This account no longer exists".
- A deleted developer: their tests stop and leave Find tests; testers see the stopped test and what they can still do; invites and applications close; chats become read-only; the profile shows "Deleted account" with ratings kept; parts where Charge was already open stay open, and a tester's Charge goes to the deleted developer's saved card (if it's declined, the payment stays delayed for the tester and is listed for admins; the platform doesn't cover it); work still under review never opens Charge, and the tester's part carries the badge "Can't be paid: <dev> deleted their account"; a listed call not paid yet is still charged automatically 7 days after it, to their saved card (declined: it stays delayed for the tester and is listed for admins, with no hold; with no saved card the call reads "<dev> deleted their account, so this can't be paid."); open disputes are still decided by the admins, a decision for the tester charging the saved card the same way; pending excerpt requests on their tests close, and excerpts already shown stay.
- A deleted tester: removed from every test (developers see "Left this test" and the name "Deleted account"); their work, payments and ratings stay; calls ahead, invites and applications are cancelled; open disputes are withdrawn; chats close. Work they sent stays in the developer's Needs you, with Pay and the usual reminders, and Pay reaches their Stripe account if their payout account was connected. If it wasn't, those rows leave Needs you with "<name> deleted their account before setting up payouts, so this can't be paid." (DEFAULT). Unsent work is gone, and so are Charge and Dispute. Their excerpt requests and shown excerpts are removed. A delayed payment still reaches their Stripe account when the developer pays, if their payout setup is finished.
- Admins still see deleted accounts in full: who deleted them, why, and the history.
- Deleting never clears a hold, a chargeback or money owed. A developer can always delete their account; the warnings are in Settings on Shared pages.

### The bell

- **Each line belongs to a side** (batch 34 §5): the developer's, the tester's, or the account's (sign-in, password, Terms, the account's deletion, the site's own notices). With both sides, the bell, its number and the Notifications page show the side in use's lines plus the account's; the other side's wait there for a switch. A line's side is what it's about: the page it opens, or for shared pages (the dashboard, Payments) its kind.
- **Reach.** A line about another person arrives only if that person is or was in one of your open tests. Invites, applications, payments and disputes always arrive, because they can come from outside an open test. Chat follows the same reach.
- **Every line opens the exact spot**, e.g. the developer's Needs you row, the tester's part, a call, the keys box, Payments. A line about a call that's over opens that call in the tester's row (developer) or in Call history (tester), marked; a call still ahead opens Live session. Each page tab lists the lines that open it.
- **Stacking.** Lines about one test on the same day stack into one entry that opens to show them ("that test's day"). Lines of the same kind stack across tests instead, one entry per kind per day, each line inside still opening its own spot:
  - developer: "Payments and holds" (payments, holds, the one bank-confirmation line, and the team's pause, stop or close of a test: the table below); "The team decided" (the team's answers about a hold); "Disputes filed"; "Disputes decided"; "Paused tests" (the daily paused reminder); "Work waiting for your review" (review reminders, a listed call's pay reminders, the daily "Pay now"); "Closing soon" ("Closing soon: 3 tests"); "Call requests";
  - tester: "You were paid" (Pay and Pay more together, a listed call's automatic charge, and the team's $0 closures); "Payments delayed"; "Delayed payments arrived"; "Tests stopped or ended"; "Tests paused or running again"; "Invitations waiting" (invite reminders); "Charge or Let it go"; "Payout"; "Applications and invites" ("Applications and invites · 4 updates today"); "Joined" ("Joined · 3 updates today"); "New terms to answer" ("New terms to answer · 2 tests"); "Excerpts" ("Excerpts · 2 updates today"); "Deadlines" ("Deadlines · 2 tests");
  - both: "Ratings to give" (every rating line and rating reminder); "The team decided" (a side paused or active again);
  - admin: "Disputes" ("Disputes · 3 new"), "Reports" ("Reports · 2 new"), "Hold lifts waiting" ("Hold lifts waiting · 1"), "Suspended accounts".
  An entry whose title has no count of its own reads "<title> · N updates <day>". Test titles are always quoted, stacked or not.
  - Example: Harbor Run and Sky Lab both pay Ana on Monday. Ana's bell shows one "You were paid" entry with both inside.
- Receipts, a pause being lifted or extended, and outages are always their own line.
- **The bell's number** is how many entries in the bell list have anything unread: a stack with 3 unread lines counts 1, and above 9 it reads "9+" (the label read aloud says the same number). It follows the same ~20-second check every signed-in page makes, and checks at once when the tab comes back into focus, so it changes without navigating.
- **Read only when opened.** A single line is read when it's clicked; a stack when it's expanded (all its lines at once) or when one of its lines is clicked. Opening the Notifications page marks nothing read by itself, and there's no "mark all as read". An unread entry keeps its dot and bold text until then, and a stack that gets a new line after it was read shows unread again and counts again.
- **One line for the same money.** A payment never gives two bell lines. A receipt names what was paid, as the dashboard's rows do: "Receipt: you paid $12.50 for Ana Silva's written answers", "…for Ana Silva's call on Oct 30", "…for the team's decision on Ana Silva's dispute", "…for a payment your bank had reversed (Ana Silva's written answers)". An automatic retry of what's owed gives only its own line, "We charged your card $10.00 for what you owed: Ana Silva's call on Oct 30." (several items: "…for what you owed: Ana Silva's call on Oct 30 and Ben Okafor's written answers"; more than 3: the first 3, then "and 2 more"), with no receipt line; that line goes in the payment email, which is its receipt. Pay what you owe gives one receipt for everything the press paid, naming the items ("Receipt: you paid $35.00 for what you owed: Ana Silva's call on Oct 30 and Ben Okafor's written answers"). The bank confirming a charge that waited for it gives that charge's one receipt (a retry that waited for the bank: "…for what you owed: <items>"), and the team's Approve that lifts a chargeback hold names the money in its one notice instead of a receipt.
- **No reasons in the bell.** A bell line never carries a reason, a note, a dispute's text or a chat message. It says what happened and opens the place where the text is shown. Emails include them in full.
- **Each reader's own time.** Every time in a bell line or an email is written in the reader's own time zone and names it: "2:00 PM EST". The app learns each person's time zone from their browser when they sign in (UTC until it knows). Times on pages use the browser's own clock, except a few the server writes into the page, which use the person's saved time zone: the bank's "confirm by" times (Developer dashboard, Developer test page, Payments), the stop lines' times (Stopped tests), when Charge opens (Tester dashboard, Tester session page) and when a listed call is charged automatically (the tester's call row and the developer's Pay box). Every time in a stop box ("Since you opened this page:" and its changes) is in the reader's saved time zone too, like the stop lines, so one box never mixes two zones (the browser's only when none is saved). Scheduling stays automatic: times are picked in the picker's own time zone, the other side sees them in theirs, and there's no time zone menu.

  [image: The developer's bell in Chicago time: "Booked: <day> at <time> CST".]

  [image: The same booking in the tester's bell, in their own zone (JST).]
- **Reminders wait while a test is paused** and start again when it runs again: the review reminders, "Pay now", "Closing soon" and the testers' deadline reminders. The exceptions: the developer's daily paused reminder; the tester's daily "Charge or let go" line, which waits only while their tester side is suspended; and the rating reminders, which come whatever the test's state and wait only while that person can't rate (Rules: Ratings).
- **One line a day for money across tests.** The developer's "Pay now", "Closing soon" and bank-confirmation lines, and the tester's "Charge or let go", each cover every test in one line, at most once a day (the closing-soon and bank-confirmation lines already sent that day are updated in place when something new joins them, and show unread again).
- **Reminders also go in the summary email** if the email setting allows it, except the ones that are always emailed (Emails, below). Each line is emailed once; a Weekly or Monthly email shows only the newest copy of each reminder.
- Admin lines about disputes, reports and lifts show "Reported more than 3 times" next to names where it applies (Administrator console).

[image: The developer's daily lines: review reminders on one schedule, "N applications wait for your answer", and a call to pay.]

**Every bell line**

| Who | Line | When | Stacks in |
|---|---|---|---|
| Developer | "<tester> joined <test>" (also after an accepted application, invite or rejoin) | each time | that test's day |
| Developer | "<tester> applied to join" / "<name> asks to rejoin"; then "N applications wait for your answer" | each time; then daily while waiting | that test's day |
| Developer | "<tester> left <test>" (also after a suspension or deletion, with the name "Deleted account" where it applies) | each time | that test's day |
| Developer | "<tester> sent / updated written answers / a recording"; after a Pay, "<tester> sent an updated version of their <part> for “<test>”" (opens its Needs you row) or, at the piece's cap, "<tester> updated their <part> for “<test>”" (opens the tester's row) | each send | that test's day |
| Developer | "<tester> asked to show an excerpt from <test> on their profile" | each request | that test's day |
| Developer | "<tester> asked for a call: <time(s)>" | each request | Call requests |
| Developer | Call lines: booked, cancelled ("<name> cancelled the call on <day>."; the reason is in the email and on the call), remaining calls cancelled, nobody joined ("Your call with <tester> on <time>: Nobody joined, so the call was cancelled."), the browsers never connected ("…: It couldn't connect, so the call was cancelled."), cancelled because the test is paused, missed because the site was down, a Forever call skipped for a clash | each time | that test's day |
| Developer | "“<test>”: review <tester>'s <part> within 1 month / 3 weeks / 2 weeks / 1 week / 3 days / by tomorrow" (a listed part adds "(after that <tester> can charge or let it go)"; an unlisted one "(it isn't listed: any amount, $0 included)"; listed work sent late "(it was sent late: any amount, $0 included)") | the marks still ahead when the work is sent, counting to its review date: 1 month (only if it was more than a month away), 3, 2 and 1 weeks, 3 days and 1 day before; none within 24 hours of sending, because "<tester> sent work" counts as the first | Work waiting for your review |
| Developer | A listed call: "“<test>”: pay <tester>'s call on <time> within 3 days." / "…by tomorrow." | 3 days and 1 day before it's charged automatically (7 days after the call), only the marks still ahead | Work waiting for your review |
| Developer | "Pay now: Ana's written answers on “Harbor Run” ($10.00): Ana can charge or let it go (until Nov 18)" (one piece) / "Pay now: 3 pieces on 2 tests: their testers can charge or let them go (the first closes Nov 18)" (several; on one test, "Pay now: 3 pieces on “Harbor Run”: …"); it opens Needs you on the dashboard | one line a day across all their tests, on every day at least one listed written part or recording is past its review date, still unpaid and open to the tester's Charge; none once nothing is (listed calls, unlisted work and work sent late are never in it); emailed whatever the setting; waits while the test is paused | Work waiting for your review |
| Developer | "“<test>”: <tester>'s <part> wasn't reviewed by <date>: pay it." ("…written answers weren't reviewed…") | daily per piece, only when the tester can't Charge it (they deleted their account, or their Charge closed) and Pay is still open | Work waiting for your review |
| Developer | "Pay your calls: your call with <tester> on “<test>” isn't paid yet (any amount, $0 included)." / "Pay your calls: 3 calls to pay on 2 tests." (calls that aren't listed) | weekly, until each is paid ($0 counts) | own line |
| Developer | "“<test>” closes to new testers on <date>." (Open until is near) | 1 month, 1 week, 3 days, 1 day before Open until | that test's day |
| Developer | "<tester> disputed a payment on <test>" | each time | Disputes filed |
| Developer | "A dispute on <test> was decided" | each time | Disputes decided |
| Developer | "Receipt: you paid $X for <what>", naming it: "Ana Silva's written answers", "Ana Silva's call on Oct 30 (charged automatically after 7 days)" (a listed call's automatic charge), "Ana Silva's written answers (charged by them after 7 days)" (a tester's Charge charged right after a chargeback hold lifted), "the team's decision on Ana Silva's dispute", "what you owed: Ana Silva's call on Oct 30 and Ben Okafor's written answers" (Pay what you owe, one for the press) | every charge that goes through, once for the same money: none for an automatic retry (its own line names the items) or for money the team's Approve takes when it lifts a chargeback hold (its notice names it) | own line |

No bell line when a tester removes their own excerpt.

### Emails

- Each person picks Off, or On with Daily, Weekly or Monthly, under "How you're notified" on the Notifications page (Shared pages).
- With email On, each email lists every bell line since the last one ("Your Playtest summary: N updates"). Each line is emailed once. Still one email per account: with both sides, its lines sit under "Your account", "As a developer" and "As a tester" headings (batch 34 §5).
- **Payment lines are always emailed the same evening, whatever the setting:** receipts, "You were paid…", Pay more, delays and arrivals, failures and holds, retries, payouts and chargebacks, and the daily money lines (the developer's "Pay now", "Closing soon" and bank-confirmation lines; the tester's "Charge or let go"). On Daily they're inside the daily summary; on Weekly, Monthly and Off they go out the same evening in "Your Playtest payment updates". The Notifications page says so (Shared pages).
- With email Off, nothing else is emailed except the lines in the first part of the table, which go in the same one end-of-day email (titled "Your Playtest account updates" when it holds one of them that isn't a payment line).
- **Every deadline reminder of a tester** ("Your deadline on “<test>” is <date>: send your work before then.", all four marks) ignores the setting too: it goes in the same end-of-day email, and several stack under one "Deadlines · 2 tests" heading there, as in the bell.
- **Admins:** the end-of-day admin summary follows each admin's own email setting (Daily, Weekly or Monthly, each listing everything new since that admin's last one; nothing with Off), and so do their bell lines (new disputes, reports and lifts waiting); the console and the bell show all of it anyway. "Playtest: new chargeback of $X" is the exception: sent right away, one email per chargeback, to every admin, whatever their setting (Stripe's response deadline).
- **One branded layout.** Every email the app sends (confirm and reset, the summaries, the payment and account updates, notices to addresses with no account, the admin daily email, the chargeback email, Contact us) is HTML built from its plain text, which is sent alongside unchanged; subjects are unchanged. Typed values are escaped; the main link is a button ("Confirm your email", "Reset your password", "Choose a password", "Open Playtest", "Open the admin console") with the plain link under it. The footer, on a light background, has the PlaytestPlatform logo (linking to the site), "You got this email because you have a Playtest account." with "Notification settings" (not on confirm or reset), and "Playtest · playtestplatform.com". The dev outbox shows each email's HTML with its plain text under it.
- Emails carry the full text that bell lines leave out: the team's reasons, Pay notes, the developer's Stop reason, End early's reason, a cancelled call's reason, a chat message. Every time is in the reader's own time zone, named ("2:00 PM EST").
- **Emails never get lost.** Every email is stored first, then sent through Resend with the stored email's own one-time key (Resend's Idempotency-Key), so it's never sent twice. If sending fails (Resend down, a bad key), the email waits with the error and is tried again by the 5-minute timed run (Rules: When timed jobs run): after 5, 15 and 30 minutes, then every hour, for 48 hours. After that it's failed for good: kept, never deleted, and listed for the admins. Each try claims its email first, so two runs at once send it once. When Resend's daily limit is reached (the free plan's 100 a day), every email waits for the next day's first run (midnight UTC) without using up its tries. The owner hears even while Resend is down: the red emails box and a bell line on the Administrator console, and the uptime monitor's keyword alert (Security and launch: The live site).
- On the live site, emails are always really sent, never written to a test outbox. A missing email key stops the deploy (Security and launch: The live site); an email sent without one waits, with the missing key as its error, and goes out once the key is set. In development without a key, emails go to the dev outbox as before.

| Email | To | When |
|---|---|---|
| **Ignore the setting** (sent even with Off, once, in one end-of-day email) | | |
| Every payment line (receipts, "You were paid…", Pay more, delays and arrivals, failures and holds, retries, payouts, chargebacks) | developer or tester | the same evening ("Your Playtest payment updates"; on Daily, inside the summary) |

| Any line for an address with no account | that address | at once |

### Outages and phones

- With outage checks on, outages come from two sources, treated differently:
  - **The uptime service's alerts** are applied: a "down" alert, then the matching "up". While the site is down its "down" alert can't reach it, so an "up" with no "down" before it records the outage from the length the up alert carries (UptimeRobot's alert duration): it started that long before the up arrived.
  - **The app's own heartbeat never moves anything on its own.** Any server activity counts as a heartbeat (page loads, API calls, the 5-minute timed run, the daily run, the server starting), so a gap means the server really answered nothing. A gap of 15 minutes or more is only listed in the admin console's Outages box, "Possible outage: no checks from 3:10 PM to 3:42 PM (32 min). Nothing has moved.", with **Approve: move every timer** and **Dismiss**, whatever its length (Administrator console). Approve turns it into an outage, applied like a reported one; Dismiss drops it and nothing moves. So pausing the monitor, a lapsed monitor plan or a local server restarted after a while no longer moves any timer: the gap waits for an admin.
  - **A reported outage's own span wins.** When an uptime report overlaps a listed gap, the gap is closed as covered by it and the report's span is applied, never widened to the gap's. Two reports of the same downtime merge into one outage (one row in the console, one bell line, reworded to the merged times), and only the part not yet counted moves the clocks; an extension that would bring an outage already applied over 24 hours waits for an admin's Approve as its own row.
- If the site was down 15 minutes or more, every running timer moves later by that time (deadlines, Open until, review dates, a listed call's automatic charge date, pay-by dates and retries) and calls inside it are cancelled with no fault. Everyone affected is told once.
- The app uses its own clock for when the site went down and came back (when the alerts and its own activity arrived), not the times inside the alert. An outage longer than 24 hours moves nothing until an admin approves it in the console.
- Every core task works on a phone; panels fold.

## Why an old tab can't act on stale information

Part of Every page.

- Every press goes to the server, which re-checks what is true now before doing anything: the tester's place in the test (working, left, ended, rated), the test (open, paused, stopped), holds, suspensions, blocks, spots, Open until, deadlines and limits.
- Each form sends the moment its page last updated. Anything newer stops the press once. Anything that makes the press impossible (the work ended, the test stopped, the request was already taken, the offer was replaced) refuses it every time.
- So an old tab can't rejoin, accept a replaced offer, accept a request someone else took, book a call on a stopped test, or pay twice: each payment carries a one-time key, and a repeated press returns the first result instead of charging again.
- Rejoining needs a current ask that the developer accepts. An old page's Join or Accept is checked against today's state and refused if it no longer applies.

## Why a press stops

New terms must be answered with Switch or Keep before Send, every call press, Charge, Let it go or Dispute. The refusal is "Answer the new terms first: Switch or Keep." and moves to the offer. Offers on ended work block nothing. Dispute currently validates its reason before checking the offer, so an invalid reason is shown first.

When the top box and a press box describe the same event within the same minute, they merge into one line, preserving the press box wording. A change counts as shown only once the live top box is visible. Written drafts refresh untouched answers from another device while preserving answers being typed. Refusals whose button disappears stay inline in its part or row (at the top if that part is gone), until the next press, without a duplicate or a blink.

Invite anyway refreshes current missing needs and names Invite; Everyone now meets the needs is information, not a failure. Join, Apply and Ask to rejoin likewise name the actual press rather than Your press.

Part of Every page.

A press stops once when something changed since the page last showed it, so nobody acts on stale information. The blue box under the button reads, top to bottom:

1. "Your <button> didn't go through.", using the button's own label: "Your Pay on Ana Silva's written answers didn't go through.", "Your Stop the test didn't go through.", "Your Save didn't go through.", "Your Send didn't go through."
2. "Since you opened this page:" and the list of changes, with their times.
3. The Alerts lines. Developer: "You can turn these stops off in Alerts on the test page." and "Don't stop me for:" with one quick button per kind in the box. Tester: "You can turn these stops off below, or with Alerts at the top of your test's page. New terms from the developer and a key marked as working still stop." (on the join page, where there's no test page yet: "…below, or with Alerts on your dashboard.") and "Turn off alerts"; when their Alerts are already Off, neither line shows.
4. Last, in bold: "Press <button> again to go ahead.", e.g. "Press Pay again to go ahead."

A refusal (not a stop) keeps its own wording but also names the press first: "Your Pay didn't go through: <reason>."

[image: Every stop box names the press: "Your Pay on Ana Silva's recording didn't go through." · Since you opened this page: … · the Alerts line · bold "Press Pay again to go ahead."]

Below is every kind of change that stops a press, for each side, then the kinds that never do.

### Developer

Buttons that stop: Next round (and Go to the next round), Pay, Pay more, Confirm with your bank (under a piece on the test page, exactly like Pay on that piece), the call buttons (Request a time, Accept, Decline, Withdraw, Cancel ▾), Invite, Accept and Decline of an application or rejoin ask, Save on Edit, and Stop the test and End early. Rate has its own short list (Rate, below). The same holds on a stopped, closed or paused test and while the developer is held or suspended, where the stop, close or pause itself stops a page opened before it once (Stopped tests on Every page). Each change kind belongs to an Alerts type, which the developer can switch off (below).

| Change | What triggers it | Alerts type | Line in the blue notice |
|---|---|---|---|
| A tester joined | a join, a rejoin, or an accepted application or invite | Joins and applications | "Ana joined" / "Ana joined again" |
| An application | a tester applies, asks to rejoin, or withdraws an application | Joins and applications | "Ana applied to join" / "Ana asks to rejoin" / "Ana withdrew their application" |
| An invite answer | a tester turns down an invite | Joins and applications | "Ana said no thanks to your invite" |
| The test filled | the last spot was taken | Joins and applications | "<test> is full" |
| A tester left | Leave, a working tester finishing with a rating, or the tester's account was deleted or their tester side suspended | Testers leaving | "Ana left the test" / "Ana finished their part (rated you)" (a closed account reads the same as a leave, with the name "Deleted account" where it applies) |
| Work sent | written answers or a recording sent or updated (after a Pay too, while the piece can still be paid more) | Work sent | "Ana sent their written answers" / "Ana updated their recording" / "Ana sent an updated version of their written answers after you paid" |
| Call changes | a call booked, cancelled, requested, withdrawn or declined | Call changes | "Ana booked a call for <time>" / "Ana cancelled their call on <time>" / "Ana requested a call for <time>" |
| A tester's Charge or Let it go | Charge opens, or the tester chooses | Payments | "Ana can now charge or let go of their written answers" / "Ana charged $X for their recording" / "Ana let their recording go: nothing charged" |
| Payments | a payment went through or failed, or the bank's chargeback was reversed | Payments | "Payment failed on <test>: …" / "Your bank's chargeback of $X was reversed: $Y refunded to your card." |
| A dispute | a tester disputes a payment | Disputes | "Ana disputed the pay for their recording" |
| The team decided | an admin decides a dispute, acts on the test, or closes open reviews at $0 | Decisions by the team | "The team decided Ana's dispute: …" / "The team closed 3 open reviews on this test for $0: “<reason>”" |
| Listing info | the description, devices, languages or similar changed | Listing changes | "<dev> changed the description" |
| A chargeback | the bank reverses a payment | none: always stops | "Your bank reversed your $X payment for <what>" |
| The subscription ended | a cancel reached its end, or a renewal lapsed | none: always stops | "Your subscription ended." |

Every change kind that stops a developer press has an Alerts type except those last two, which are deliberate: a chargeback and the subscription ending always stop, like holds, pauses, a stopped test and refusals. A tester's Switch or Keep, a key reported broken, an update of a piece already paid the most and a rating from a tester whose part had already ended are never in the table: they are quiet live-box lines (Changes that never stop a press).

On a paused test (any cause) the Alerts menu lists only the kinds that can still happen: Testers leaving, Call changes, Payments, Disputes and Decisions by the team. On a stopped test (the developer's or the team's) it lists only Payments, Disputes and Decisions by the team. The menu keeps the developer's ticks; it only shows fewer types.

**Your own changes in another tab** (made by the developer in another tab or device since the page loaded) aren't in Alerts, so they can't be switched off:

- **Changes that count:** an Edit save (any field), Pay, Pay more, Confirm with your bank, End early, Stop the test, Next round (the draft made), any call change (request, withdraw, accept, decline, cancel), Invite and Cancel invite, and Accept or Decline of an application or rejoin ask.
- **They stop every developer press once, on the test page and on Edit**, even a press about something else: Save, Next round and Go to the next round, End early, Stop the test, Pay, Pay more, Confirm with your bank, the call buttons, Invite, Accept and Decline. The stop box lists them as "You … in another tab" lines, with their times: "You paid Ana Silva $10.00 for the written answers in another tab", "You paid Ana Silva $5.00 more for their written answers in another tab" (a Pay more the bank confirmed afterwards counts from when it went through), "You saved the listing in another tab: spots, description", "You started the next round in another tab", "You booked a call with Ana Silva for Thu, Nov 5, 3:00 PM in another tab", "You requested a call with Ben Okafor for …", "You invited Ana Silva in another tab", "You accepted Ben Okafor's application in another tab", "You ended Ana Silva's work early in another tab", "You stopped the test in another tab". The second press goes ahead, or is refused as usual if it can no longer happen.
- **Live:** the same lines show in the top box at once, and raise the pill, on the test page and on Edit. Edit keeps its own per-field notes for another tab's Save (Edit test); the stop line is in addition.
