- **Never stopped by them** (DEFAULT reading of the owner's words): Rate and excerpt Allow and Deny. Ratings and excerpt decisions made in another tab aren't lines that stop other presses either; they only race with the same press.
- **Special cases, on press:** End early on testers the other tab already ended drops them, and the rest go through on the second press; Stop when the other tab already stopped the test is then refused (already stopped); Next round when the other tab already made the draft links to it.
- **Special cases, live and on press** (a line in the live box at once, and the page updates while keeping what you typed):
  - Pay on the same piece: the other tab paid it, so its Pay box disappears (the Needs you row goes; the tester row shows the paid amount and an empty Pay more). What was typed is dropped.
  - Pay more on the same piece: the box updates to the new paid total and room; the amount and note you typed stay.
  - Call changes: the Live session updates at once; anything you were requesting that is now booked or requested is marked "Already booked" or "Already requested", and the rest of your request form stays.
  - Invite and Cancel invite: the invite panel updates at once (Invited <date>, or tickable again).
- **The same press on the same item is refused, with no stop box:** "You already rated Ana Silva.", "You already allowed this excerpt.", "You already declined this application.", "You already sent your side." The page updates to show it.
- **Everything else from another tab** (Alerts ticks, chat, the side of a dispute, a call's problem report): no line in the top box; the side of a dispute and a problem report stop only a press on the same thing.
- On a stopped, closed or paused test, and while the developer is held or suspended, these stop a press the same way (Stopped tests on Every page).

[image: Work sent: Ben sent their answers since the page loaded, so Pay stops once and lists it under the button.]

[image: A tester left: Cam left while the Stop pop-up was open; the press didn't go through and the change shows under Stop.]

[image: Joins and work sent on Edit: Ana sent answers and Ben joined while Edit was open, so Save stops once with the list.]

[image: The subscription ended while the test page was open: "Your subscription ended." in the live box; a press stops once for it.]

[image: Alerts on a paused test: only testers leaving, call changes, payments, disputes and the team's decisions.]

[image: Alerts on a stopped test: payments, disputes and the team's decisions only.]

[image: The same piece paid in another tab: the Pay box here disappears and the live box says so.]

### Tester

Buttons that stop: Switch / Keep, Send (written answers and recording), Charge, Let it go, Dispute, Request a time, Accept, Decline, Withdraw and Cancel call. New terms are different for Send and the call presses: an open offer blocks them until it's answered, instead of stopping them once (New terms come first, below). Rate has its own short list (Rate, below). Leave and Block never stop. On a stopped, closed or paused test, after their work ended early, and on a test they left or were removed from, what still works follows the same rules, and the stop or pause itself stops a page opened before it once (Stopped tests on Every page). The join page has its own short list (Join page).

**Alerts: On / Off.** Testers have one press, "Alerts: On" or "Alerts: Off", at the top right of the session page. One short grey line under it says what stops a press in the state shown, and swaps when the button is pressed:
- On: "Stops a press once for: new terms, your key, pay, calls, delayed pay, your dispute, listing changes, other tabs."
- Off: "Stops a press only for: new terms, your key marked as working."

It's saved per tester for all their tests, and it's one setting wherever it shows: "Turn off alerts" in a stop box flips the button at the top at once (the box's press then reads "Alerts are off"), turning Alerts back On there offers "Turn off alerts" again, and the tester's other tabs and devices follow on their next poll. With Alerts Off, only changes to their contract stop a press (new terms, and their key marked as working); every other change still shows in the live box, greyed, and never stops. Refusals are unchanged.

| Change | What triggers it | Line in the blue notice |
|---|---|---|
| New terms (stops even with Alerts Off) | the developer offered new terms; the page scrolls up to the offer. Charge, Let it go, Dispute, Send and the call presses are blocked until Switch or Keep (below) | "<dev> offered new terms: <changes>", with "Answer the new terms first: Switch or Keep." on blocked presses |
| Your key marked as working (stops even with Alerts Off) | the developer marked the tester's key as working | "The developer marked your key as working (HR-2222)" |
| Your work was reviewed | the developer paid for it, or paid more for it with Pay more | "<dev> paid $X for your written answers" / "<dev> paid you $X more for your written answers" |
| Call changes | the developer's requests (new, withdrawn or taken), a booking, their own request accepted or declined, or a cancelled call; counted from when the page first loaded in this tab, so a change the live box already showed still stops the next press once | "<dev> requested a call for <time>" / "<dev> withdrew the request for …" / "The call time <time> is no longer available: another tester took it" / "<dev> accepted your call request: <time>" / "<dev> declined your call request for <time>" / "Your call on <time> was cancelled" |
| A delayed payment, or its arrival | the developer's payment was declined, then went through | "<dev>'s payment for your <part> ($X) is delayed. You'll be told when it arrives." |
| Your dispute | the developer added their side, or the team decided | "<dev> added their side to your dispute" / "The team decided your dispute: the pay was raised to $X" |
| Listing info | an Edit Save changed the listing information (title, product, description, build link, devices, languages, categories, who can join, Mature); a rename alone never stops | one line per Save: "Marc Hanna edited the listing at 6:55 PM: title (now “lllllq”), description, build link · See it" |
| A key given | the developer's Save gave them one of the free keys (the test had none when they joined) | "Marc Hanna gave you an access key for “<test>”" |
| Your own changes in another tab | anything the tester did in another tab or device since the page loaded (below) | "You sent your updated answers in another tab" / "You charged Nora Lane $10.00 for your recording in another tab" / "You requested a call for Thu, Nov 5, 3:00 PM in another tab" / "You left the test in another tab" |

[video: Ana has typed their answers; the developer pays their recording; Send stops once with the blue list; the second press sends.]

[image: The tester's stop box: "Your Send didn't go through." · Nora Lane paid $15.00 for your recording · "Turn off alerts" · "Press Send again to go ahead."]

[image: Charge stops once and names itself: the developer changed the listing after the page loaded.]

[image: "Alerts: On" at the top right of the session page, with its grey line "Stops a press once for: new terms, your key, pay, calls, …".]

[image: Alerts Off: a payment made after the page loaded shows greyed in the live box, and Send goes straight through.]

[image: With Alerts Off, an open terms offer still comes first: the page goes up to the Switch or Keep box.]

**Your own changes in another tab** (made by the tester in another tab or device since the page loaded), on the session page, the join page and their invitations:

- **Changes that count:** Send and Send updated (answers or recording), Switch or Keep, Charge, Let it go, Dispute, any call change (request, withdraw, accept, decline, cancel, the rest of a set), a call's problem report, Leave, Join, Apply, Ask to rejoin, Withdraw application and No thanks to an invite.
- **They stop every tester press there once:** Send, Switch / Keep, Charge, Let it go, Dispute, the call buttons, Join, Apply, Ask to rejoin, Withdraw application, Accept invitation and No thanks. The lines read "You … in another tab", with their times: "You sent your updated answers in another tab", "You charged Nora Lane $10.00 for your recording in another tab", "You let your written answers go in another tab", "You requested a call for Thu, Nov 5, 3:00 PM in another tab", "You opened a dispute in another tab", "You switched to the new terms in another tab", "You left the test in another tab", "You applied to join in another tab", "You said no thanks to the invite in another tab". The second press goes ahead, or is refused if it can no longer happen ("You already disputed this payment. A part can be disputed once.").
- **They follow Alerts** like the other kinds: with Alerts Off they show greyed and don't stop. The same press made there is always a plain refusal.
- **Live:** they show in the top box and raise the pill.
- **Never lines:** Rate (and the end of their part it brings) only races with Rate itself ("You already rated Nora Lane."), and so do excerpt requests and Remove. Leave and Block never stop, though a Leave made in another tab is a line that stops the others.

**New terms come first.** While an offer of new terms is open, it blocks these presses until the tester answers it: Send my answers / Send my updated answers, Send my recording / updated recording, and every call press (Request a time, Accept, Decline, Withdraw, Cancel call, Cancel the remaining calls, Join call).

- **On the press** nothing happens to the work or the call. A blue line under the pressed button says "Answer the new terms first: Switch or Keep.", and the page moves up to the Switch / Keep box, highlighted with a ring for a few seconds. The form's own checks (an empty Required question, the 50,000 characters, a red call row) wait: the offer comes first. Every further press does the same until the tester answers; no second press gets through.
- **The server refuses the same way**, with the same line, so an old page that hasn't shown the offer yet is refused too; that page loads the offer first, then moves up to it.
- **Answering:** Switch or Keep both unlock (Keep stays on the old terms). After that the presses work as usual, with their usual stop-once, and Switch keeps its rule: "Switching means answering the new questions: send your updated answers."
- **With Alerts Off** the block still applies: an offer is about the tester's own contract.
- **A newer Save** replaces the offer and keeps the presses blocked until the newest one is answered. An offer whose work closed (the test stopped or closed, the tester's work ended, they left) blocks nothing.
- **Not blocked** (they keep the stop-once above): Charge, Let it go, Dispute, Rate, Leave, Block, Alerts, chat, drafts' Save, attachments and Record (recording to a draft isn't sending).
- Join call in the tester dashboard's Upcoming calls is refused with the same line under it; the offer itself is on the session page.

### Rate

Rate stops once only for changes about that person and your shared work, in every state (running, paused, stopped):

- **Tester's Rate:** the developer's Pay or Pay more on their work; a dispute of theirs decided; and, on a running test while they're still working, any call change between them and the developer since the page loaded (a request, a booking, a decline, a withdraw or a cancel: "Nora Lane requested a call for Thu, Nov 5, 3:00 PM"), because rating ends their part and closes their calls ahead and requests. With Alerts Off, call changes don't stop it.
- **Developer's Rate:** a dispute with that tester decided; and, on a running test while that tester is still working, the tester's call changes with them (not with Call changes ticked off in Alerts). (Their Rate opens only once everything the tester sent is settled, so nothing else about the work can change.)
- **Both:** the test stopping, closing, or that tester's work ending early, on a page opened before it (Stopped tests on Every page).

Rate ignores everything else: other testers joining, leaving or sending, other testers' calls, listing edits, keys, Alerts, every change of your own made in another tab (a Pay more or a call change included), the other person's suspension or hold, and the test being paused (a pause never stops Rate, even on a page opened before it). A Rate made in another tab only refuses this one plainly: "You already rated Ana Silva." / "You already rated Nora Lane.". A tester is never told that a developer is suspended or on a hold. A dispute opened meanwhile isn't a stop: Rate becomes disabled with its line (Rules: Ratings).

### Changes that never stop a press

- **A tester switching terms:** one quiet line in the developer's live box, "Ana switched to the new terms" (several in a day: "3 testers switched to the new terms"), because work already sent keeps its price, so nothing a press could change. It never joins a stop box that's open under a button either. A Keep shows nothing.
- **A rating from a tester whose part had already ended** (they left, were ended early, were blocked, or the test stopped): one quiet line in the developer's live box, "ff rated you. You can rate them until <date>." (or "…once everything they sent is settled."), never the stars. Ratings aren't an Alerts type. A working tester who rates is leaving, so that stops once under Testers leaving instead: "ff finished their part (rated you)".
- **Renames:** a change of the product or test name is info only. The press goes through with nothing added under it; the rename is recorded in each working tester's contract changes.
- **A key reported broken:** one quiet line in the developer's live box, "Ana reported their key doesn't work", with no bell.
- **An update of a piece already paid the most:** one quiet line in the developer's live box, "Ana sent an updated version of their written answers (already paid the most)", since nothing more can be paid on it.
- **Excerpt requests:** asking, Allow, Deny and the tester's Remove go through whatever changed, including on paused and stopped tests, on a page opened before a stop and after the developer's own changes in another tab; only a request that no longer exists is refused, and the same decision made in another tab is a plain refusal ("You already allowed this excerpt."). Only the tester removes an excerpt: a developer's Take down pressed on a page loaded before it went is refused with "Only the tester can remove an excerpt from their profile."
- **The tester's Leave and Block:** they go through on the first press (Leave's own confirm pop-up stays). They are still refused when they can't happen ("You already left this test.").
- **The developer's side of a dispute:** "Send your side" never stops. It's refused only with "This dispute was already decided." or "You already added your side."
- **Your own edits:** what you do on the page never stops you; your changes in another tab stop as described above. Spots and Open until never stop a tester either.

[image: Leave never stops: the listing changed after the page loaded, and Leave (after its own confirm) went through on the first press.]

### How it works

- **One stop per change.** Each change stops a press once, and the blue box lists every change since the page last showed one.
- **Nothing is swallowed.** What the page has "seen" starts at its first load in that tab and moves on only with lines the top box actually showed, so a redraw (a poll, a refresh after a press, "You now meet this test's needs", new terms) never skips a change. A stop or pause moment joins the other changes in one box, oldest first, instead of replacing them.
- **The second press goes through** unless something new happened after the box appeared; then it stops again, listing only the new changes.
- **Alerts can switch kinds off.** Developers tick types in Alerts on the test page; testers turn Alerts Off. A switched-off type no longer stops a press and shows greyed in the live box. Holds and pauses, a stopped test, a chargeback, the subscription ending, new terms for a tester, and refusals always stop.

[image: Ticked types no longer stop your buttons; they still show, greyed, in the live box.]

[image: A ticked type still shows in the live box, greyed ("You turned these stops off in Alerts" on hover).]

- **Red comes before blue.** A red field stops the press in the page, before it reaches the server, so a blue notice can only show on the next press, once the red is fixed.

[image: Open until 40 days ahead on the free plan with a test already open: the red at the field stops Post in the page, so the server's blue notice can't show yet.]

[image: With the date fixed, the next press reaches the server and the blue notice appears under Post the test.]

- **A refusal is not a stop.** A change that makes the press impossible (the test stopped, the work ended, the request was already taken, the offer was replaced) refuses it every time, with no "Press again". Those refusals are listed on each page.

## Why going Back is safe

Part of Every page.

- Back and Forward show the saved page at once, then refresh it from the server. Nothing on a restored page is trusted when a button is pressed.
- A page shown again by Back checks for changes at once, not at its next poll, and redraws with what it finds. A page whose refresh doesn't take (it still shows the old version a few seconds later) loads again, once, but never under someone's typing or an open box (Every page: How state stays consistent).
- Signing out, or the account being deleted, ends the session in every tab; signing out sends every tab to the sign-in page. Signing in as someone else switches every other tab to the new account, on the page the sign-in landed on. A restored page of the old account is never trusted: its presses are refused and the tab switches.
- A hold or suspension moves a developer's open pages to the dashboard top, including a restored one.
- Restored forms keep only text the person typed and didn't send. Amounts, offers and call times are read again from the server before any action.

## Developer dashboard

### What it's for and who sees it

The developer's home: every test, what needs them, booked calls and their own numbers. Only the developer sees it; testers have their own dashboard.

From Every page: the live box, long lists, number format, and the blue box when suspended or on hold.

### What's on it (top to bottom)

- **"Your bank wants you to confirm (N)"**, at the very top, above everything else (above the blue box too, during a hold or suspension), and hidden when nothing waits. It lists every charge made while the developer was away that their bank wants confirmed (a tester's Charge, a listed call's automatic charge, a dispute decision, an automatic retry of what's owed, something charged right after a chargeback hold lifted, a subscription renewal), soonest deadline first, in a plain scroll box about 3 rows tall: everything is in it, with no "Show N more". Each row says the amount, what it's for and the deadline, with **Confirm with your bank →**:
  - a sent part: "$12.50 on “Harbor Run” · Ana Silva's written answers · confirm by Nov 2, 3:10 PM" ("Ana Silva's recording" for a recording; "Ana Silva's work" for anything else of theirs);
  - a call: "$25.00 on “Harbor Run” · Ana Silva's call on Oct 30 · confirm by Nov 2, 3:10 PM";
  - a dispute decision: "$5.00 on “Harbor Run” · the team's decision on Ana Silva's dispute · confirm by …";
  - an automatic retry of what's owed during a payment hold: "$25.00 on “Harbor Run” · what you owe · confirm by …";
  - a payment the bank had reversed, charged again: worded as on the test page, naming the reversed payment's piece: "$25.00 on “Harbor Run” · a payment your bank had reversed (Ana Silva's written answers) · confirm by …";
  - a renewal, which belongs to no test: "$7.00 for your plan · confirm by Nov 2, 3:10 PM".
  - With under 24 hours left, the "confirm by" part is red and names the day: "confirm by today, 3:10 PM" (or "confirm by tomorrow, 3:10 PM" when the 24 hours cross midnight).
  - Every time here (the deadline, a call's day, "today" / "tomorrow" and the red under-24-hours check) is in the developer's own saved time zone, never the server's clock, so a developer far from the server sees the right day and time near midnight (with no saved zone yet: the server's clock).
  - It follows the dashboard's poll, with no full reload, during a hold or suspension too: a new charge appears; a row leaves when it's confirmed (here, in another tab or on the test page), when it's cancelled, and at its deadline (it then counts as declined: a payment hold for a charge, a lapse for a renewal); a row turns red as it passes 24 hours left.
  - Phones: the same box, full width, each row stacked with its button full width under it.
  - Only the developer side has it: a mixed account doesn't see it on its tester dashboard.
- **Blue box**, only during a hold or a developer-side suspension. Under it, while it lasts: "Excerpt requests" (Allow, Deny) and "Disputes: add your side" (each open dispute with the tester's text and the Add your side box). Each box scrolls once it holds more than 3 items.

  [image: Payment hold: the blue box at the top of the developer dashboard.]
- **"Done: you stopped <test>."** or **"Done: you ended <N> testers' work on <test>."** (green), after Stop the test or End early from a test page during a hold or suspension.
- **"Your account is active again · <time>"** (on a mixed account, "Your developer account is active again") for 3 days after a reinstatement.
- **Welcome line** with a "Post a test" shortcut next to the name.
- **Stat tiles:** Parts to review, Testers joined (all tests), Spent so far (accepted work), Pays on average (with "of the listing, over N pieces of work", as on the profile: Public profiles), Typical review, Rating. Big numbers shorten.
- **Ratings to give:** every rating the developer can give right now, newest first: "Rate Ana Silva on “Harbor Run” · 12 days left". A row appears once that tester has rated and everything they sent is settled (Rules: Ratings), and follows every Rate rule live: it goes when Rate stops being available (a dispute opens, the developer's own side is suspended or on a hold, they rated) and comes back when it is again. Hidden while the developer is suspended or on a hold.
- **Needs review:** one row per test with work or calls waiting (an update sent after a Pay counts while it can still be paid more), e.g. "Harbor Run · 3 parts to pay", newest first; in the last 7 days before the soonest of them closes it adds " · Pay closes on <date>." A piece whose Pay closed leaves it (Rules: Close-out). Each row opens that test's Needs you.
- **Needs you:** applications, rejoin asks, call requests, disputes and excerpt requests (Allow, Deny, with the question the excerpt answers) from every test, newest first. Each opens its own row on the test page, marked. A call request row ("<tester> asked for a live call · <test>", with "Answer in Live session →") opens the test page's Call requests panel at that request (Developer test page).
- Each of these lists, and Your tests below, is a plain scroll box (Long lists on Every page).
- **Calls:** every booked call, soonest first, each with "Check your call setup" (Call page). Join call appears only while the call can be joined. Each row opens the call on the test page, where it can be cancelled.
- **Your tests:** one card per test. Category icon left of the name; tags under the title (who can join, Open until, keys left, invites waiting, applications, unread messages, Mature, NDA); a status label; feedback bars with stats at the bottom right. After Open until the card says "Joining closed · testers working until <latest deadline>". Clicking a card opens the test page.
  - Three very different tests (a game test with written answers, a recording and live calls; a free web-app test; an invite-only test with access keys).

    [image: A game test with written answers, a recording and live calls.]

    [image: A free web-app test (Finance, Productivity): its written answers pay nothing.]

    [image: An invite-only test with access keys and an NDA.]
  - An open test with work waiting, a full test, a paused test (payment hold), a stopped test.

    [image: An open test with work waiting for review.]

    [image: A full test: 2 of 2 spots taken.]

    [image: A paused test: the developer is on a payment hold.]

    [image: A stopped test.]
- There is no pause countdown; the daily bell reminder says when a paused test closes.

### Buttons

- **Post a test:** opens Post a test.
- **Confirm with your bank →** (Your bank wants you to confirm): a charge on a test opens that test page at the piece's Confirm with your bank (the paused view during a hold or suspension), highlighted for a few seconds (the bell's one bank-confirmation line opens this box); the confirming happens there, with the same stop-once as Pay on that piece (Developer test page). A renewal opens Payments at its row, highlighted, where it's confirmed inline (Payments).
- **A test card:** opens Developer test page; a draft's card opens Post a test, filled in with the draft's details.
- **A Needs review or Needs you row:** opens that exact row on the test page; a call request opens the Call requests panel there, with that request highlighted for a few seconds. Accept and Decline happen only in that panel.
- **A Ratings to give row:** opens that tester's Rate box on the test page, highlighted for a few seconds. A row from an old page lands there with the usual refusal.
- **Join call** (Calls box): opens the call page.
- **Cancel ▾** (Calls box, only during a hold or suspension; otherwise it's on the test page): a reason box, required when the call is less than 24 hours away. The tester gets "<dev> cancelled the call on <day>." (the reason is in the email and on the call) and the call leaves both sides' upcoming lists.
- **Send your side** (Disputes box, while restricted): once per dispute, up to 2,000 characters, never stopped (Developer test page).
- **Pay what you owe** (in the blue box): opens Payments in a new tab.

### What can stop a press

- Cancel in the Calls box (only there during a hold or suspension) sends no page time, so nothing it hasn't seen can stop it: it goes through on the first press, and only the refusals below apply.
- Which changes stop a press, and the notice text: Why a press stops.
- Always refused, in blue under the button: "The call with <name> was already cancelled."; "The call has started, so it can't be cancelled. Report a no-show instead if someone didn't come."; "The call is less than 24 hours away: say why you're cancelling (the other side sees it)."
- Join call checks the call live and disappears when it's cancelled. A press from a page loaded before a cancellation reads "Your Join call didn't go through: <name> cancelled this call at <time>." under it, and the row leaves the list.

### How it looks in each state

- **Test** (on its card): Draft (a next round, not yet open); Open: "Accepting testers" (green) while at least one spot is free, "Full" (grey) when every spot is taken; Joining closed ("Joining closed · testers working until <date>"); Paused ("Paused: account suspended / payment hold / chargeback hold / paused by the team"); Stopped (a Stopped label); Closed ("Closed after 7 days paused").

  [image: Draft: the next round, not open yet.]

  [image: Closed after 7 days paused.]
- **Account active:** as above.
- **Payment hold, chargeback hold or developer side suspended:** the blue box at the top (under "Your bank wants you to confirm" when a charge waits for the bank), with the "Excerpt requests" and "Disputes: add your side" boxes under it; Ratings to give, Needs review and Needs you are hidden; each card (not a draft's) opens its test page in the paused view, with only Stop the test, End early and Cancel call (Every page); Calls rows get their own Cancel ▾; names are plain text with no hover card; Post a test leaves the header. Every other developer page except Payments, Settings and the bell list moves them back here.
- **Deleted:** every tab logs out.

### Bell lines that open this page

- "Your tests are running again", "Your developer account is active again" and the team's Approve that lifted a chargeback hold ("The team approved lifting your hold: …, and your tests are running again." or "…Your tests stay paused while your account is suspended."): the top.
- "Pay your calls: …" (several tests): Needs review.
- "Pay now: …" and "Closing soon: …": Needs you.
- "Your bank wants you to confirm …" (the one line for every waiting charge): the "Your bank wants you to confirm" box at the top.
- "<tester> finished <test>. You can rate them…" and "Reminder: rate <tester> …": Ratings to give.
- "The site was down from <a> to <b>…": the top.

## Post a test

### What it's for and who sees it

A developer creates a test: the product, the work, the pay, who can join and the deadlines. These become each tester's terms when they join. Only accounts with the developer side on can post ("Turn on “post tests” in your profile to post a test."), and the email must be confirmed.

From Every page: red field checks, kept drafts ("Not posted yet"), ranges shown next to boxes, number boxes, and email confirmation.

### What's on it

"* marks required" at the top. The form has four panels.

[image: The top of Post a test: “* marks the fields you have to fill in”.]

**What are testers trying?**

1. **Product** (up to 60 characters): the game, website, app or other product.
2. **Name of this test** (required, 10–60 characters).
3. **What should testers do** (required, 10–20,000, with the counter "N / 20,000"). Counters show on all three; under 10 characters the name's and the steps' read "6 / 10 minimum", and the red line, the same at the field and from the server, is "Name of this test: at least 10 characters (6 so far)." / "What should testers do: at least 10 characters (6 so far)." (trimmed). Edit checks the minimum only on a field that changed, so an older test's short name or steps never block saving another field.

   [image: Counters on Product, Name of this test and What should testers do.]
4. **Link to the product or build** (must start with https://).
5. **Categories** (at least 1): Game, Productivity, Social, Entertainment, Education, Health & fitness, Finance, Shopping, Business, Developer tools, AI, Other.
6. **Languages** and **devices, platforms or skills** needed.
7. **Mature (18+):** the grey line under the tick box: "Tick this if your test has mature content, and say why. Never allowed: sexual content involving minors, illegal content, real-world gore. Other graphic content must be marked Mature." Ticking it shows a required "Why is this test mature?" box at once, at least 5 characters, with the counter "N / 500". Its red line, the same at the field and from the server: empty, "Say why this test is mature (18+): it's required for mature content."; 1–4 characters, "Say a little more: at least 5 characters." The Terms of Use state the same rule (Terms of Use, Shared pages).

**Deliverables (parts)**: up to three.

- Written answers (1–20 questions, typed one per line, each up to 20,000 characters, with a counter "N / 20,000"; a long question sits in a scroll box about 6 lines tall), a Recording (with think-aloud), Live calls.
- **Questions are not required by default.** Each typed question has a **Required** tick beside it, unticked at first; a question is required only when it's ticked. Under the questions a grey line says the rule: "Testers answer at least one question. Tick Required on any they must answer. Each answer is at least 50 characters." A tester's answers go through once at least one question is answered and every Required one is (Tester session page). A Required question carries " · required" on the listing and the join page, "*" right after its words on the answer form and in answer sets, and a "required" mark in By question. Ticking or unticking Required changes the questions like any other question change on Edit (new terms are offered, and it counts toward the questions' 3 changes), but it never moves a tester's current answers into "Previous questions and answers": only a change to a question's words does.
- **At 20 questions** a line shows right in the questions box, under the last question: "At most 20 questions." It's a notice, not a mistake: not red, and Save and Post still work. Deleting a question removes it. The server still refuses more than 20 (on Post, Edit and posting a draft).
- Each part is a card with its tick, a price (calls: "up to $ /h"), a Free tick and a Required tick. Required is only a label: it blocks nothing, and shows on the listing, in testers' terms, on their session page and on the developer's tester rows. Free parts are clearly marked before a tester commits.
- Prices: written and recording $1–$1,000 each, calls $10–$200 an hour, or free. Each field shows its range, and rounds to the cent when it's left (Every page).
- **A ticked part needs a price.** $0 comes only from the Free tick, never from a blank box. A ticked part with its price (or rate) left empty is red at that box, on leaving it and on the press: "Enter a price for the written answers, or tick Free." / "Enter a price for the recording, or tick Free." / "Enter an hourly rate for live calls, or tick Free." The server refuses it with the same line.
- A price over $200 needs the tick "I confirm this part pays more than $200." when it's set over $200 or raised from $200 or less to over it.

**Who and how long**

- **Spots:** 1–10, with "At most 10 testers per test." under the field; the server refuses more (Post, Edit and Next round alike). A test that already had more than 10 keeps them: Edit can lower them, never raise them ("At most 10 testers per test. This test keeps its 20 spots: you can lower them, not raise them."), and its Next round starts at 10. It's a whole-number box: only digits, never below 1 (Every page).
- **Open until (last day to join):** required, at least 3 and at most 30 days after today (the test opens today), counted in the developer's own time zone. The line under it says, live as the date changes, that work can arrive after Open until: "Testers can join until this date. Each gets 14 days from when they join, so someone joining on Nov 20 has until Dec 4 to send work. You pay each piece within 7 days of it arriving or by that tester's deadline, whichever is later, or the tester can charge you." The days are today to the chosen date, and the last possible deadline is Open until plus those days. Each tester's deadline is the day they join plus those days. There's no separate "days after joining" field.
- **Who can join:**
  - Anyone can join: join at once while open with spots; first come, first served.
  - Approved only: testers apply; the developer accepts or declines. A subscriber perk: on the free plan it carries a "Subscribers" tag, and Post is refused: "Approved-only testers need a subscription ($7/month)." with a Payments link (new tab).
  - Invite only: never in Find tests, not even for invited testers; only invited testers join, from their dashboard's invites, the bell or the email.
  - Any test can also invite. An invite plus a waiting application or rejoin ask joins the tester at once.

**Optional**

- **NDA:** its own card, "Keep it confidential? (NDA, optional)", with three points: an NDA stops testers sharing the build or what they saw, and they must accept it before joining; with or without one, the developer can always use the feedback they receive; testers can never show work from a test under an NDA as excerpts on their profile, and without one only with the developer's permission. The text box, up to 1,000 characters ("N / 1,000"): "Leave blank for no NDA. If filled, testers must accept it before joining." Testers accept it on Join, Apply and Ask to rejoin. There's no preview on Post or Edit; testers see it on the join page and their session page. The NDA template gets a legal review before launch.
- **Access keys:** one per line; each new tester gets a free one.

**The Charge tick**, required, just above Post the test (on posting a draft too): "I understand that a tester can charge my card, up to the listing price, for work they sent by their own deadline that I haven't paid once 7 days have passed since they sent it and that deadline has passed, and listed calls 7 days after they take place. How payments work" (the end covers the automatic charge of listed calls: Rules, Money). "How payments work" opens the Terms of Use's section 4 in a new tab (Terms of Use, Shared pages). The Open until line above explains it.

**The updated-Terms tick**, only while the account hasn't agreed to the current Terms of Use version: one more required tick above the button, in the same style, "I agree to the updated Terms of Use. Read what changed" (Terms of Use, Shared pages). The order above Post the test is the updated-Terms tick, then the Charge tick.

All numbers are in Rules.

### Buttons

- **Post the test:** checks everything, then opens the test, lists it according to who can join (Find tests for Anyone and Approved only; invites only for Invite only) and goes straight to the new test's page. There is no summary step: the developer reviews the fields they filled in on this page before pressing it.

  [video: Filling in the four panels, pressing Post the test, landing on the new test's page.]

  [image: Anyone can join: the new test shows in testers' Find tests.]

  [image: Invite only: not in Find tests; the invited tester sees the invite on their dashboard.]
  - Elsewhere: unless it's invite only, the test appears in Find tests and in matching testers' "New tests for you" (Tester dashboard, Find tests and the join page), and the developer lands on Developer test page.

### What can stop a press

- **Red, at the field** (when you leave the field, and on Post; the server checks too, with the same words), listed by the button as on every form (Mistakes in a form, Every page).

  [video: Each box turns red when you leave it; Post then finds what only the server checks (a link without https://, a key typed twice).]

  [image: Red when you leave the field: required fields empty, a price under $1, spots over the limit.]

  [image: Red when you leave the field: Open until only 2 days ahead (it must be 3 to 30), and fewer keys than spots.]
  - A required field left empty; a name or steps under 10 characters.
  - A price outside its range, or over $200 without the tick; a ticked part with no price and no Free tick.
  - Open until missing, or less than 3 or more than 30 days after today.
  - More than 10 spots ("At most 10 testers per test.").
  - Fewer access keys than spots; a key typed twice ("<key> is typed more than once. Each key goes in once."); a key that's too long.
  - A link not starting with https:// ("The build link must start with https://"); http:// is refused too.
  - A question over its limit ("Keep each question to 20,000 characters.") or an NDA over 1,000 ("Keep the NDA to 1,000 characters."), said in red at the field, not only by the counter.
  - Mature ticked with no reason, or one under 5 characters (the two lines under Mature, above).
  - The Charge tick left unticked: Post the test (or posting a draft) is refused by the server until it's ticked, red at the tick: "Tick to confirm you understand how payments work.", listed with the other mistakes.
  - The updated-Terms tick left unticked (when shown): refused the same way, red at the tick: "Tick to agree to the updated Terms of Use."
  - Just above the button: "Fix the N mistakes above, then press Post the test again." with one linked bullet per red field.

    [image: Every red at once: empty required fields, a price under $1, 60 spots, Open until 45 days ahead, an NDA over 1,000 characters, a question over its limit and fewer keys than spots, with the linked list above Post the test.]
- **Blue, under the button:** "Confirm your email first. We sent a link to <email>." with "Resend the link" under the button.
- **Blue, under the button, on the free plan:** with an open test already (Open, Full or Paused; a test whose joining has closed doesn't count), Post is refused: "You already have an open test. Subscribe ($7/month) to run several at once." with a link to Payments (new tab). The form keeps what was typed. The same applies when a draft is posted.

  [image: A second open test on the free plan: Post is refused under the button, and the form keeps what was typed.]
- **Blue, under the button, at 10 open tests** (subscribers): "You have 10 open tests, the most you can have at once. Stop or close one to post another." This covers posting a draft too. The count is checked on the server at the press: if another tab stopped a test meanwhile, the post simply goes through.

  [image: At 10 open tests, Post is refused under the button and the form keeps its text.]
- **Red comes first:** a red field stops Post in the page, so the server's blue notice shows only on the next press, once the red is fixed.

  [image: Open until 40 days ahead on the free plan with a test already open: the red at the field stops Post in the page, so the blue notice can't show yet.]

  [image: With the date fixed, the next press reaches the server and the blue notice appears under Post the test.]
- How presses stop and are refused across the app: Why a press stops.

### How it looks in each state

- **Account active:** the form.
- **Payment hold, chargeback hold or developer side suspended:** the page is closed; opening it moves the developer to the dashboard top, and Post a test leaves the header.
- **A draft** (a Next round not opened yet) isn't a test page: it opens here, filled in with its saved details. Its card, its link, its Edit page and Next round all land here. Post the test opens it for testers. The draft can also be deleted here (confirm pop-up).

  [image: The subscription ended and the developer has an open test: posting the round-2 draft is refused with "You already have an open test. Subscribe ($7/month) to run several at once." and Open Payments.]
- A new test starts Open. Later changes go through Edit test.

## Developer test page

### What it's for and who sees it

The place to run one test: what changed, what needs the developer, who is in it, calls, invites and answers. Only the test's developer sees it. Admins don't get this page: they see the disputed work in their console and their own boxes on the public test page.

From Every page: the live box, stop-once, long lists, pop-ups, number format, the paused and stopped banners, and the blue box when suspended or on hold.

### What's on it (top to bottom; every panel folds)

1. **Overview**: title, status pill, who-can-join badge, category, part and deadline badges, keys-left badge, "terms · spots", and "Open until (last day to join) <date>" on the right (changed only in Edit test); after that date, "Joining closed · testers working until <latest deadline>". Buttons: Edit, Alerts, Next round (or "Go to the next round"), End early, Stop.

   [image: Overview: title, status, badges, terms and spots, Open until, and the Edit, Alerts, Next round, End early and Stop buttons.]
2. **Banners** when they apply: the red banner for a paused or stopped test (title, reason, failed presses, what's still allowed: Every page); "<test> closes in …" when under 3 days are left before joining closes.
3. **Live box**. It also shows quiet lines that never stop a press: "Ana switched to the new terms" with "See their contract" (scrolls to Ana's row with their contract shown; several in a day: "3 testers switched to the new terms"; a Keep shows nothing), "Ana reported their key doesn't work", and "ff rated you. You can rate them until <date>." from a tester whose part had ended (Why a press stops). Quiet lines never join a stop box. A new dispute links to its Needs you row. When a tester rates, the page follows on its next poll, without reloading: the live line, that tester's Rate box on their row, a "Rate ff · 14 days left" row in Needs you, and the dashboard's Ratings to give.

   [video: Ana (right, phone) sends their answers and switches to the new terms; the developer's live box updates and See their contract scrolls to their row.]
4. **Access keys** (read only, shown only when the test uses keys; never stops a press):
   - **Keys that don't work** (amber, at the top, only for current working testers whose key is marked as not working): "Ben Okafor's key doesn't work (HR-2222)", or stacked, "2 testers' keys don't work: Ben Okafor (HR-2222), Cam Lee (HR-3333)". Each row has a select tick and a "Works now" tick (unticked at first); above them "Select all (N)", below them one "Dismiss selected" button, disabled until something is ticked and reading "Dismiss 2" when two are. Dismissing hides those rows for this developer; a row with "Works now" ticked also clears the key's broken mark (Buttons, below). A new report on the same key shows it again. The rows leave by themselves when the tester leaves or finishes.
   - "Free (not given to anyone yet)": the free keys as small chips with the count and the open spots ("4 free · 2 open spots: each new tester gets one."). A key typed back from a tester who left or finished says so: "HR-3333 · reused (Ben Okafor)" or "HR-2222 · reported as broken (Ben Okafor)".
   - "Given": each given key with its tester ("KEY-1111 · Ana Silva"); a key reported as not working shows crossed out.
   - Free and Given each sit in their own scroll box about 4 lines tall, and they stack on phones. One line: "If a tester's key doesn't work, delete a free key in Edit and send it to them in a message." ("in Edit" opens Edit's access keys in a new tab). It updates live when keys are added or deleted, when a joining or working tester is given one (Edit test), and when a key is reported. Key rules are in Edit test.

   [image: The read-only Access keys notice: free keys as chips with the open spots, and each given key with its tester.]

   [image: Keys that don't work: two rows, each with its tick and Works now; Select all; Dismiss selected.]

   [image: "Dismiss 2": Ben's key is marked as working again, Cam's warning is only hidden.]

   [image: The warning is gone; HR-2222 is no longer crossed out, HR-3333 still is.]

   [image: The notice chips: "HR-1111 · reused (Ana Silva)" and "HR-3333 · reported as broken (Cam Lee)".]
5. **Needs you**, grouped per person, each with a fold for their sent parts: one green Pay for each sent piece and each call that took place; each open Rate, "Rate ff · 14 days left", linking to that tester's Rate box; applications (Accept, Decline); rejoin asks (with the time they'd get back); call requests ("<tester> asked for a live call: <time> · <length> · <Listed or Not listed>", with no buttons of their own: the row and its "Answer in Live session →" link open the Call requests panel at that request, highlighted for a few seconds, and the row leaves once the request is answered, withdrawn or closed); disputes, with "Add your side (optional, once; the team reads both)"; excerpt requests (Allow, Deny), each with the question it answers above the quote in small grey text (2 lines, then "Show more"), and, when the test now has an NDA, "<test> has an NDA now: if you allow this, it stays hidden while <test> has an NDA.". In a piece's last 7 days before it closes its row says "Pay closes on <date>."; once it closed unpaid, the row leaves and the piece reads "Closed on <date>: not paid." (Rules: Close-out). A charge made while the developer was away that their bank wants confirmed shows under its piece: "Your bank wants you to confirm a $X payment for <piece>, made while you were away. Confirm it by Mon, Nov 2, 3:10 PM." with **Confirm with your bank** (Buttons, below). The piece is named as on the dashboard ("Ana Silva's written answers", "Ana Silva's call on <time>", "the team's decision on Ana Silva's dispute", or for a reversed payment charged again "a payment your bank had reversed (Ana Silva's written answers)"), and the "Confirm it by" time and a call's time are in the developer's own saved time zone. A piece's written answers, shown formatted with their attachments and recordings (each downloadable or playable), sit in a scroll box about 16 lines tall when they're longer, laid out as every answer set is (each question a small grey label, its answer indented under it: Tester session page); the Pay box stays under it. A part sent for earlier questions is labelled "Sent for the questions until <date>" and is paid at the price it was sent at. A listed part sent after the tester's own deadline is labelled "Sent late (deadline was <date>)": its Pay box starts at the listing and takes any amount, $0 included (below the listing needs the reason the tester reads), and it never opens Charge. An update the tester sent after a Pay is its own row while the piece can still be paid more, "Ana sent an updated version after you paid ($10.00 paid · up to $990.00 more)", with that piece's Pay more box (Buttons, below). A piece the team closed reads "The team closed this for $0: “<reason>”".

   [image: Needs you, grouped per person: Pay for sent work, a call to pay for its measured length, a dispute waiting for your side.]

   [image: On an Approved-only test: applications to Accept or Decline, and a rejoin ask with the time they'd get back.]

   [image: A dispute waiting for the developer's side, and a call that took place, measured by the app.]
6. **Testers**:

   [image: Testers: the avatar row with Ana's hover card, and one row per tester with parts sent, pay and their contract.]
   - An avatar row (about 12, then "+N more joined · M open": joined testers beyond the shown avatars, and spots still open; a part that is 0 is left out). Each hover card shows rating, accepted parts, the profile link and "Chat with <name>"; no excerpts (they're on the tester's profile).
   - A scroll box of tester rows with a name search: required and optional parts sent, paid per part and in total, the agreed scope (their current contract, switched or kept), their Rate box once it opens (Buttons, below), "Pay more" on every paid piece (written answers, recordings and calls, listed or unlisted) until its cap is reached or it closes (Buttons, below), a "Previous questions and answers" fold with earlier sets (only what was sent, never the tester's drafts; each question a small grey label above its indented answer, "(no answer)" in grey for an empty one), "See their contract" (with their contract changes, renames included), and the excerpts they show from this test (only the tester can remove one). After a Keep the row says "On their terms from <date>". An update sent after a piece was paid the most reads "Updated after you paid · already paid the most" on that piece.
   - **The Live call part** shows the total paid for all their calls on the right, like the other parts ("Live call · Not required · paid $45.00"). Under it, a fold "Calls (N)" lists every call that's over, newest first, each with its outcome badge: "Took place · 29 min · paid $14.50", "Took place · 29 min · not paid yet", "Cancelled by Ana · 3:05 PM" with the reason under it, "Nobody joined", "Missed: the site was down", "Missed: the test was paused". Each call that took place has its own Pay or Pay more box under it, with the room for that call. More than 3 calls sit in a scroll box about 3 calls tall, with those boxes inside (Long lists on Every page). Calls still waiting for a first payment also link to their Needs you item.
   - The bottom line: "Paid in all: $X · Calls: 1 booked ahead, 1 done" ("done" is a call that took place).
   - "No longer working (N)" below, each row tagged Left, Ended early, Stopped or Removed.

   [image: A tester row's "Calls (4)": took place and paid (with Pay more), cancelled by Ana with their reason, nobody joined, missed because the site was down.]
7. **Contract changes** (folded, after Testers): one entry per Save that changed the terms or the names, newest first. Each entry has its date and time and the fields that changed; before → after for each field, shown as in the offer box (the NDA and steps in scroll boxes, more than 3 questions in their scroll box, parts and prices as terms lines); who chose what ("Switched: Ana Silva, Ben Okafor · Kept: Cam Lee · Not decided yet: Dee Marsh"; testers who left since are marked "(left)", and each name opens that tester's row); and the Save's effects on others ("2 applications closed · 1 invite re-sent"). Renames are entries too, and so is the developer's own Stop: "<time> · You stopped the test" with the reason testers read, in italics in its scroll box. The entries scroll when there are many and update live. Each tester row's "See their contract" stays that tester's own path.

   [image: Contract changes: one entry per Save, before → after, Switched / Kept / Not decided yet with links to the rows, and each Save's effects.]
8. **Live session** (it follows the page live: new requests from either side, bookings and cancellations show on each tester card as they happen): the rate line; tick testers (each with their terms and "N of 3 booked") or Select all; the request form, checked live for clashes (Buttons, below); the **Call requests** panel: every waiting request on this test in both directions, soonest first, each with the tester, the time(s) (Once, Daily or Weekly, N calls or Forever), the length, the rate, who asked, and Accept and Decline (theirs) or Withdraw (yours), in a plain scroll box about 5 rows tall. The rate reads "Contract $30/h" for a listed call, or "Not listed"; when that tester is on older terms than the listing it says so: "Their terms: $30/h (the listing is now $24/h)". A shown request that closes or is answered elsewhere says so in its row within seconds, and its buttons go, without waiting for the page to refresh, e.g. "Closed: the tester finished their part", "Closed: the tester left", "Closed: the test was stopped", "Withdrawn", "Declined" or "Booked". A refused Accept, Decline or Withdraw names the press that was made: "Your Withdraw didn't go through: …"; then one card per tester with only their calls ahead and in progress, in a scroll box about 3 calls tall, soonest first, each call ahead with "Check your call setup" (Call page), and call sets. Calls that are over move to the tester's row (above). The list of tester cards scrolls on its own once it's taller than about 2 screens, with the tick list and the request form staying above it. Calls can be requested with any tester, whether or not a live call is a listed part. Each tester shows "Live call · $X/h" when a call is listed and in their contract, otherwise the label "Not listed"; any part not listed in a tester's contract carries the same label. All of one tester's calls on a test can come to at most $1,000 together (Rules); when less than an hour's worth is left at that tester's rate (or under $200 for calls that aren't listed), a small grey line says so: "$X left of the $1,000 for calls with Ana on this test." It never stops anything.

   [image: Live session: each tester's booked calls in a scroll box about 3 calls tall, soonest first; calls that are over moved to the tester's row.]

   [image: The Call requests panel: waiting requests from both sides, soonest first, with Accept / Decline or Withdraw; one highlighted after opening it from Needs you.]

   [image: "$100.00 left of the $1,000 for calls with Ana Silva on this test.": small, no stop.]
9. **Invite testers**: "Invited" (waiting invites); the device and language ticks (the test's current needs); "Search by name", with the "Find testers" results right under it in their own scroll box (hidden while the box is empty); then "People you've worked with" (with "Worked with you on N tests", a fold linking those tests, each with how that tester's part went: "jj · ended", "Harbor Run · in it now", "Sky Lab · left", "Kelp Forest · finished" when they rated) and "Suggested" (top 5). The panel follows the page live: when the test's needs or a tester's devices or languages change, the ticks, each row's "Doesn't list …" tag, the greyed rows and the Suggested order are worked out again; ticked testers stay ticked (unless now greyed) and the search text stays.

   [image: Invite testers: People you've worked with and Suggested, with orange labels for missing needs.]

   [image: Find testers by name: Dee is ticked; the orange label shows the need they miss.]

   [image: Invite anyway: the inline box lists the needs Dee doesn't meet.]
10. **By question:** each written question (answers grouped by the exact question text, each long question in its scroll box) with every tester's answer, its time, a "required" mark on a question the developer ticked Required (not required is the default, so it carries no mark) and Paid, Rejected (older work) or Not reviewed yet.

After Edit's Save, the page opens with a green line under the panels: "Saved. Offered the new terms to N working testers." (or, when nobody's terms changed, "Saved. New testers join on the new listing; testers already in keep their contract."), followed by what the Save did to others: "Closed N applications: <names>.", "Invites re-sent on the new listing: <names>." and "Not re-invited: <names>." when they apply.

### Buttons

**Overview**

- **Edit:** opens Edit test.
- **Alerts:** "Tick what should not stop your buttons. A payment hold or pause, a stopped test and final refusals always stop." Kinds: Joins and applications; Testers leaving (a tester who deleted their account or was suspended counts as leaving); Work sent; Call changes; Payments (going through or failing); Disputes; Decisions by the team; Listing changes. What you did in another tab isn't a kind: it can't be switched off (Why a press stops). Ignored lines still show, greyed, in the live box. Under each stop box: "Don't stop me for: <kind>". The menu and those chips are one setting: pressing a chip saves the type as ticked, and at once the chip reads "<kind> (off)", the menu shows it ticked and every other stop box's chip follows; unticking it in the menu turns the chips back. The developer's other tabs and devices follow on their next poll. Each tick saves only its own type, so an older menu never undoes a chip's press. On a paused or stopped test the menu lists only the kinds that can still happen (Why a press stops), and keeps the developer's ticks.

  [image: Alerts: tick what should not stop your buttons.]
- **Next round:** makes a draft copy titled "(round N)" and goes straight to Post a test, filled in with its details; nothing is public until the developer presses Post the test there; records stay with the original round. Refused after a team stop.

  [image: Next round: the draft “(round 2)” opens in Post a test, filled in; Post the test opens it for testers.]
- **Go to the next round** (in place of Next round once the test has its next-round draft): a press, not a plain link. On a running test it stops once like Next round, for the same changes and Alerts types (a tester sent or updated work, left, a call change, a payment…), in the blue box under the overview's buttons ("fbbb updated their written answers"); the second press opens the draft. With nothing new it opens the draft at once. On a stopped, closed or paused test it follows the same stop-once, and a page opened before the stop or pause stops once for it, like the other presses there (Stopped tests on Every page); it's never refused for a pause. If the draft was deleted meanwhile, it's refused: "This test has no next round any more: its draft was deleted."
- **End early** (confirm pop-up "End their work now"): tick testers (or "All (N)") and give one reason they read. The "Why? (each tester you tick reads it)" box is required, at least 5 characters (after trimming) and up to 10,000 ("N / 10,000"). The End early button stays disabled until the box has at least 5 characters and at least one tester is ticked, greyed with the hint "Say why first" while the box is empty; with 1–4 characters, leaving the box or pressing shows the red "Say a little more: at least 5 characters." The server refuses an empty, too-short or too-long reason anyway. The reason survives a reload until it's sent (kept drafts, Every page). Their work ends; calls ahead are cancelled and a call in progress with them ends (Call page); what they sent still waits for Pay; unsent parts close with no pay; they can be invited back later. Testers read the reason in italics in a scroll box on their session page, and in full in the email; the bell line doesn't carry it. Tester bell: "Your work on <test> was ended early"; calls: "…cancelled because your session was ended."

  [image: End early waits for a reason: the button stays disabled with "Say why first"; the counter reads 0 / 10,000.]

  [image: With a reason and a tester ticked, the button is enabled; the counter shows the length.]
- **Stop** (confirm pop-up "Are you sure you want to stop “<test>”?"): ends every tester's work, cancels every call and request ahead (a call in progress ends at that moment: Call page), takes no new testers, and can't be edited or reopened. What was sent still waits for Pay. The pop-up has a required "Why? (testers read it)" box, the same as End early's: at least 5 characters and up to 10,000 ("N / 10,000"), Stop disabled until then ("Say why first" while empty, the red "Say a little more: at least 5 characters." under 5), and kept across a reload. The server refuses an empty reason ("Say why you're stopping the test (testers read it)."), a short one ("Say a little more: at least 5 characters.") or a too-long one. It's needed on a paused view too. Testers read the reason in italics in a scroll box on their session page, under the stopped line, and in full in the email ("<dev> stopped “<test>”: “<reason>”"); the bell line "<test> was stopped" doesn't carry it. It's also in Contract changes (above), each tester's contract changes, and the admin's test timeline ("The developer stopped the test: “<reason>”"). The team's Stop keeps its own reason, shown only to the developer. Edit, End early and Stop disappear, and so do Invite testers and the call-request tools; Next round stays (not after a team stop).

  [image: Stop: the confirm pop-up “Are you sure you want to stop …?”.]

  [image: The tester's page after Stop (phone): the stopped line says what they can still do.]

**Needs you and the Pay box**

- **Pay:** there's no Reject: the developer pays an amount from $0 up. Every Pay and Pay more box (Needs you, tester rows, calls) has one green Pay button, the amount, "Note to <tester> (optional)" with its counter "N / 1,000", the ticks, and beside the amount the room left, which is the piece's cap in all (Rules: Extra pay cap): "up to $1,000.00 in all" on a first Pay of written answers or a recording, listed or not; "up to $990.00 more ($1,000.00 in all)" on Pay more; "up to $X ($200/h × 29 min)" on a first Pay of an unlisted call; or "up to $X (what's left of the $1,000 for calls)" when that limit is the one that binds. An amount above it turns red at the box as they type ("This is more than this piece can be paid (up to $1,000)." / "This is more than this piece can be paid (up to $990 more, $1,000 in all).", "This is more than you can pay for this call (up to $X at $200 an hour).", "This is more than you can pay for this call (up to $X: what's left of the $1,000 for calls on this test)."), and Pay is disabled; the server checks again, and at the cap it answers "Paid the most this piece can be paid ($X)."
  - The box starts at the listing (or, for a call, the rate × its measured length); for an unlisted part it starts empty. The amount rounds to the cent when the box is left (Every page).
  - The note becomes required at $0: "Say why you're paying $0 (the tester reads it)." Paying a listed part less than the listing also needs it: "Say why you're paying less than the listing's $X (the tester reads it)." Both are red at the box. The tester reads the note with the payment on their session page and in the email, never in the bell line.
  - The line under the button: listed, "<tester> can dispute the amount once."; unlisted, "This part isn't listed: pay any amount, $0 included. <tester> can dispute the amount once."
  - A listed written part or recording sent on time also says when Charge opens: "If you don't pay by Nov 5, Ana Silva can charge you $8.00, their contract price, or let it go." The date is in the developer's own saved time zone, the same day the tester reads ("If <dev> hasn't paid by …"); so are a listed call's automatic-charge lines on both sides.
  - Paying more than the listing is allowed up to the piece's cap in all (Rules: Extra pay cap). After a piece is paid, its row keeps the same box as "Pay more" (Testers, below).
  - Every Pay above $0 needs the tick "I understand this payment of $X can't be refunded and is charged now.", naming the exact amount (a changed amount unticks it); over $200 the tick "I confirm this payment is over $200." is needed too. Paying $0 needs no tick.
  - The stop-once check runs first; then the card is charged at once, and nothing shows success before the result. If the bank wants the developer to confirm, its check opens inline, directly under the Pay or Pay more button (Rules: Your bank wants to confirm): confirmed, the payment goes through; cancelled or failed, "Your bank didn't confirm the payment, so nothing was charged." and no hold, and pressing Pay again is a new attempt that can go through. While it's open the button stays disabled and the piece is locked, and it's never charged twice.
  - Pay closes on the piece's own closing date (Rules: Close-out); a press after it is refused: "Pay closed on <date>."
  - A $0 payment closes the row like any payment. The tester reads "Paid $0: "<reason>"". Any amount can be disputed once, $0 included.
  - Tester bell: "<dev> paid you $X for your <part> on “<test>”" ("<dev> paid you $X more…" for a Pay more); developer: a receipt. "Paid so far" updates on both sides.

  [image: One green Pay; beside the amount the room left ("up to $1,000.00 in all"); the note is optional; "Ana Silva can dispute the amount once."]

  [image: Over the cap, red as you type: "This is more than this piece can be paid (up to $990 more, $1,000 in all)." and Pay is disabled.]

  [image: At $0 the note is required, in red at the box: "Say why you're paying $0 (the tester reads it)."]

  [video: An amount over the cap turns red at once; $0 asks for a reason; a valid amount with its tick pays.]
- **An unlisted part** ("Not listed"): the box starts empty. Written answers or a recording can be paid up to $1,000 in all, Pay and Pay more together; a call up to $200 an hour for its measured length in all, within the $1,000 for all of that tester's calls on the test. Paying $0 is allowed and closes the row. It gets the same review reminders as listed work until its review date, then they stop; the row stays until it's paid, even $0. It never opens Charge.

  [image: No Reject anywhere: "This part isn't listed: pay any amount, $0 included. Ben Okafor can dispute the amount once."]
- **A declined card** starts a payment hold at once: "Your card was declined, so <name> wasn't paid $X for the <part> yet. Your tests are paused until you pay what you owe: open Payments." The tester sees "payment may be delayed". Holds are in Rules. Any other problem with the payment service is not a decline: "Payments are having a problem right now. Nothing was charged; try again shortly.", with no hold and nothing owed. The same line answers a Pay or Pay more whose reservation timed out under load (rolled back, nothing charged).
- **Pay more on an update** (the update's Needs you row): any amount up to the piece's cap, with the usual ticks; $0 closes the row with an optional note, nothing charged, and the tester reads "<dev> paid $0 more for your update.". It pays the same piece: no new piece, no Charge, no review reminders (the part was already reviewed). The row leaves once Pay more is pressed (any amount) or Pay more closes. An update at the cap never gets a row (Testers, below). Disputes are unchanged: an update doesn't reopen one, and the existing rules apply to the piece's total.
- **Accept / Decline an application or rejoin ask:** Accept joins them ("You're in: <test>" or "You're back in <test>: your deadline is <date>."); Decline tells them it wasn't accepted.
- **Add your side** to a dispute: optional (an admin can decide without it), once, up to 2,000 characters with a counter. The box stays until the admin decides, with no time limit, on a stopped, paused or closed test too, and in the dashboard's "Disputes: add your side" box while restricted. Once sent, it becomes the read-only text. "Send your side" never stops; it's refused only with "This dispute was already decided." or "You already added your side." The tester's next dispute press stops once; the admin sees the side appear live ("added <time>"), and their Decide stops once if it arrived after their page loaded (Administrator console).
- **A call that took place:** the app measures its length: "Length: 29 min (both in the call 2:21–2:50 PM)", up to 150% of the booked length; "Call in progress / measuring" until it's final. Nobody enters a length. The call's Pay box has "Rate ($/h)" and the amount, which fill each other live: typing a rate fills the amount (rate × length), typing an amount fills the rate (amount ÷ length × 60, to the cent per hour). The one typed last wins, and the server works out the other and checks the caps. A listed call's rate starts at the contract rate; an unlisted call's starts empty and can't go over $200/h. There's no way to change the length: the developer just presses Pay. Paying less than the measured length × the rate needs the usual reason ("Say why you're paying less …"), and the tester can dispute the amount once. Calls whose length was lowered before this changed keep it as recorded.
  - **A listed call is charged automatically 7 days after it** if the developer hasn't paid it by then (the date moves later with pauses and outages). Until then its Pay box says so: "If you don't pay by Nov 5, 2026, it's charged automatically: $25.00 (30 min × $50.00/h)." (the measured length × the contract rate, within what's left of the $1,000 for that tester's calls). There's no Charge or Let it go for a listed call, anywhere. What happens at that date: Rules, Money. An unlisted call is never charged automatically: it waits for Pay, any amount, with the weekly "Pay your calls" line.

  [image: The measured length: "Length: 29 min (both in the call …)"; the Pay box's Rate and Amount use it.]

  [image: Rate and Amount fill each other: typing $40/h filled $19.33; typing $10.00 filled the rate back ($20.69/h).]
- **Confirm with your bank** (under a piece, for a charge made while the developer was away: a tester's Charge, a listed call's automatic charge, a dispute decision, an automatic retry, or something charged right after a chargeback hold lifted): it runs exactly Pay's stop-once check on that piece first: the same check, under the same lock on the test, ignoring the same Alerts types, so whatever would stop Pay on that piece stops Confirm, and nothing else does (for a reversed payment charged again, the piece is the one the bank reversed). Any change the page hasn't shown (a stop, close or End early, a dispute, another tester's dispute included, the tester's Charge or Let it go, a Pay or Pay more in another tab, a call change…, as set in Alerts) stops it once in the blue box under the button; the second press opens the same inline check as a press. Refusals stay plain, with no stop box: "You already confirmed this payment." (confirmed in another tab), "This confirmation expired: nothing was charged." and "This payment was cancelled: nothing was charged.". Payments and the dashboard's "Your bank wants you to confirm" box link straight here, with the piece highlighted. The page doesn't refresh itself while the check is open.
- **Rate** a tester, on their row: the box ("Your rating of <name> is final. Both ratings show once you rate.", 1–5 stars and an optional comment) is hidden until that tester has rated and everything they sent is settled, and then stays open for 14 days; it's disabled, with a grey line, while a dispute with them is open. Every rule, and the refusals: Rules, Ratings.

**Testers**

- **Pay more** on every paid piece (current and past testers; written answers, recordings and calls, listed or unlisted): the same Pay box, with its "up to $Y", the same ticks and the optional note, until the piece's cap is reached, and at most until the test's last money date: Pay more closes together with the last Pay / Charge on the test (Rules: Close-out; a Next round has its own dates). In its last 7 days the box shows a small grey line, "Pay more closes on <date>."; after that the box is gone, and a press from an old page is refused: "Pay more closed on <date>." Disputes and delayed payments are not affected. The part line already shows what was paid, so the box doesn't repeat it. **Once a piece reaches its cap** (Rules: Extra pay cap), a grey line takes the box's place while Pay more is open: "Paid the most this piece can be paid ($20.00)." (Pay and every Pay more counted, a dispute's raise too). Each call that took place has its own box in the "Calls (N)" fold. Unpaid pieces are paid in Needs you. Tester bell: "<dev> paid you $X more for your <part> on “<test>”".
- **Chat with <name>** (hover card, row, profile): opens the per-test chat. After a block either way, a suspension or any other refusal: "Unable to chat with this tester."

**Live session**

- **Request a time:** up to 3 time rows, each Once, Daily or Weekly (2–8 calls, or Forever), and one length (10–180 min). Each time (a Daily or Weekly set's first one) must start at least 5 minutes from now on the live site; when the app runs outside production (local testing) the lead is 1 minute, or what CALL_MIN_AHEAD_MINUTES in the server configuration sets. The server passes its own lead to the request forms, so form and server always agree. The time box won't take an earlier time ("Pick a time in the future.", red at the box), and the server refuses one with the same line. The call room still opens 10 minutes before the start. The length is also capped by what's left of the $1,000 for that tester's calls on this test, at the call's rate (the contract rate for a listed call, $200/h for one that isn't listed), in whole minutes: the hint shows it, "10–95 min (the $1,000 for calls with Ana)", and a longer length is red at the box and refused on the server: "At most 95 minutes: the $1,000 for calls with Ana on this test." The row keeps "Length", the box and "min" together on one line, never squeezed (on phones too), with the hint beside them; the red line goes under the whole row. With several testers ticked, the smallest cap applies, named. Accepting a waiting request that's longer, on either side, is refused with the same line. There's no "max call time" field. The tester gets the request; if they asked for the same time, it books at once ("Booked: you both picked <time>"). A request, an offer or an Accept that would take a tester's calls on this test over $1,000 is refused on both sides: "Calls with Ana on this test can come to at most $1,000; $X is left, so this call can't be booked."

  [image: Refused at the limit: "Calls with Ana Silva on this test can come to at most $1,000; $100.00 is left, so this call can't be booked."]

  [video: The warning under an hour's worth, then a 60-minute request at $150/h refused with what's left.]
- **Clash checks while filling Request a time** (the same on the tester's side): each time row is checked as it's typed and whenever the page's data changes, for every ticked tester and every call in a Daily or Weekly set (for Forever, its first 8):
  - **The same time as the other side's waiting request** (same start, length and repeat) isn't red: a blue line under the row, "fbbb asked for exactly this: Request a time books it." The press books it at once ("Booked: you both picked <time>"), and both requests leave the panel.
  - **Red, an overlap with a call or waiting request between the two of you:** "Overlaps fbbb's request for Mon, Nov 2, 1:38–1:48 PM." / "Overlaps your request to fbbb for …" / "Overlaps your call with fbbb on …".
  - **Red, an overlap with your other calls** (another tester, any of your tests): "You have a call with Ana Silva then (1:30–2:00 PM)." The tester gets the same for their own calls.
  - **Red, two rows of the same form overlapping:** on the later row, "Overlaps time 1 in this request (…)."
  - A set with a clash names the first one: "The 3rd call (Thu, Nov 12) overlaps …".
  - The limits (3 waiting requests, 3 booked times, the $1,000 for a tester's calls) are red at the form too, with the same words as the refusals below.
  - Red rows follow the form rule (Mistakes in a form, Every page), and the server checks everything again on the press.
  - **A press never clashes with itself.** From the press until the page reloads, the checks use what was there when Request a time was pressed, never the request or the booked call that press made, and the form clears as soon as the press goes through. So no red line flashes after a request that went through, on either side, for Once, Daily or Weekly.
  - The typed rows, length and ticks are kept across a reload until they're sent (kept drafts, Every page).

  [image: Request a time while fbbb's request for the same time waits: a blue "fbbb asked for exactly this: Request a time books it." under the row; another row red with "You have a call with Ana Silva then (1:30–2:00 PM)."]
- **Accept / Decline / Withdraw** a waiting request, only in the Call requests panel (a Needs you row opens it). Accepting books it; the other side is told.
- **Booked calls:** timer, who is in the call room, Add to calendar, Join call (10 minutes before the start to 30 minutes after the booked end; it opens the call room: Call page), Cancel ▾ (reason required under 24 hours), "Cancel the remaining calls" for a set.
- **After a call:** **Report a problem** (No-show, Late, a note), once, after the call's start. Admins see reports in their daily email.

**Invite testers**

- Rows must be ticked (typing never invites). Missing needs show as orange labels: 3 named, then "+N more", which opens to show the rest ("fewer" folds it again); testers who can't join (suspended, or in 10 tests) are greyed with "Can't join this test".
- **A searched person who can't be invited** shows as a greyed row with why, and no tick box: "fbbb · already invited, waiting for them to join" (it brings their row in the Invited list into view, marked, where Cancel invite is), "fbbb · already in this test", "fbbb · applied: answer them in Needs you →" or "fbbb · asked to rejoin: …" (each jumps to the application), or "Can't join this test". Blocked people (either way) and deleted accounts are never shown, so "No testers match" stays for them. Applicants are greyed in People you've worked with too, and never suggested.
- **Invite is disabled while nobody can be invited.** While every spot is taken, the Invite button is greyed with the line under it "This test is full. Add spots in Edit to invite more people." ("in Edit" opens Edit at the spots field). On a paused or stopped test, or once joining has closed, it's greyed with that state's own line ("Joining closed on <date> (Open until), so nobody can be invited.").
