Ticking still works, so a selection isn't lost, and Cancel invite on the Invited list still works. It follows live both ways, with its own check every few seconds (and on each poll), never waiting for the page's own refresh: a spot opening (someone leaves, spots are raised) enables it, filling disables it with its line, and Open until passing while the page is open disables it at that moment.

  [image: Missing needs on an invite row: 3 named, then "+2 more".]
- Order: a tester **meets** the test with one required device and one required language (where any are required). **Score** = (3 × platform average + their stars) ÷ (3 + their ratings), so new testers start near average. **Invitable** = not in the test, not invited, under 10 tests, not paused; blocked or deleted testers never appear.
  - People you've worked with: invitable first, then your rating of them, tests together, most recent.
  - Suggested: the top 5 invitable testers new to you, filtered by the ticks, by meets, score, accepted parts, matches.
  - Find testers: invitable first, then meets and score.
  - Every list is worked out on the server over all testers, with no silent cap. **Search by name** (from 1 character, after a short pause in typing) finds every tester, invitable or greyed (with why, above), whose name matches (Searching by name, Every page), the exact name first, in a scroll box about 8 rows tall; above 100 matches it shows the first 100 with "Showing 100 of N: type more of the name". The device and language ticks narrow it too.
- **Invite:** the tester gets "<dev> invited you to <test>" and a daily reminder until they answer. A ticked tester who misses a need opens the inline Invite anyway box (Every page). If the tester had applied or asked to rejoin, they join at once.
- **The Invite anyway box** has one line per ticked tester naming everything they're missing, both kinds: "fbbb doesn't list a language this test needs (Chinese or Portuguese) or a device it needs (Windows PC, Switch, VR headset +5 more)." With only one kind missing, only that part ("…doesn't list a device this test needs (Windows PC or iPad)."). A list longer than 3 shows its first 3, then "+N more", which opens the rest in place. With more than 3 testers, their lines sit in a scroll box about 3 lines tall, with Invite anyway and Cancel below it. The server checks the box against the same full lines, so a box gone stale is still caught.
- **Cancel invite** on the Invited list. "Left this test" or "You ended their work" show beside those names.
- An invite stays open while the test is full and is sent again when an Edit changes the listing; it closes when the test or the developer can't take testers (any pause, hold or suspension, a stop), at Open until, when the developer cancels it, after a block either way, or when the tester is deleted. After a pause or hold the developer can invite again; nothing reopens by itself. What the tester sees: Tester dashboard.

**Access keys**

- **Dismiss selected** in the keys warning: hides the ticked rows for this developer (stored per report, so a new report shows again). For a row with "Works now" ticked it also clears the key's broken mark: the key is no longer crossed out in Given, in Edit's In use or in the tester row, and it can be reused later without the "reported as broken" warning. That tester's key notice changes from "Marked as not working" to "The developer marked your key as working. If it still fails, you can report it again." It's a change to their contract: their next press from an older page stops once for it, even with Alerts Off, and there's no bell. A row without "Works now" is only hidden; the key stays marked broken.

### What can stop a press

- **Stop-once** on every button (Next round and Go to the next round, Pay, Pay more, Confirm with your bank, the call buttons, Invite, Accept and Decline, Stop and End early), for any change the page hasn't shown yet, listed under the button, and for every change the developer made themselves in another tab or device ("You … in another tab": Why a press stops). Rate stops only for its own short list (Why a press stops: Rate). Every kind of change, its Alerts type and the notice text are in Why a press stops. The same holds on a stopped, closed or paused test and while the developer is held or suspended: every press still allowed stops once for what changed, and a page opened before the stop or pause stops once for it ("You stopped this test at 3:10 PM.", "The team paused this test at 3:10 PM."); the second press goes ahead (Stopped tests on Every page). "Send your side" and excerpt Allow and Deny never stop.

  [video: Ben (right, phone) sends their answers while the developer presses Pay for Ana; Pay stops once and lists the change, the second press goes ahead.]

  [image: Pay stops once: Ben sent their answers since the page loaded.]

  [image: Cam left while the Stop pop-up was open: the press didn't go through and the change shows under Stop.]

  [image: Invite stops once: Fay removed iPhone and iPad from their profile after the page loaded.]
- **Invite** also stops for: "<name> joined this test…", "<name> can't be invited right now", "<name> can't join more tests right now", and, whatever is ticked in Alerts, for these changes since the page loaded:
  - the test's required devices or languages changed (Edit in any tab): "The test's requirements changed: Devices: Windows PC → iPad · Languages: Russian, Turkish → English";
  - a ticked tester's profile changed in a way that matters to this test: only the languages and devices this test requires count, so adding or removing anything else (French, PlayStation or Xbox when the test doesn't need them) never stops Invite and isn't listed. It's one line per tester, the net change since the page loaded, naming only the required items, with one time (the latest change): "fbbb now lists Chinese and Windows PC, which this test needs." / "fbbb no longer lists Chinese, which this test needs." / "fbbb now lists Windows PC but no longer lists Chinese (this test needs Chinese or Portuguese)." Edits that cancel out (added, then removed again) give no line and no stop. For a ticked tester the panel hadn't shown yet, it says only when they no longer meet a need: "Fay Ito no longer lists a device this test needs (iPhone or iPad)."

  The box ends "Press Invite again to go ahead.", and the panel shows the updated tags at the same time. On the second press, a ticked tester missing a current need gets Invite anyway with the current needs; Invite anyway stops once more if the needs it shows are no longer current.
- **Always refused** (blue, under the button, naming the press: "Your Pay didn't go through: …"):
  - "This test was stopped."; "The team stopped this test."; "This test was already stopped." (the developer and an admin stopped it at the same moment: the first wins).
  - "<name> already left the test."; "<name>'s work already ended."
  - "<name> already charged $X for this."; "<name> let this go: nothing was charged."; "You already paid $X for this."; "Pay closed on <date>."; "Pay more closed on <date>."
  - Rate: "Rating opens once the team decides <name>'s dispute." (the box is disabled with it); "You can rate <name> once they've rated you and everything they sent is settled."; "You can rate once your account is active again."; "The 14 days to rate <name> have passed."
  - Stop: "Say why you're stopping the test (testers read it)."; Stop and End early: "Say a little more: at least 5 characters."
  - From a page opened before Invite was disabled (above): "<test> is full. Add spots to invite more people."; "Joining closed on <date> (Open until), so nobody can be invited."
  - Call lines: "This time isn't available."; a request that already closed, with why: "This request closed: the tester finished their part." (the tester rated, ending their part) or "This request closed: the tester left." (Leave, or their tester side suspended); the clashes above, checked again on the press; "You already have 3 requests waiting with <name>…"; "<name> already has 3 booked times with you…"; the $1,000 limit for a tester's calls, and the length it allows ("At most 95 minutes: …").
- **On a paused test** (any cause), or while the developer is held or suspended, only Stop the test, End early and Cancel call work (test page and the dashboard's Calls box), plus excerpt Allow and Deny, the side of a dispute and Confirm with your bank; on a test the team paused while nobody is held or suspended, Rate works too. They follow the usual stop-once (Rate keeps its own short list; excerpts and the side of a dispute never stop), and a page opened before the pause stops once for it (Stopped tests on Every page). Any other press from an older page (an Edit Save, Pay, Invite, Next round…) is refused; Go to the next round only opens the draft, so it's never refused for a pause, a hold or a suspension (it stops once like the other presses). For a team pause the developer is taken to the test page's red banner, which lists it: "Your Save on <test> didn't go through: the team paused this test." For a hold or suspension they go to the dashboard top.
- **Subscriber perks:** on the free plan, Invite and Next round stay visible with a small "Subscribers" tag; a press is refused with "Inviting testers needs a subscription ($7/month)." or "Next round needs a subscription ($7/month)." and a Payments link (new tab). Invites already sent stay valid when a subscription ends. A page open at that moment gets "Your subscription ended." as a stop-once change.

### How it looks in each state

- **Draft:** not a test page. Its card, its link and its Edit page open Post a test, filled in with the draft's saved details.
- **Open:** as above.
- **Joining closed** (after Open until): as above, with "Joining closed · testers working until <latest deadline>" in the overview. Invite is disabled with its line; working testers carry on until their own deadlines.
- **Paused (any cause: the team, a payment or chargeback hold, or a developer-side suspension):** the paused view. It shows the red banner, the overview with only Stop the test and End early, a compact Booked calls list (time, tester, length) with Cancel on each (a call that has started reads "In progress", or "Waiting to connect" while the two browsers haven't connected), the test's excerpt requests (Allow, Deny), the disputes with Add your side, "Your bank wants you to confirm" for charges made while away, "Rate your testers" (each tester's Rate box, only on a team pause with nobody held or suspended), and Alerts limited to what can still happen. A call in progress ended when the pause began (Call page). Hidden live: Needs you, Testers, the Live session tools, Invite, Edit, Next round, chat links and the answers. During a hold or suspension the developer reaches it from the dashboard card; Edit, chat and every other test page still go to the dashboard top. Testers follow the paused-test rules (Rules); on a team pause the chat stays open both ways.

  [video: The team pauses the test while it's open: on the test page the banner appears and only Stop, End early and Cancel stay; on Edit, Save is refused and lands on the test page banner with its line; after Ana leaves, Stop stops once with "Ana left the test", and the second press stops the test.]

  [image: Save on a team-paused test is refused and routed to the test page: under the team's reason, the banner lists "Your Save on Harbor Run: lighthouse didn't go through."]

  [image: Ana left after the page loaded: Stop on the team-paused test stops once with the change, as on a running test.]
- **Stopped:** the page shows only what still works. Gone: Invite testers, the call-request tools in Live session, Edit, Stop and End early. Left: the Stopped label and the red banner "This test is stopped." with what still works, Next round (it starts a new draft from this test), Needs you (Pay, a call's Pay, a dispute's side), the Testers panel (rows, the "Calls (N)" folds, Rate, Pay more until it closes), Contract changes, chat, Alerts (limited) and the answers. What's left follows the usual stop-once (What can stop a press, above). At the close-out, Pay, Pay more and the testers' Rate close (Rules: Close-out).

  [image: Stopped: the Stopped label and the stopped line; only what still works: Needs you, the Testers panel and past calls; Next round stays.]
- **Stopped by the team:** the page moves to the top of the overview, where the banner "The team stopped this test." stays, with the reason, and names any press that didn't go through. Then the stopped rules apply, except that Next round isn't offered.

  [video: The team stops the test while a call is being requested; the request is refused and the banner names the press that didn't go through.]
- **Closed after a pause:** like stopped, with the reason in the banner.

### Bell lines that open this page

- A tester joined, rejoined or left → that tester's row.
- An application, rejoin ask or "N applications wait for your answer" → Needs you.
- "<tester> asked for a call: …" → the Live session's Call requests panel, with that request highlighted (as from its Needs you row).
- Work sent or updated, "review … within…", "pay <tester>'s call on … within 3 days" / "…by tomorrow", "…wasn't reviewed by <date>: pay it.", "Pay your calls" (one test) → that piece or call in Needs you.
- A dispute filed → its Needs you row; a dispute decided → the tester's row.
- (The daily "Pay now", "Closing soon" and bank-confirmation lines, and the rating lines, open the developer dashboard: Developer dashboard.)
- "Your call with <tester> … starts at <time>." → that call in Live session.
- A call line about a call ahead → that call in Live session; about a call that's over (cancelled, nobody joined, paused, outage, skipped) → that call in the tester's "Calls (N)" fold, opened and marked.
- The team paused, stopped or closed the test, lifted the pause, or "will close later" → the overview banner.
- "“<test>” closes to new testers on <date>." and chat lines → the test page.

What testers see of all this is in Tester session page and Tester dashboard, Find tests and the join page; profiles and chat are in Shared pages.

## Edit test

### What it's for and who sees it

The developer changes a test's listing. Edits apply to new testers; working testers get an offer to Switch or Keep. The fields and red checks are the same as on Post a test. Only the test's developer sees it.

From Every page: the live box, stop-once, red field checks, kept drafts, long lists, and the blue box when suspended or on hold.

### What's on it

- "← Back to the test", the title "Edit “<title>”", the Alerts menu (Developer test page), and a note that Edit changes the listing for new testers only.
- The Post form, filled in.
- **Access keys** ("each joining tester gets one") in two boxes:
  - **In use** (read only): keys given to working testers, each with its tester; a key reported as not working is crossed out ("· not working"). Below them, greyed, the keys of testers who left or finished, with "(left)" or "(finished)" after the name, so the developer can see which ones they could reuse. It follows changes live.
  - **Free:** one plain text box holding every free key, one per line. The heading counts live as they type: "Free (3 keys · 3 open spots)". The developer deletes a line to remove that key and types a new line to add one. Red under the Free box, and only there, when there are fewer keys than open spots: "Add 1 more key: 3 open spots, 2 free keys."; working testers without a key count too: "Add 2 more keys: 3 working testers and 4 open spots need 7, you have 5." (the server refuses the same). Free keys go first to working testers without one, at Save (Buttons, below), then to new testers.
  - The line under the boxes: "Keys in use can't be added. Keys from testers who left or finished can be typed in again; you'll see if one was reported as broken."

  [image: One Free box: every free key one per line ("Free (2 keys · 2 open spots)"); In use lists Ben's key and, greyed, Ana's and Cam's "(left)".]
- **"Changes left: N of 3"** next to the label of each limited field (the steps, questions, languages, devices, build link, and parts and prices; never the NDA), once anyone is affected (the first join, application or invite).
- **Save**, visibly disabled (greyed, the app's disabled button style) with the tooltip "Nothing has changed yet" while nothing changed; it lights up as soon as anything changes. Kept typed changes read "Not saved yet" next to it (kept drafts, Every page).
- **The Charge tick** (the same words and link as on Post a test) appears just above Save only while Save is enabled, that is once the form has a change; it never shows on an unchanged form. It's required for that Save, and it unticks whenever the form is back to unchanged, so after each Save. Unticked, the server refuses the Save with the same red line at the tick: "Tick to confirm you understand how payments work."
- **The live box** sits under Save. When that spot is below the screen, an update raises the pill "1 new update ↓", and pressing it scrolls down to it (Every page).

  [image: Edit's live box sits under Save: Ana sent their written answers while Edit was open.]

### Buttons

- **Save** changes the listing for new testers. Elsewhere:
  - **Working testers whose own terms change** (parts, prices, Required labels, the live rate and call length, questions, the NDA, the deadline) get an offer at the top of their session page, Switch or Keep (Tester session page), and the bell line "<dev> updated the listing: <changes>". A tester whose terms don't change gets no offer. Work already sent keeps its price. A newer Save replaces a waiting offer.
  - **A changed NDA** is always a new offer, never applied silently.
  - **Earlier questions, answers and NDAs** stay in the tester's Terms history.
  - **Waiting applications** close when prices, parts, questions, devices, languages, the description, the build link or the NDA change, or when the test becomes Invite only. The tester is told ("<test>'s listing changed, so your application was closed. You can apply again on the current listing.", stacked across tests as "3 tests you applied to changed: your applications were closed" / "<test> is now invite only, so your application was closed.").
  - **Open invites** are sent again on the new listing ("<dev> updated <test>: your invite was sent again on the new listing.", stacked across tests the same way).
  - **Listing information** (title, product, description, build link, devices, languages, categories, who can join, Mature) changes at once for working testers, outside their contract: their session page shows the current values (Tester session page), and the Save is one line for them, "<dev> edited the listing at 6:55 PM: …" (Why a press stops: Tester).
  - **Keys for working testers:** on a test that uses keys, a Save gives each still-working tester without a key (the test had none when they joined) one of the free keys, oldest joiner first; testers whose work ended get none. The tester gets the key box live, a top-box line and a bell line, "<dev> gave you an access key for “<test>”" (never the key itself).
  - **Switching to Anyone can join** lets waiting applicants in ("You're in: <test>") or tells them "<test> changed since you applied".
  - **More spots** tells invited testers "A spot opened on <test>…"; filling the test closes applications and keeps invites open.
  - **Renames** (the product or the test name) are info for everyone and never stop a press. Each one is recorded in every working tester's contract changes, "Renamed: "<old product> · <old test>" → "<new product> · <new test>" · <date, time>", including a rename saved while a tester's press was in flight. It's also an entry in the developer's Contract changes panel.

    [image: A rename in the tester row's contract changes: "Renamed: "Harbor Run · Harbor Run: first 3 levels" → "Harbor Run · Harbor Run: night harbors"".]
  - After Save, the developer lands on the test page with the green line "Saved. Offered the new terms to N working testers." (or "Saved. New testers join on the new listing; testers already in keep their contract."), plus the applications it closed and the invites it re-sent (Developer test page). When a tester later Switches, the top box shows one quiet line, "Ana switched to the new terms" with "See their contract", which opens the test page at that tester's row in a new tab; several in a day stack into "3 testers switched to the new terms". A Keep tells the developer nothing. Neither stops a press, because work already sent keeps its price.
- **3 changes per field, counted once anyone is affected.** The count starts when the first tester joins, the first application arrives or the first invite is sent, whichever comes first, and keeps counting even if that applicant later withdraws. Before that, edits are free and nobody is told. From then on each of these 6 fields has its own 3 changes: what to do (the steps), questions, required languages, required devices and technology, the build link, and parts and prices (one counter for the part ticks, prices, Required and Free ticks, the live rate and call length). The NDA isn't limited: it can change any number of times, and testers accept a changed NDA again. One Save that changes two of them uses one change of each. Each field shows "Changes left: N of 3" next to its label, and the counts update live when another tab saves. A field at 0 locks on its own, with the note "You've changed the <field> 3 times since testers joined. Contact us in Settings to change it again."; every input in it is disabled (for parts and prices: the ticks and the price, rate and call-length boxes, the over-$200 tick too), and the other fields stay editable. If a locked field still holds a change here (another tab's Save locked it meanwhile), the line at Save names it, "Your change to the live call rate ($24.00/h → $30.00/h) can't be saved: you've changed the parts and prices 3 times since testers joined.", with **Put it back** beside it (restores the saved value; the other changes then save); the field's name scrolls to the field, marked. Changing parts or prices still sends working testers the Switch / Keep offer. Access keys aren't limited: a key given to a tester stays theirs, so adding keys never changes anyone's terms. An admin's "Allow 3 more" gives every field at 0 three more changes (Administrator console).

  [image: After 3 saves that changed the steps, the steps field locks with its note; every other limited field shows its own “Changes left: N of 3”.]
- **Dates.**
  - Open until (last day to join) is required and can only move later, before it passes, and never more than 30 days after the test opened (it was posted, or its current Next round opened), so tester days never pass 30. The hint next to it: "only later, up to <cap date> (30 days after the test opened)". An earlier date: "Open until can only be extended: it's <date> now." A later date past the cap, red at the field and from the server: "Open until can be at most 30 days after the test opened (<cap date>)." Once passed: "Joining closed on <date> (Open until), so it can't be extended." A test already past the cap keeps its date; it just can't move later. Pause and outage shifts still move it later, past the cap too (Rules).
  - Moving it later by some days gives every tester that many more days: new testers get the longer time, and every working tester's deadline moves the same number of days later. The line under the date says, live, the Post line with the real days per tester after the change (the days now plus the extension): "Testers can join until this date. Each gets 19 days from when they join, so someone joining on Nov 25 has until Dec 14 to send work. You pay each piece within 7 days of it arriving or by that tester's deadline, whichever is later, or the tester can charge you."

    [image: Open until can only move later: an earlier date is red.]

    [image: A date past the cap is red: "Open until can be at most 30 days after the test opened (Dec 6)."; the line under it counts the days per tester after the change.]
  - There's no separate deadline field: each tester's deadline is the day they joined plus the tester days.
- **Access keys.** Save works out the change against the keys this page loaded, not the current ones, so another tab's work is never undone: removed = loaded keys whose line is gone; added = lines that weren't loaded. Keys another tab added are left alone, and so are keys a joining tester was given whose line is still there.
  - **Refused, red under the box, one line each:** a key in use by a working tester ("HR-1111 is in use by Ana Silva, so it can't be added."), broken or not; a key typed twice ("<key> is typed more than once. Each key goes in once."). After a red line, the box shows the current free keys with the developer's other edits still applied, and pressing Save again saves the rest, unless something new happened in between.
  - **Typed keys another tab already added** merge: the Free box shows each key once, with no red, and Save isn't refused.
  - **Keys another tab deleted** leave this tab's Free box by themselves and never come back by themselves (a deleted free key may have been sent to a tester in a message); to add one again, the developer types it again.
  - **A key you deleted here (not saved yet) that a joining tester was given** is not a mistake and never red: as soon as the page sees it, a blue line in the keys note, under "Updated at <time>:", says "Ben Okafor joined and was given K-2, which you had deleted here." and K-2 leaves this tab's deletions (it's in use now). Several stack: "Ben Okafor and Cam Lee joined and were given K-2 and K-3, which you had deleted here." If the page hadn't shown it yet, Save stops once with the same line; the second press saves the rest of this tab's key edits.
  - **Allowed, with a warning:** a key from a tester who left, was removed, or finished goes back to Free when typed in. It shows an amber line with a "Reused" tag: "HR-3333: reused, previously given to Ben Okafor.", or, for a key that tester last marked as not working, "HR-2222: reported as broken by Ben Okafor." It's a warning only: it doesn't count as a mistake, isn't in "Fix the N mistakes" and doesn't block Save. Nothing returns to Free by itself.
  - Every key a refusal calls "in use" is a working tester's and is listed under In use.
  - Testers whose key doesn't work never get a new one automatically: the developer deletes a free key here and sends it to them in a message; the Access keys notice on Developer test page links here. The developer can also mark the key as working there.

  [image: A working tester's key can't be added (red, as you type): "HR-2222 is in use by Ben Okafor, so it can't be added."]

  [image: Typed back with a warning: "HR-1111: reused, previously given to Ana Silva." and "HR-3333: reported as broken by Cam Lee.", with a Reused tag, never a mistake.]

### What can stop a press

- **Saved in another tab or device** (on Edit and on posting a draft). Edit keeps what you typed; it shows what the other tab saved:
  - **A field you haven't touched** takes the saved value at once, silently: no note and no stop, so your Save never undoes it.
  - **A field you typed in keeps your text.** Under it, a blue note shows what the other tab saved: "Saved in another tab or device at 3:54 PM:" and their value. A short value sits on the same line as the heading (a field name and its value, e.g. "Name of this test: Night harbors"); long text (the steps, questions, NDA) folds after 3 lines with "Show more". Each value has a small **Use theirs** button that replaces your text with theirs and removes the note. Where one note covers several fields (the parts and prices, the questions), each is listed with its own Use theirs. A later save elsewhere updates the value shown, and making your text match theirs removes the note.
  - **On Save,** each typed field whose text still differs from what's saved now stops once, one line per field in the blue box: "Instructions: your version will replace the one saved in another tab at 3:54 PM." The box ends "Press Save again to save your changes (fields you didn't touch keep the newer values)."; the second press saves yours on top. A field you didn't touch never stops Save.
  - **The same value is not news:** when another tab saves exactly what this tab already has in that field (typed or untouched), there's no note and no stop for it; if nothing else differs, Save goes straight through.
  - **Tester spots and Open until** keep the older rule: they take the other tab's value even if you typed in them. Their note says what it replaced, before → after on one line ("Changed in another tab or device at 7:16 AM: Spots: 4 → 5"), and, if you had typed something different, adds **"What you typed (not saved):"** with your text and a **Copy** button. Save stops once for them with "You changed the spots in another tab" (or "…the Open until…").
  - **Access keys are unchanged and always shown**, even a change this tab also made, because they decide which keys can still be added: the other tab's adds and removes are applied to the Free box with your own edits kept on top. The keys note has two headings: "Changed in another tab or device at <time>:" over your own saves elsewhere, one line per kind, keys comma-separated ("Added: HR-8888, HR-9999, HR-7777" · "Removed: HR-5555"), and "Updated at <time>:" over what other people caused, a key given to a joining tester ("Ana Silva joined and was given HR-4444", never under Removed; "Ben Okafor joined and was given K-2, which you had deleted here."). A note with both shows both. Long lists follow the blue-box rule (Long lists on Every page).

  [video: Two tabs on Edit; the right one saves new instructions and keys; the left one keeps what was typed, with "Saved in another tab or device at …:" and Use theirs under the instructions, and its Save stops once.]

  [image: Another tab saved new instructions while this tab had typed its own: the text stays, and the blue note under it shows "Saved in another tab or device at 3:54 PM:", their text folded after 3 lines, and Use theirs.]

  [image: An untouched field took the other tab's value with no note; spots still show "Spots: 4 → 5" with What you typed (not saved) and Copy.]

  [image: The keys note: "Changed in another tab or device at …:" with "Added: …" · "Removed: …", and "Updated at …:" with "Ben Okafor joined and was given K-2, which you had deleted here.", never red.]

  [image: Save stops once: "Instructions: your version will replace the one saved in another tab at 3:54 PM."; press Save again to save yours on top.]
- **Other changes** (joins, work sent, calls and so on, as set in Alerts) stop Save once, listed under it. Every kind, with its notice text, is in Why a press stops.
- **Your own other changes in another tab** stop Save once too, whatever Alerts says, as on the test page: a Pay or Pay more, Confirm with your bank, End early, Stop, Next round, a call change, an invite, Accept or Decline ("You paid Ana Silva $10.00 for the written answers in another tab", "You started the next round in another tab"). They also show in Edit's live box at once. Another tab's Save keeps its per-field notes and lines above.

  [image: Ana sent written answers and Ben joined while Edit was open: Save stops once with the blue list, and the key boxes and open spots are already up to date (K-2 is now Ben's).]
- **A field can be red and blue at once**, e.g. fewer access keys than open spots after another tab added spots.

  [image: You deleted a key's line; another tab added a spot (blue): now fewer free keys than open spots (red).]

  [image: You deleted one key's line; another tab removed another (blue): the free keys no longer cover the open spots (red).]
- **Always refused:** "This test was stopped." If an admin stops or pauses the test while it's being edited, Save is refused and the developer goes back to the test page, whose banner names the press that didn't go through ("Your Save on <test> didn't go through: the team paused this test.").

  [video: The team stops the test while Edit is open; Save is refused and the test page's banner names the Save that didn't go through.]
- **Subscriber perk:** on the free plan, Approved only under Who can join shows a small "Subscribers" tag, and a Save that switches to it is refused: "Approved-only testers need a subscription ($7/month)." with a Payments link (new tab). A test that is already Approved only stays so when a subscription ends, and its applications can still be accepted or declined.

### How it looks in each state

- **Open:** the form. **Draft:** a draft's Edit page opens Post a test, filled in with its details.
- **Paused by the team:** Save is refused, and the developer is taken to the test page's paused view, whose banner lists "Your Save on <test> didn't go through: the team paused this test."
- **Stopped or closed:** red "This test was stopped." and no form.
- **Payment hold, chargeback hold or developer side suspended:** the page is closed; the developer moves to the dashboard top.

### Bell lines that open this page

None; bell lines about a test open the test page.

New-tester side: what changed shows on the join page and in Find tests (Tester dashboard, Find tests and the join page).

## Tester dashboard

### What it's for and who sees it

The tester's home: what to do next, money waiting, calls, invites and new tests. Only the tester sees it.

From Every page: the live box, long lists (each list is a plain scroll box), number format, and the blue box when their tester side is suspended.

### What's on it (top to bottom)

- **Payout problem banner**, when a payout failed: "Your bank couldn't receive a payout. Update your payout details." (Shared pages).
- **Stat tiles:** Earned, Waiting for review, Rating, To do.
- **Tests you were removed from** (only after a suspension, see below).
- **Search by developer**, at the top of the tests (full width on phones): typing 1 or more characters narrows the lists below to every test whose developer's name matches it (Searching by name, Every page), across every test the tester has been in: working, finished, left, ended, stopped or closed, blocked developers' past tests included. Results, an exact name first, then most recently joined first, show in one scroll box. No match: "No tests with a developer named “<typed>”." A deleted developer matches "Deleted account". Clearing the box brings the dashboard back.

  [image: Search by developer: "nora" finds three tests with Nora Lane, an old ended one included.]
- **Ratings to give:** every rating the tester can give right now, newest first: "Rate Nora Lane on “Harbor Run”". A row appears once everything they sent on that test is settled and no dispute of theirs is open (Rules: Ratings), goes live when Rate stops being available and comes back when it is again. It opens the session page's Rate box, highlighted for a few seconds. Hidden while their tester side is suspended.
- **Charge or cancel:** tests with parts where Charge is open (written answers and recordings; a listed call is charged automatically instead), e.g. "Harbor Run · 2 parts". Each row opens the part on the session page. When Charge opens, and when it closes, is in Rules; a piece whose Charge closed leaves this box and Waiting for the developer.
- **To do:** unsent parts with deadlines, each with "Send work", which opens the part; also new terms to Switch or Keep, and the developer's call requests ("<dev> asked for a live call", with "Answer in Live session →", which opens the session page's Call requests panel at that request). A test can be in both Charge or cancel and To do.
- **Waiting for the developer:** sent work waiting for review ("Written answers sent: waiting for review", "Screen recording sent: …"), and applications or rejoin asks waiting. A sent part that's listed in their contract also says when Charge opens: "If Nora Lane hasn't paid by Nov 5, you can charge or let it go then." (its review date, the later of their own deadline and 7 days after sending, in their own time zone, the same day the developer reads; it moves with pauses like the date itself). An unlisted part never opens Charge, so it has no such line; listed work sent late reads instead "Sent late (deadline was <date>): Nora Lane decides what to pay. You can't charge for late work."
- **Upcoming calls:** the next 7 days, each with Join call while the call can be joined (the same rule as the session page) and "Check your call setup" (Call page).

  [image: The tester dashboard's Upcoming calls, with Join while the call can be joined.]
- **Invitations:** Accept (opens the join page) and No thanks. They follow the developer's side live:
  - A new invite appears in place, with a top-box line, "Marc Hanna invited you to “ddddd”" (a re-sent one: "Marc Hanna updated “ddddd”: your invite was sent again on the new listing."), raising the pill.
  - A closed invite's card disappears, with a neutral line in the live box: "Your invite to “Harbor Run” was closed." Why it closed is never said there.
  - An invite sent again on a new listing updates its card in place: "Sent again on the new listing · <date>".
  - On a full test the card shows "Full right now: your invite stays open".
  - When invites close and stay open: Developer test page (Invite testers).
- **Tests you left:** tests they left or were removed from, before the deadline, not stopped and not rated, with Ask to rejoin.
- **New tests for you.**
- **Tests visited:** every test whose join page the tester opened but never joined, most recent visit first. Each card shows the test, the developer and its state now, updated live, and opens the join page, which shows the matching state and refusal: "Accepting testers", "Full", "Closed to new testers on <date>", "Paused", "Stopped" or "Closed", "Applied (waiting)", "Invited" (it opens the invitation). An invite-only test they aren't invited to reads "Invite only", and its page shows only "This test isn't available right now.", with nothing of the listing. Tests of a developer blocked either way are left out. Each card has a small "Remove" (×) that drops it at once, with no confirm; opening the join page again brings it back, and joining takes it off. Hidden while their tester side is suspended.
- **History:** past tests and how each ended. Each ended test's entry holds the tester's own deal history, in order: the terms they joined on, every offer with what changed and what they chose, renames, their previous question sets, calls that are over (with their outcome badges) and payments. It never shows other testers' choices.
- Paused tests the tester joined or applied to show with a "Paused" tag. Their applications wait, and work again after the lift unless something else closed them; an invite to a paused test closes (Invitations, above).
- A test they applied to or visited that became invite only stays here, marked "Closed to new testers (invite only)". An open invite of theirs still works.

### Buttons

- **A row** in any box: opens that part, call, offer or test.
- **Send work:** opens the part on Tester session page.
- **Accept** (invite): opens the join page. **No thanks:** closes the invite; the developer's page shows "<name> said no thanks to your invite".
- **Ask to rejoin:** as on the join page, below.
- **Ask to rejoin** on a "You were removed from this test" row, while the test is open, has a spot and isn't paused; **Dismiss** removes the row.

### What can stop a press

- No thanks follows the same rules as Join and Accept, here and on the join page:
  - On an invite the developer sent again on a new listing meanwhile, it stops once (blue) with "<dev> updated “<test>”: your invite was sent again on the new listing" and "The new listing: …"; the second press says no thanks to the new invite.
  - A test that filled up keeps the invite open, so No thanks just declines it.
- No thanks, Join or Accept on an invite that closed meanwhile is refused, with no stop box, and its card disappears. The reason never says "hold" or "suspended":
  - a pause, a hold or the developer's suspension: "“<test>” isn't taking new testers right now, so this invite was closed.";
  - Open until passed: "“<test>” closed to new testers on <date>.";
  - the test stopped, or the developer deleted their account: "This test was stopped.";
  - cancelled, a block, or anything else: "This invite is no longer open."
- Join call is refused if the call was cancelled, moved or closed since the page loaded: "Your Join call didn't go through: <name> cancelled this call at <time>." The row then leaves the list.
- Join call is also refused while that test has an open offer of new terms: "Answer the new terms first: Switch or Keep." under the button, on every press until the tester answers it on the session page (Why a press stops: New terms come first). Under that line a link, "Answer them on the session page →", opens the session page at the Switch / Keep box, highlighted.

### How it looks in each state

- **Active:** as above.
- **Tester side suspended:** the blue box at the top says their account (on a mixed account, their tester account) is paused, they were removed from their tests, how to appeal (Contact us in Settings), and that pay for work already accepted still reaches their Stripe account. Every other signed-in page moves them here, Find tests and profiles included, except Payments (with payout setup), Settings and the bell list. Below the box: their excerpts ("Your excerpts", each with Remove), and "Tests you were removed from", where each row opens that test's limited view (Tester session page). Names in rows are plain text with no hover card. Invitations, New tests, Ratings to give and Tests visited are hidden; To do says "Nothing to do while your account is suspended."
- **After reinstatement:** no automatic re-invite. The tests they were removed from stay at the top, labelled "You were removed from this test", with Ask to rejoin while the test is open, has a spot and isn't paused, until they ask or dismiss. The developer can also invite them again as usual.
- **Deleted:** every tab logs out.

### Bell lines that open this page

- "Your tester account is active again", outage lines → the top.
- "Charge or let go: …" (the one daily line) → Charge or cancel.
- "Rate <dev> on <test>" and "Reminder: rate <dev> on “<test>”" → Ratings to give.

## Find tests

### What it's for and who sees it

Testers find tests to join. It lists open public tests (Anyone can join, Approved only) until their Open until. Invite-only tests are never listed, not even for testers who were invited (they reach the test from their dashboard's invites, the bell or the email). Paused, full, stopped, closed and deleted tests, and tests whose joining has closed, are not listed either. Admins get the page too, listing every test (below).

From Every page: the live box, long lists.

### What's on it

- A title and intro, then a **"Search tests or developers"** box at the top: test name, product name or developer name, matched by the name-search rule (Searching by name, Every page) with an exact match first, searched on the server over every open test, together with the category chips and the filters (Device, Part, Sort by, Show). No match: "No open tests match “<typed>”." (The tester dashboard's "Search by developer" is separate.)
- **Cards:** product and title, categories, languages and devices needed, spots left, price per part, who can join, the time each tester gets and the last day to join ("14 days per tester · join by Nov 20"), Mature and NDA badges, "You left this test".

  [image: Find tests: "14 days per tester · join by <date>"; the invite-only test isn't listed, not even for invited testers.]
- Each card lists every unmet need in amber (3, then "+N more").
- **Order:** tests needing a device the tester has, then tests open to any device, then the rest; newest first within each.
- **Sort by "Most time to finish"** sorts by the days each new tester gets (the "N days per tester" on the card), most first; ties put the later Open until first, and tests without Open until go last.
- Tests of a developer the tester blocked, or who blocked them, are never listed (Public profiles: Block).

### Buttons

- **A card:** opens its join page.

### How it looks in each state

- **Active:** as above. A test that fills, pauses, stops or passes its Open until leaves the list; one that frees a spot comes back.
- **Tester side suspended:** the page is closed; it moves to the dashboard top.
- **Admins** (a "Find tests" link in the admin header): the same page and search box (which also finds a developer's email: with 1 or 2 characters, from its start), but listing every test, drafts included, newest first (the newest 500, with "Showing the newest 500 tests: search to narrow."). Each card carries its state: Open, Full, Joining closed, Paused, Stopped, Closed, Draft or Invite only, with tick boxes for those states above the list ("No tests match “<typed>”." / "No tests match these ticks"). A card shows "N of M spots open" and "Posted <date>", and opens the test's public page with the admin's boxes (timeline, contract changes, payments). Admins never get Join or Apply there.

## Join page

If another tab already made a waiting application, Apply refuses "You already applied to join this test in another tab." Ask to rejoin refuses "You already asked to rejoin this test in another tab." The note is retained, no duplicate notification is sent, and the waiting state with Withdraw redraws.

### What it's for and who sees it

The listing of one test, where a tester joins, applies, accepts an invite or asks to rejoin. Testers see it from Find tests, an invite or a link.

From Every page: the live box, stop-once, Join anyway, kept drafts, email confirmation.

### What's on it (top to bottom)

- "← All tests", the live box, the header card (product, price block, badges).
- An invitation card with No thanks, if invited.
- The steps, in a scroll box about 6 lines tall.
- "How it works", "<dev> wants to know" (the questions, each long one in its scroll box, a Required one followed by " · required"; more than 3 in a scroll box about 3 questions tall: Long lists, Every page; under them "Each answer is at least 50 characters."), "Your deal" (parts, prices, Required labels, and "Your deadline: Dec 3 (14 days from joining)": the deadline they'd have if they joined now), "How <dev> reviews" (the developer's stats).

  [image: The join page: "Your deadline: <date> (14 days from joining)"; a long question and long steps in their scroll boxes.]
- A state line when it can't be joined (below).
- **The join box:** the NDA to accept (in a scroll box about 12 lines tall), then one of Join (Anyone can join), Apply (Approved only), Accept invitation (invited) or Ask to rejoin (left or ended before their deadline). Right above the button, **"Changed since you opened this page"** lists the listing saves since the page loaded, one line each with its time ("Marc Hanna edited the listing at 6:55 PM: …"), newest first, 3 then "Show N more", following the page live; it clears when the tester presses and the stop box takes over. "You now meet this test's needs" stays its own notice.
- A tester's hover card on the developer's name has "Chat with <name>" (Shared pages).

### Buttons

- **Join / Accept invitation:** checks requirements, spots (two people never take the last spot), Open until (the last day to join), the NDA, a confirmed email, a free access key and the 10-test limit. Then the tester is in, on the current listing, with their own deadline (the day they join plus the test's tester days), and lands on Tester session page. Accepted invites and approved applications count from the moment they become a tester. The developer's bell: "<name> joined <test>". If that took the last spot, other applicants and invitees are told.
- **Apply:** sends an application; the developer's bell: "<name> applied to join <test>". If the test is now open to anyone, it joins at once. **Withdraw application** takes it back.
- **Ask to rejoin:** only before their deadline, never after a stop or rating. The developer accepts in Needs you; the tester comes back on the current listing with the exact time they had left. An invite plus an ask, in either order, joins at once.
- **Join anyway / Apply anyway / Ask to rejoin anyway:** when the profile lacks a device or language the test needs, an inline box "What this test needs" names everything missing, both kinds, in the Invite anyway box's words: "Your profile doesn't list a language this test needs (Chinese or Portuguese) or a device it needs (Windows PC or iPad). Join anyway?" (a list longer than 3: its first 3, then "+N more"), with Join anyway, "Edit my profile" (new tab) and Cancel. It checks again every few seconds ("Just updated") and closes when everything fits: "You now meet this test's needs. Press Join to join." That notice comes in addition to the save's other changes, which still stop the next press once. Join anyway opens the session page (the box stays meanwhile); Apply anyway and Ask to rejoin anyway keep the tester here, so the box closes once the press goes through and the page shows its done line: "Application sent. You'll hear back when the developer answers." / "Sent. You'll hear back when the developer answers."
- **No thanks:** closes the invite.
- **Join on the current listing / Withdraw application:** shown in "This test changed since you applied."
- The 10-test limit applies to Join, Apply, accepting an invite or application, and Ask to rejoin: "You can't join more tests right now."

### What can stop a press

- **Stop-once:** every listing save since the page last showed one, one line per save, any field: "Marc Hanna edited the listing at 6:55 PM: product name, title (now “lllllq”), description, build link, recording price, live call rate"; an NDA change alone reads "Marc Hanna changed the NDA at 6:58 PM: read and accept it again below" and clears the NDA tick; an application closed ("<test> is full: your application was closed"); a new invite on the updated listing; your own changes in another tab ("You applied to join in another tab", "You withdrew your application in another tab", "You said no thanks to the invite in another tab"), which follow Alerts like the session page's (Off: they don't stop; the same press made there is a plain refusal). Spots stop only when the test is now full, Open until only when it passes; a title change is info only. The box ends "Press again to join on the current listing." How stop-once works everywhere: Why a press stops.
- **Needs changed** while Join anyway is open: "<dev> added <X> to the devices this test needs" and "Your press didn't go through. Check what this test needs now, then press again."
- **Updated Terms of Use:** for an account that accepted an older version, the join box has one more required tick above the button, "I agree to the updated Terms of Use. Read what changed" (Terms of Use, Shared pages); unticked, the press is refused, red at the tick: "Tick to agree to the updated Terms of Use."
- **No thanks** stops and is refused as on Tester dashboard.
- **Always refused** (blue, under the button): "This test was stopped."; "This test closed to new testers on <date>."; "This test isn't taking new testers any more."; "This test is paused, so it isn't taking new testers right now."; "This test is full."; "You've already joined this test."; "You rated this test, so you can't join it again."; "You left this test, so you ask to rejoin and the developer accepts."; "This test takes applications: press Apply to join."; "This test is invite only."; "Accept the NDA to join."; "No access key is free right now, so this test can't take another tester until the developer adds one."; "You blocked <dev>: unblock them in Account → Blocked people to join."; "Confirm your email first. We sent a link to <email>." (with "Resend the link" under the button)

### How it looks in each state

- **Open:** the join box.
- **Joining closed:** "This test closed to new testers on <date>." Waiting applications and open invites closed at Open until.
- **Full:** "This test is full." Invited testers: "<test> is full right now. Your invite stays open; we'll tell you when a spot opens."
- **Paused:** testers who joined or applied (or were invited before it paused) see it with a "Paused" tag and "This test is paused right now."; their applications wait and work again after the lift unless something else closed them, and an open invite closed at the pause. Anyone else sees "This test isn't available right now."
- **Invite only, for a tester who isn't invited, applying or in it:** only "This test isn't available right now.", with nothing of the listing. A tester who was in it before keeps the page, with Ask to rejoin.
- **Opened from a developer profile's Open tests after the test left that list** (the profile page was older): only the reason, with "← Back to the profile": "This test isn't available right now." (invite only for them, paused, a block), "This test is full.", "This test closed to new testers on <date>." or "This test was stopped." (Public profiles).
- **Every visit** by a tester who isn't in the test is noted for their dashboard's Tests visited.
- **An application or ask to rejoin is followed live.** Accepted while the page is open: "You're back in “<test>”: your deadline is <date>." (a first application: "You're in: “<test>”.") with "Go to your session →", and the page moves there unless something is being typed. Declined: "Your ask to rejoin wasn't accepted." / "Your application to “<test>” wasn't accepted."; any other change (closed, withdrawn) redraws the page, and the "Sent." line goes.
- **Stopped or closed:** red "This test was stopped." or "This test isn't taking new testers any more."
- **Draft:** not found.
- **A block either way** (the tester blocked the developer, or the developer blocked them): a test of that developer the tester was never part of (no contract, invite or application from before the block) reads "This test isn't available right now." with nothing of the listing shown, as for a paused test. On a test they were part of, Ask to rejoin is refused with "This test isn't taking new testers right now.", and so are Join and Apply when the developer blocked them (when the tester blocked the developer, Join says "You blocked <dev>: …", below). The developer's block is never revealed.
- **The tester's own test:** "This is your own test…"
- **Tester side suspended:** the page is closed; it moves them to the dashboard top.

### Bell lines that open this page

- Invites and invite reminders, "…here's a new invite…", "A spot opened…", "…is full right now. Your invite stays open…" → the invitation card.
- "…your application was closed", "…is now invite only…", "<test> changed since you applied", "Your ask to rejoin … wasn't accepted" → the join box.

The developer's side of joining and invites is on Developer test page.

## Tester session page

### What it's for and who sees it

Everything a tester does inside one test they joined: read their terms, send work, run calls, get paid, charge, dispute, leave or rejoin, and rate. Only that tester sees it.

From Every page: the live box, stop-once, pop-ups, kept drafts, long lists, stopped tests. Testers see "paused" and "delayed", never hold, suspended, failed or chargeback.

### What's on it (top to bottom)

1. **Product and test name**, and at the top right **Alerts: On / Off** with its grey line (Why a press stops); hidden once nothing left on the page can stop.
2. **Access key box**, if the test has keys and their work goes on: the key, and "This key doesn't work". Once reported: "Marked as not working. The developer was told. They can send you another key in a message." If the developer later marks it as working: "The developer marked your key as working. If it still fails, you can report it again." On a test that had no keys when they joined, the box appears live once a Save gives them one (Edit test).

   [image: The tester reads "The developer marked your key as working." (their next older-page press stops once for it, even with Alerts Off).]
3. **Live box:** every change except new terms. With Alerts Off, lines that won't stop anything show greyed. A listing save is one line, "Marc Hanna edited the listing at 7:21 PM: description, build link, categories · See it"; See it scrolls to Your agreed scope and marks the changed lines for a few seconds.
4. **Switch / Keep offer**, when the developer offers new terms (also when it arrives while the page is open): "<dev> updated the listing: <changes>." A changed NDA shows as "Before" and "Offered" in two scroll boxes about 12 lines tall, changed steps as two scroll boxes about 6 lines tall, and changed questions as a before and an after list, each in its scroll box past 3 questions (Long lists, Every page). When the questions changed, it adds: "Switching means answering the new questions: send your updated answers. Answers you already sent stay payable at $<their price>." (only the first sentence when nothing was sent yet). Answers sent for earlier questions stay in the developer's Needs you as "Sent for the questions until <date>" and are paid at the price they were sent at. Until the tester answers it, the offer blocks Send and every call press: the page moves up to this box, highlighted for a few seconds (Why a press stops: New terms come first).
5. **State lines:** why presses are refused (e.g. "You left this test."), the paused banner, the reason their work was ended early (in italics, in a scroll box), "<test> closes in …".
6. **Header card:** the developer (hover card with "Chat with <name>"), the badge row (status, the categories with their icons, the parts, devices and languages when the test has them, "N testers on this test", never "0 testers"; Stopped when stopped), Chat (unread count), the description (the steps, in a scroll box about 6 lines tall), their own deadline ("Due …", a countdown under 24 hours, "late" after), "Open the product", the NDA accepted date. The description, build link and badges are the listing's current ones, following it live.
7. **Stopped line**, when the test stopped or closed or their work was ended early: what happened and what still works, built from what is actually available, e.g. "Nora Lane stopped the test on Nov 3. You can still charge or let go of unreviewed work, dispute, rate and chat. Work you sent is still reviewed." (ended early and closed use their own first half). A team stop never names who stopped it: "This test was stopped at 3:10 PM. You can still … Work you sent is still reviewed." (the time in their own zone, with the date after 12 hours). Under it, the developer's reason for their Stop, in italics in a scroll box (never the team's reason: a team stop reads only that the test was stopped).
8. **Rating box** and **rejoin card** when they apply. The Rate button shows once they sent some work or had a call, and is disabled, with one short grey line under it, while something is still open (Rules: Ratings); after the test's close-out the box is replaced by "Rating closed on <date>.".
9. **Your agreed scope** holds two parts. **The contract** (parts and prices with Required / Free, the live rate and call length, the questions in a scroll box past 3, the NDA, the deadline) is fixed at joining and changes only through Switch / Keep. **The listing information** (title, product, what to do, build link, devices, languages, Mature) always shows the current values, live. Each information line changed since they joined carries a small "Updated <date/time>" tag; tapping it shows before → now in place. Access keys are in neither: their own key never changes. Then **Terms history**: their own deal history, in order: the terms they joined on; every offer, with what changed and what they chose (Switched, Kept or not decided), with dates; renames ("Renamed: "<old product> · <old test>" → "<new product> · <new test>" · <date, time>"); each listing save's before and after; the developer's Stop ("Test stopped", with the reason in its scroll box); their previous question sets; and payments. It never shows other testers' choices.

   [image: The tester's own deal history: the terms they joined on, each offer and what they chose, the rename, their earlier questions, and payments, in order.]
10. **Your parts:** each part's Required or Not required, in the contract or not ("Not listed" when not), sent or not, "Paid so far $Y" (with the developer's note in italics, and for a call the rate when one was typed), and status: draft, sent, under review, paid, "Paid $0: "<reason>"", payment delayed, "The team closed this for $0: “<reason>”", or "Rejected" for older work. Each part repeats its term at the top ("Written answers: $8 · Required"). Past 4 payment lines (the Pay, then each extra) they sit in a scroll box (Long lists, Every page). A listed part sent after their deadline reads, until paid, "Sent late (deadline was <date>): Nora Lane decides what to pay. You can't charge for late work." Charge or Let it go, and Dispute, sit on the part; in the last 7 days before a piece closes the Charge box says "Charge closes on <date>.", and a piece that closed unpaid reads "Not paid (closed on <date>)" (Rules: Close-out). A part that isn't listed is paid at the developer's choice (up to $1,000 in all for written answers or a recording; $200 an hour for a call's measured length, within the $1,000 for all their calls on the test) and never opens Charge. Any part the developer paid can be disputed once, $0 included. If the developer deleted their account, work still under review carries "Can't be paid: <dev> deleted their account". A paid written part (above $0) of a test with no NDA has "Show an excerpt on your profile". A sent, listed part waiting for the developer says, under its part line, when Charge opens (its review date, in their own time zone, moved by pauses like the date itself): "If Nora Lane hasn't paid by Nov 5, you can charge or let it go then.", the same line as on their dashboard. Under the Live call part, each call is one short grey line, "Wed, Oct 28, 5:49 AM · 30 min · waiting for <dev> to pay" (or "paid $X", "Not paid (closed on <date>)"; older calls may read "charged" or "let go"), with Dispute under it when it applies; the part line above already shows the contract rate and what was paid. A listed call still waiting says when it's charged automatically, 7 days after the call (moved by pauses and outages), in their own time zone: "If Nora Lane doesn't pay by Nov 5, it's charged automatically." (only while that date is ahead). Once it's charged but the money waits (the developer's hold or suspension, or their bank's confirmation), the call reads "Payment for this call may be delayed." and the bell says "Payment for your call on “<test>” may be delayed"; never failed or hold. If the developer deleted their account, the call is still charged to their saved card; with no saved card it reads "<dev> deleted their account, so this can't be paid.". There's no Charge or Let it go on a call.
    - **A paid part says whether it can still be paid more** (grey, never a stop): "Nora Lane has paid $10.00 for this. It can be paid more, up to $1,000.00 in all." while Pay more can still add something; "This part is already paid the most it can be ($20.00)." once it's at its cap; "Pay more closed on <date>." once Pay more closed (Rules: Close-out). After an update closed at $0: "Nora Lane paid $0 more for your update." with the note.
    - **Above Send, a grey line** (never a stop): on a paid part, "Nora Lane has paid $10.00 for this. An update can be paid more, up to $1,000.00 in all." (at the cap: "This part is already paid the most it can be ($20.00): Nora Lane can't pay more for an update."; after the close-out: "… Pay more closed on <date>, so Nora Lane can't pay more for an update."); on an unpaid listed part once their deadline passed, "Your deadline passed on <date>. You can still send this: Nora Lane decides what to pay for late work, and you can't charge for it."

    [image: "Paid $0: "<reason>"" on the part; it can be disputed once like any amount.]

    [image: The Live call part: one grey line per call, "<time> · 30 min · waiting for Nora Lane to pay", with "If Nora Lane doesn't pay by <date>, it's charged automatically." under it.]
11. **Live session** (the developer's panel, tester side; it follows the page live): their rate ("Live call: $40/h"), or "Not listed" when a live call isn't a listed part or isn't in their contract; the request form, with the same clash checks as the developer's (Developer test page); the **Call requests** panel with their own waiting requests and the developer's, soonest first (Accept and Decline, or Withdraw), in a scroll box about 5 rows tall; as on the developer's side, a request that closes or is answered elsewhere says so in its row at once ("Withdrawn", "Declined", "Booked", "Closed: …") and its buttons go, and a refusal names the press ("Your Accept didn't go through: …"); calls ahead and in progress ("Call in progress / measuring" until its length is final), each call ahead with "Check your call setup" (Call page). The request form's Length is capped by what's left of the $1,000, as on Developer test page ("At most 95 minutes: the $1,000 for calls with <dev> on this test."). When less than an hour's worth is left of the $1,000 for their calls on this test, a small grey line says "$X left of the $1,000 for your calls on this test."; it never stops anything.

    [image: The tester's wording: "$100.00 left of the $1,000 for your calls on this test."]
12. **Call history (N)**, a fold: every call that's over, newest first (more than 3 in a scroll box about 3 calls tall: Long lists, Every page), with the same outcome badges as the developer's tester row ("Took place · 29 min · paid $14.50", "Cancelled by <name> · 3:05 PM" with the reason, "Nobody joined", "Missed: the site was down"…). A call that took place shows its measured length, "Length: 29 min (both in the call 2:21–2:50 PM)".

    [image: Call history: the same calls and badges on the tester's session page; their Live session keeps only the calls ahead.]
13. **The written part** and **the Recording part.** The written form shows the current questions only, each long one in a scroll box about 6 lines tall. Only a question the developer ticked Required has the "*", right after the question's words (never out at the far right of the row); the others are not required and carry no mark. Whatever is ticked, at least one question must be answered before sending, and every answer given is at least 50 characters (Send my answers, Buttons, below); under an answer box below the minimum a grey counter reads "12 / 50 characters minimum" (red after Send). With more than 3 questions, the questions and their answer boxes sit in a scroll box about 3 questions tall (each answer box keeps its toolbar); "Anything else (bugs, ideas)", the "N / 50,000" counter, the files box and Send stay below it, always visible, and a red mistake on a question scrolls the box to it. A fold per previous set sits below it ("Previous questions and answers (until <date>)", newest first, read-only, at most 3). A previous set keeps what the tester had when they switched: "Sent <date>" for answers they sent, "Draft (not sent)" for answers they never sent, or both, "Sent <date>" first and then "Draft (not sent)" with edits made after sending, each a read-only fold. Every answer set reads the same way wherever it shows (the developer's piece view, this page, "Previous questions and answers" and the team's "See the work"): each question a small grey label (a Required one followed by "*"; an old answer whose question can't be found reads "Question N", not required, with no "*"), its answer indented under it in normal text, and "(no answer)" in grey for one left empty; long ones keep their scroll boxes. Drafts are the tester's own: the developer only ever sees what was sent. All the answers together can be up to 50,000 characters ("N / 50,000"), with a small toolbar for Bold, Italic and Code block, and up to 5 attachments (500 MB each: images, PDF, plain text, zip; a bigger one: "Files can be at most 500 MB.") plus recordings. An upload shows its percentage; a failed one is abandoned at once (its row and any partial file go), so pressing again starts clean. The Recording part takes up to 5 recordings, 500 MB each, uploaded or recorded on the page with Record (the screen, and the microphone if allowed, with a timer and Stop); each shows a preview, its length and size, and Remove (with a confirm).

    [image: The tester's previous set: "Sent <date>" first, then "Draft (not sent)" with the edit they typed after sending.]

    [image: The developer's fold for the same set: only what was sent.]
14. **Can't finish?** with Leave.

### Buttons

- **Alerts: On / Off:** one press, saved for all their tests, and the same setting as each stop box's "Turn off alerts". Off: only new terms from the developer (and their key marked as working) stop a press (Why a press stops).
- **This key doesn't work:** always goes through, once (a tester can't take a report back; the developer decides). The key shows crossed out in the developer's Access keys notice, tester row and Edit, the developer's notice gets a warning row, and their live box gets one quiet line, "Ana reported their key doesn't work" (no bell). They may send another key in a message, or mark the key as working.
- **Switch / Keep:** Switch moves the tester to the new terms (work already sent keeps its price); Keep stays on the current ones. The developer's live box shows a quiet "<name> switched to the new terms"; a Keep tells them nothing. Both act on the offer shown, even one that arrived while the page was open. If a newer offer replaced it, the press stops once and shows the newest, and the second press decides on that one; with no offer left at all: "These terms are no longer on offer." Either answer unlocks Send, all call presses, Charge, Let it go and Dispute, which an open offer blocks.
- **Send my answers / Send my recording**, then "Send my updated answers / recording": sends the work ("Sent late (deadline was <date>)" after their deadline). Written answers need at least one question answered, plus every question the developer ticked Required, and each answer given at least 50 characters (trimmed; formatting marks such as ** and ``` don't count, spaces inside do); an optional question left empty is fine, and "at least one question" means one answer of 50 or more. "Anything else (bugs, ideas)" doesn't count and has no minimum. A short answer is red at that answer, "Write at least 50 characters (12 so far).", and the server refuses with "Each answer needs at least 50 characters." naming the question; Save draft takes anything, and answers sent before the rule stay as they are. The form checks this on the press and the server checks the same rule, with the same question list, one answer per question (listed, unlisted, or after the questions changed and a Switch). An empty Required question is red at that question (the first one; the box scrolls to it), with "Answer every required question before sending." in the list by the button; when none is required and nothing is answered, "Answer at least one question before sending (any one; none is required)." appears under Send, without making an answer red, and clears as soon as any answer has text. After a send, "Send my updated answers" / "Send my updated recording" stays disabled (greyed, tooltip "Nothing has changed since you sent it.") until the answers, notes, attachments or recordings differ from what was sent; changing them back to exactly what was sent disables it again. The server refuses an identical resend with the same line: no new piece, no bell. **A paid part can be sent again** (answers or recording, attachments included), with the grey line above Send saying what an update can still be paid (above): below the piece's cap it goes to the developer's Needs you as an update with Pay more; at the cap it's a quiet line for the developer (Rules: Money). An old rejection and a piece the team closed stay locked ("The team closed this for $0, so it can't be changed."). Work sent after their deadline is "Sent late"; an update of a late piece stays late, and an update sent after the deadline makes the piece late. The Recording part's recordings (up to 5) are sent together as one piece; a 6th add is refused ("You can add at most 5 recordings. Remove one first."), and Send while one is still uploading is refused ("A recording is still uploading. Send once it's done."). An upload that didn't arrive whole is removed: "That upload didn't finish properly. Try again." Recordings can be removed or added and sent again. Written answers send the current set of questions and replace the sent version. After a switch to changed questions, the whole earlier set is kept as a previous set, answers to unchanged questions (drafts included) stay filled in, and changed or new questions start empty. While the test is paused, Send is hidden and drafts can still be saved. While an offer of new terms is open, Send is refused with "Answer the new terms first: Switch or Keep." and the page moves up to the offer (Why a press stops: New terms come first). Bells: "Work sent: …" to the tester; "<name> sent / updated …" to the developer, whose Needs you gets the piece.
- **Charge / Let it go** on a contract part left unpaid, once Charge opens at its review date (Rules): written answers or a recording sent by their own deadline. Work sent late never has either: "You can't charge for work sent after your deadline." A listed call has neither: it's charged automatically 7 days after it (Rules: Money), and an old page's Charge or Let it go on one is refused: "This call is charged automatically, so there's nothing to choose." Charge takes a typed amount up to the listing minus what was paid for that part; the amount rounds to the cent when the box is left (Every page). The button names the amount ("Charge Nora Lane $9.00") and goes through on its own. Above $200 a tick is required, naming the typed amount: "Charge Nora Lane $X now (over $200)"; the button stays disabled until it's ticked, and the server refuses an unticked charge over $200. Let it go has a checkbox, "Let it go won't charge <developer>, and you won't be paid for this". Its button stays disabled until ticked; there is no confirmation pop-up. Works after leaving too, and while the test is paused: during the developer's payment or chargeback hold the charge is recorded and paid when the developer pays what they owe (on a chargeback hold, when the team approves and the reserved money is taken) ("Charged $X. Payment may be delayed until the test resumes."). Never for a part or call outside the tester's contract. The developer sees "<name> charged $X for their <part>" or "<name> let their <part> go: nothing charged" and can no longer Pay it. A declined charge starts the developer's hold; the tester sees "Payment for your work on <test> may be delayed". If the developer's bank wants them to confirm, the charge waits ("Payment may be delayed") until they confirm within 3 days, or it counts as declined. If the developer deleted their account, Charge still works on parts already open to charging, charged to their saved card. Charge and Let it go close on the piece's own closing date, at least 14 days after its Charge opened (Rules: Close-out); a press after it reads "Charge and Let it go closed on <date>.".

- **Dispute** any part the developer paid, once (any amount, $0 included; an unlisted part too; an older rejection too), with a reason (at least a sentence, up to 2,000 characters). The box says how far a decision can go: "You can dispute this once. Our team decides; a decision can only raise the pay, up to $990.00 more." (what's left of the piece's cap). The developer's Needs you and bell get it (their email says the same "A decision can only raise the pay, up to $X more."), and admins get "Dispute on <test>: …". Not for a part they settled themselves with Charge or Let it go, nor one the team closed ("The team closed this for $0, so it can't be disputed."). Dispute is open while the piece is below its cap, the same cap Pay and Pay more have (Rules: Dispute range), so a recording listed at $10 and paid $20 can still be disputed. Once the piece has been paid its cap (Pay and every Pay more, after any earlier decision), there's nothing to dispute: "Dispute the pay" shows greyed with the line "Already paid the most this piece can be paid, so there's nothing to dispute." It updates live, e.g. after a Pay more reaches the cap; one cent below it, Dispute works.
- **Live session:** Request a time (up to 3 rows, Once, Daily or Weekly with 2–8 calls or Forever, one length; clashes are checked while filling it, as on Developer test page), Accept, Decline or Withdraw requests in the Call requests panel, Join call (10 minutes before the start to 30 minutes after the booked end; it opens the call room: Call page), Add to calendar, Cancel ▾ (reason required under 24 hours), cancel the remaining calls of a set, Report a problem (No-show, Late) after the start. Calls can be requested whether or not a live call is a listed part. A request or Accept that would take their calls on this test over $1,000 is refused ("Calls with <dev> on this test can come to at most $1,000; $X is left, so this call can't be booked."). The developer is told each time. While an offer of new terms is open, Request a time, Accept, Decline, Withdraw, Cancel call, cancelling the remaining calls and Join call are refused with "Answer the new terms first: Switch or Keep." until the tester answers it.
- **Leave** (confirm pop-up "Are you sure you want to leave “<test>”?", built from the current state when it opens): never stops; it's refused only when it can't happen ("You already left this test."). Sent work is still reviewed and paid; unsent parts close with no pay; calls ahead are cancelled and a call in progress ends (Call page); the page stays, with live updates. The developer: "<name> left <test>". They can ask to rejoin while the test is open. Leave is hidden once the test stops or closes or their work is ended early: there is nothing left to leave, and their part ends when they rate (DEFAULT).
- **Ask to rejoin** (rejoin card): after leaving or being ended early, before their deadline, never after a stop or rating; the developer accepts and the tester comes back on the current listing with the time they had left (Tester dashboard, Find tests and the join page).
- **Rate and finish** (confirm pop-up while still working; a plain Rate otherwise): 1–5 stars and an optional comment. It's pressable once everything they sent is settled and no dispute of theirs is open; until then the button is disabled with its grey line (Rules: Ratings). The warning lists unsent contract parts (required ones marked) and that calls ahead are cancelled. Rating ends their part; no rejoin afterwards; Dispute stays. Their calls ahead are cancelled, and the call requests still waiting between them close with the reason "the tester finished their part" (Leave, and a removal by a tester-side suspension, close them with "the tester left"). The developer's Rate for them opens then (Rules: Ratings).
- **Chat** opens the per-test chat. It stays open while the test is paused by the team; while the developer is on a hold or suspended, the tester can read it but the message box is replaced by "Messages are paused for now.
