You can still read this chat." (Shared pages). **Block** and **Report** the developer are on the developer's profile; Block never stops.
- **Show an excerpt on your profile** (on a written part the developer paid for, above $0, on a test with no NDA): pick one answer and trim it to 10–500 characters, shown as a plain quote with formatting stripped, always reading "From <test>" (there's no "Don't name the product" tick any more; excerpts already shown or pending with the product hidden keep their "a <category> test" wording). The developer allows or denies it, and the decision comes as a bell line only. At most 3 shown or pending ("You can show at most 3 excerpts. Remove one first."), and one pending request per part. Requests never stop once. If the test now has an NDA, the button is hidden, and a press from an older page is refused with "This test now has an NDA, so its work can't be shown as an excerpt." A request still pending when the test gets an NDA can still be allowed: it goes straight into hidden ("Allowed · hidden while <test> has an NDA" in their list) and shows if the NDA is removed.

### What can stop a press

- **Stop-once** on every button except Leave, Block and Rate (which has its own short list: Why a press stops, Rate), for any change the page hasn't shown yet: new terms (the blocked presses are listed below), the key marked as working, a payment for your work, call changes, listing info, payments, your dispute, and your own changes in another tab or device ("You sent your updated answers in another tab"; Why a press stops: Tester). With Alerts Off, only new terms and the key marked as working stop. Every kind, with its notice text, is in Why a press stops. "This key doesn't work" never stops. The box names the press that was made, Let it go included: "Your Let it go didn't go through." (or "Your Charge didn't go through.").
- **An open offer of new terms blocks** Charge, Let it go, Dispute, Send (answers and recording) and every call press (Request a time, Accept, Decline, Withdraw, Cancel call, Cancel the remaining calls, Join call) until the tester presses Switch or Keep: each press, and the server, answers "Answer the new terms first: Switch or Keep." under the button, and the page moves up to the highlighted Switch / Keep box. Rate, Leave and the rest keep their existing rules (Why a press stops: New terms come first).
- **What still works keeps the same stop-once** (Stopped tests on Every page): on a stopped or closed test, after their work was ended early, or on a test they left or were removed from: Charge, Let it go, Dispute, Rate, chat, asking to show an excerpt and Remove excerpt, the money and Rate presses until they close (Rules: Close-out), and while their own tester side is suspended only Dispute (How it looks, below); on a paused test: saving drafts, Charge, Let it go, Dispute, Rate, Leave, Cancel call, chat and excerpts. Each stops once for a change the page hasn't shown (Leave, the excerpt presses and chat never do; Rate keeps its own list). On a page opened before the stop or the pause, Charge, Let it go, Dispute and Cancel call stop once for it ("Nora Lane stopped this test at 3:10 PM.", "This test was paused at 3:10 PM."), Rate for a stop only, and a Cancel call after a stop is refused ("This call was cancelled when the test stopped."). Leaving or being removed no longer blocks Dispute or excerpt requests.

  [image: Leave never stops: the listing changed after the page loaded, and Leave (after its own confirm) went through on the first press.]
- **Always refused** (blue, under the button, naming the press):
  - After a stop, a close or their work ended early, a press of something now gone (Switch, Keep, Send, an upload, Remove, Request a time, Accept, Decline, Withdraw, Cancel call, "This key doesn't work") from an old page is refused with no stop box, and the page moves to the top, where the stopped line names it: "<dev> stopped the test on <date>, so your Send didn't go through. Work you sent is still reviewed." (ended early and closed use their own first half; the team's Stop: "This test was stopped at 3:10 PM, so your Send didn't go through. Work you sent is still reviewed.").
  - "You left this test."; "You rated this test, so your part is finished."; "<dev> stopped the test on <date>. Work you sent is still reviewed."; after the team's Stop, "This test was stopped at 3:10 PM. Work you sent is still reviewed." (never who, and always with its time, on every path: the time in their own saved zone, with the date added after 12 hours, never the date alone); "This test ended on <date>. Work you sent is still reviewed."; "<dev> ended your session early on <date>."; "Your session on <test> ended." (after a block); "This test closed on <date>."
  - Charge: "<dev> paid $X for this meanwhile."; "You can't charge for work sent after your deadline."; "You can choose once 7 days have passed since you sent it…"; "This part is already paid in full."; "This call is charged automatically, so there's nothing to choose." (Charge or Let it go on a listed call from an old page); "A payment for this is being confirmed right now."; "Charge and Let it go closed on <date>."; while their tester side is suspended, "You can do this once your account is active again."
  - Switch / Keep: "These terms are no longer on offer."
  - Send and every call press while an offer is open: "Answer the new terms first: Switch or Keep." (every press, until Switch or Keep).
  - Dispute: "You already disputed this payment. A part can be disputed once."; "You settled this part yourself (Charge or Let it go), so there's no developer decision to dispute."; "The team closed this for $0, so it can't be disputed."; "Already paid the most this piece can be paid, so there's nothing to dispute."
  - Send my updated answers / recording: "Nothing has changed since you sent it."
  - Rate (the box shows the same line, disabled): "Charge or let go of your unreviewed work first."; "Rating opens once the team decides your dispute."; "Rating opens on <date>, or sooner if <dev> reviews everything you sent."; "Rating closed on <date>."; "You can rate once your account is active again."
  - Calls: "This time is no longer free."; "You already have 3 booked times in this test."; the $1,000 limit for calls and the length it allows; "This call was cancelled when the test stopped." (Cancel call on a page opened before the stop); the Join refusals ("Your Join call didn't go through: <name> cancelled this call at <time>.", "You can join from <time>.", "This call is over.").
  - Chat, while the developer is restricted: "Messages are paused for now, so this wasn't sent." (the typed text stays).
- The stop and work-ended lines also appear in the live box, and the Switch / Keep box disappears. The live box's line for the team's Stop is "This test was stopped", with its time; the developer's own Stop reads "<dev> stopped the test".

### How it looks in each state

- **Open, working:** as above.
- **Paused** (the developer is on a payment or chargeback hold, their developer side is suspended, or the team paused this test): what doesn't work right now is hidden: Send, the call-request tools and Join call (the server still refuses them). A blue banner at the top lists what still works, built from what is actually available, e.g. "This test is paused right now. For now you can save drafts of your parts, charge or let go of work left unreviewed, dispute, rate, leave the test, and cancel booked calls. Sending work and requesting calls come back when it resumes." When their deadline moves with pauses, it adds "Your deadline is paused too: it moves later by however long the pause lasts." Payment for their work may be delayed. When the pause lifts, the deadline moves later by the length of the pause. Chat follows the rule above.
- **Calls during a pause:** a call in progress ends at once (Call page); calls starting within 3 days are cancelled with no fault ("Your call on <day> was cancelled because the test is paused."); later ones stay booked (Rules).
- **Stopped or closed, or their work ended early:** the page shows only what still works. Gone at once (hidden live, and refused on the server): the Switch / Keep offer, Send, Record, attachments and drafts' Save, Request a time, Accept, Decline, Withdraw, Cancel call, Join call, the access key box with "This key doesn't work", Leave, and Alerts when nothing left can stop. It shows the live-box line, the Stopped label and the stopped line, their agreed scope, every part with its status (sent, under review, paid with the amount, payment delayed), the work they sent (readable), Call history and Terms history. Charge or Let it go, Dispute, Rate, chat, asking to show an excerpt and Remove excerpt work, with the usual stop-once. A paused test hides what doesn't work too, under its banner (above).
- **The tester left, was ended early or rated:** the page shows the same read-only view as a stopped test, with the matching line (ended early: the developer's reason, in italics); the rejoin card when it applies.

  [image: Ended early: the reason in italics in a scroll box on the tester's page (never in the bell line).]
- **The tester side is suspended:** they were removed from the test. Opened from "Tests you were removed from" on their dashboard, the page shows a limited view, with the line "Your account is paused. You can still dispute a payment." at the top: their parts with status and what each was paid, the work they sent (read-only), and **Dispute as the only press**, which follows the usual stop-once. Charge and Let it go are hidden and wait for the reinstatement (nothing is lost: Charge never happens on its own, and the suspended time is added to their Charge windows: Rules, Close-out); Rate is hidden too. Sending work, drafts, calls (already cancelled when they were removed), chat, the key box, Leave and excerpt requests are hidden; excerpt requests come back when the suspension lifts. A tester whose developer is suspended or on hold keeps the full page, with the paused rules above.

  [image: A suspended tester's limited view of a test they were removed from: "Your account is paused. You can still dispute a payment.", their parts, and only Dispute.]

### Bell lines that open this page

- "You're in…", "You're back in…", "<test> is running again", "<test> was stopped", "…ended early", "<test> ended", "Your session on <test> ended.", "Your deadline on “<test>” is <date>…", chat → the top.
- "<dev> updated the listing: …" → the Switch / Keep offer.
- "Work sent…", "<dev> paid you $X…", "<dev> paid $0…", "…paid you $X more…", "…paid you $X for your call on <date>" (a listed call's automatic charge), "…is delayed…", "Payment for your call on “<test>” may be delayed", "<dev> paid $X for your <part>", "“<test>”: the team closed your <part> for $0", "Your dispute … was decided" → that part or call.
- "<dev> gave you an access key for “<test>”" → the session page, where the key is.
- "Your call with <dev> … starts at <time>." → that call in Live session.
- (The daily "Charge or let go" line and the rating lines open the tester dashboard: Tester dashboard.)
- A call line about a call ahead → that call in Live session; about a call that's over → that call in Call history, opened and marked.

The developer's side of each press is on Developer test page.

## Shared pages

Pages both sides use. From Every page: notices, the live box, kept drafts, and the closed pages during a hold or suspension.

## Header and side switch

Shared pages use the saved side when no side is specified by their path. Admin Account has no Payments or Blocked people links.

- One account can be a developer, a tester or both. The header switches between the two sides, each with its own menu, dashboard and pages. Messages, Notifications, Payments, Profile and Settings belong to neither.
- The header switch posts directly to the switch route. Other side links use that route: same-site visits and direct visits continue automatically; cross-site and email visits require the switch confirmation. Only a checked POST changes the saved side. Opening a side-specific page also remembers that side. Each tab shows its page's side.
- An admin account has no side or side switch. Its white header has Admin, Developers, Testers and Tests, with icons, the bell, Account and Sign out. There is no Back to the app link. The bar wraps on phones. Developers and Testers have separate lists filtered by role, with name and email search; an account with both roles appears in both. Tests opens the admin Find tests view. These pages refuse non-admins.
- During a hold or suspension the header loses Post a test, Messages and Profile (and Find tests for a suspended tester side). Suspensions are per side: on a mixed account, switching to the restricted side lands on its dashboard top with that side's blue box, and the other side works as normal.

## Public profiles and hover cards

### What's on them

- **Developer profile** (as testers see it): "Posting tests since", Pays on average (the average share of the listing paid, with "of the listing, over N pieces of work", or "-" and "no paid work yet"), Typical review, Rating ("4.6 ★ (12 ratings)"), comments without the rater's name, and Open tests in a scroll box. Open tests holds only what this tester could open now: invite-only tests aren't listed, as in Find tests, unless this tester is invited to, applying to or in that test. Tests the tester is part of keep their place: one they're **working in** always shows, whatever its spots or Open until, with "You're in this test", and opens their session page; one they have an **open invite** to stays listed when it's full, with "Full right now: your invite stays open" (as on their Invitations card), and opens the join page; a **pending application** shows "Applied (waiting)" (a test that fills closes it as before, and then the test leaves the list). It stays current on the page's poll: a test that pauses, fills up, stops, passes Open until, turns invite only for this tester, or whose invite to them closes leaves the list, and a newly open test appears. A test pressed from an older page opens with only its reason (Join page); going Back to the profile shows the list without it at once, never the old list for the next poll (Why going Back is safe).
- **Tester profile** (as developers see it): "Tester since", Accepted parts ("N of M sent · N sessions with accepted work"), Rating, About (bio, devices, languages), up to 3 shown excerpts (plain quotes, each "From <test>"; older ones whose tester hid the product read "a <category> test"; each with the question it answers above it in small grey text, 2 lines, then "Show more", and "Anything else (bugs, ideas)" for that box), "Chat with <name>".
- **"Your tests with <name>"** on a tester's profile, only for a developer viewing it: every one of that developer's own tests the tester has joined, most recent first, in a scroll box. Two tick boxes above it, **Open** and **Closed**, both ticked at first; unticking both shows all too. Open means still running (taking testers, or joining closed with testers still working); Closed means stopped, ended or closed. Each row: the test name (opens the developer's test page), "Joined <date>" and the tester's state there (Working, Finished, Left, Ended). None yet: "<name> hasn't been in any of your tests yet." Other developers' tests never show. The tester's own view and admins don't get it; it isn't shown when there's a block either way.

  [image: "Your tests with Ana Silva" on their profile, with the Open and Closed ticks, one open test (Working) and one stopped (Finished).]
- **Hover cards** on names: the Testers-panel hover card (developer side) and testers' hover cards on a developer's name. Each has "Chat with <name>". Excerpts are shown only on profiles, never in hover cards. Excerpts from a test that now has an NDA are hidden from profiles, and come back if the NDA is removed.
- **Admins** also see admin-only boxes on profiles, with Suspend, Reinstate and Delete (Administrator console). The admins' own view of Open tests lists every test.
- **During a hold or suspension** profiles are closed: a tester whose tester side is suspended, or a developer on hold or with their developer side suspended, who opens one moves to their dashboard top.
- **Blocked profiles**, with the normal header and a "← Back" link, never a bare error page:
  - **They blocked you:** only "This profile isn't available." The block is never revealed.
  - **You blocked them:** their profile opens as usual (name, ratings, stats, bio, excerpts), with **Unblock <name>** where Block was. While blocked there's no Chat with and no invite (Report stays), and a developer's Open tests is replaced by "You blocked <name>, so their tests are hidden."
  - An unknown or deleted id shows "This page doesn't exist." with the header.

### Buttons

- **Chat with <name>:** opens the per-test chat (on a profile, the most recent shared test; hidden when there is none). After a block either way, a suspension or any other refusal: "Unable to chat with this tester." / "Unable to chat with this developer."
- **Block** (confirm pop-up "Block <name>?", button "Block <name>"). The pop-up says what happens, then "They aren't told. You can unblock in Account → Blocked people.":
  - on a tester's profile (the developer blocks): "Their sessions on your tests end now (work they sent stays payable), calls between you are cancelled, their applications and your invites close, and chat closes. They won't see your tests any more."
  - on a developer's profile (the tester blocks): "Your sessions on their tests end now (work you sent stays payable, and you can still charge, dispute and rate it), calls between you are cancelled, your applications and invites close, and chat closes. You won't see their other tests any more."

  Either way a call in progress between them ends at once (Call page: "Call ended: you blocked <name>." for the one who blocked, "Call ended: the call was cancelled." for the other), the tester gets the neutral bell "Your session on <test> ended.", and the session page drops Join call, Request a time and the call requests on its next poll.
- **Unblock <name>** (on the profile of someone you blocked): no confirm; "Unblocked." shows, and the profile comes back in full at once (Chat with, Open tests). Requests, offers and invites between you close. Whoever blocked whom, the tester never sees that developer's tests they weren't already part of: not in Find tests, not by a direct link ("This test isn't available right now."), not in bell lines or emails about new tests, and Ask to rejoin is refused neutrally (Join page). Tests they were part of stay on their dashboard with their history, payments, Charge, Dispute and Rate (a working session ends at the block). Waiting applications between them close; the tester reads only "“<test>”: your application was closed.", and the developer gets no line. Unblocking brings everything back as it was before (Find tests, profile, links); nothing the block closed reopens by itself.
- **Report <name>:** a reason (at least 5 characters) and Send to the team. Once per person, ever. Afterwards: "You reported <name> on <date>." The reported person isn't told; admins get "New report: <a> reported <b>". A call's no-show or late report stays on the call.
- Block and Report exist only on profiles: the tester profile on the developer side and the developer profile on the tester side.

### What can stop a press

- Block: "You already blocked <name>." (blocked in another tab). Report: "You already reported <name> on <date>."

## Your profile

- **Developer:** "Your developer profile" with "See it as testers do", name, and their ratings.
- **Tester:** "Your tester profile" with "How developers see you", name, bio, devices and languages (at least one of each), and their ratings.
- **Excerpts** (tester): "Your excerpts" on their own profile lists them, pending, shown or denied, each with the question it answers and Remove (which never stops once, and sends the developer no bell line). Only the tester removes an excerpt: the developer has no Take down. One from a test that now has an NDA is marked "Hidden while <test> has an NDA", or "Allowed · hidden while <test> has an NDA" when the developer allowed it under the NDA. While their tester side is suspended, the same list, with Remove, is on their dashboard under the blue box; they can't make new requests until it lifts. Blocks, holds and suspensions don't affect excerpts.
- The profile editor also has the Developer / Tester / both switch.
- Names need at least 1 character. Saving changes what others see at once; a tester's new device or language updates open Join anyway and Invite anyway boxes.
- **Changed in another tab or device:** the page updates silently with what was saved elsewhere, keeping what is being typed. The first press after such a change stops once (Settings, below).
- Closed during a hold or suspension.

## Settings (Account)

Opened from your name in the header. It shows only the side you're on.

- **Top:** the name and email card ("not confirmed yet" with Resend the link), headed "<name> · Developer" or "<name> · Tester".
- **The other side:** when it's off, one small line with a button, "Want to test other people's products too? Start testing too" or "Want to post tests too? Start posting tests too". When both are on, nothing here (the header's ⇄ switches sides). An admin account never gets this line.
- **Settings links:** the profile of the side you're on (Tester profile or Developer profile), Payments, Notifications and alerts, Blocked people, Privacy and Contact us.
- **Change your password** (a form on this page; signs out other devices), **Signed-in devices** and **Delete account**. Blocked people and Delete account are account-wide: a block is between people, and Delete account removes the whole login, both sides.
- **Changed in another tab or device** (Payments, the profile editor and this page, from Change password up): the page updates silently on its poll, with no live line, and fields being typed in keep what was typed. The first press after such a change stops once with a blue box, "Changed in another tab or device since you opened this page:" and what changed in short form ("Card: Visa ending 1111 → Visa ending 4242", "Subscription: cancelled, ends Dec 1", "Devices: Windows PC → Windows PC, iPad", "Password changed at 3:10 PM"), then "Press <button> again to go ahead." Typed values are kept, and the second press saves them on top. A field changed elsewhere to exactly what this tab has isn't listed. Repeats are plain refusals ("Your subscription is already cancelled.", "You're already subscribed."). Delete account, Sign out of all devices and the email setting keep their own rules.

  [image: Settings for a developer: "Nora Lane · Developer", the line "Want to test other people's products too? Start testing too", the Settings links, Change your password, Signed-in devices and Delete account.]
- **Contact us** (its own page, linked from here) emails every admin, with the whole message (up to 50,000 characters, "N / 50,000"); admins reply by email. At most 5 messages per account a day; a 6th shows, under Send: "You've sent 5 messages today. We'll reply by email." It stays open for suspended and held accounts, and is how they appeal. Requests about personal data (for example, a copy of it) go through Contact us.
- **Delete account** (confirm pop-up "Delete your account?"): every open tab signs out with "This account no longer exists". What deletion does is in Every page.
- **Deleting as a tester:** the pop-up is a full warning with their real numbers:
  - "Pay already sent to your Stripe account stays there. Your Stripe account is separate from Playtest: you can still sign in to Stripe and withdraw it."
  - "$X is waiting for your payout setup. Finish it before deleting, or it can't reach you." with a "Set up payouts" link (only when some is waiting).
  - Delayed payments still reach their Stripe account when the developer pays, if payout setup is finished.
  - "Work you already sent can still be paid by the developer, to your Stripe account if it's set up. After deleting, you can't charge for it or dispute it."
  - "You leave every test you're in. Calls ahead are cancelled, and your invites and applications are cancelled. Open disputes are withdrawn."
  - "Your chats close. The ratings you gave and got stay."
  - "You can't sign in again, and this can't be undone."
- **Deleting as a developer** always works. The warning lists, with counts, work waiting for review, parts testers can still charge, and open disputes, then: "Testers can still charge for parts already open to charging. Work you haven't reviewed can't be charged after you delete." What they owe stays owed. For a subscriber it adds "Your subscription ends now, with no refund for the rest of the month."
- **Signed-in devices:** "Sign out of all devices" signs this account out everywhere, this device included, and every open tab goes to the sign-in page.
- During a hold or suspension Settings stays open with Contact us, the email setting, Sign out of all devices and Delete account; the profile editor and profile link are hidden.

## Terms of Use

- The Terms of Use page reads "Version 2 · DRAFT, wording to be reviewed before launch" and stays marked draft for the lawyer's review. Everyone agrees to it at sign-up, and each account's accepted version is kept.
- **What changed**, a short note at the top, newest version first. Version 2 (Oct 28, 2026; nothing has launched, so later changes were folded into it rather than raising the version):
  - The content rule (section 5) now reads "Never allowed: sexual content involving minors, illegal content, real-world gore. Other graphic or mature content must be marked Mature (18+), with a reason.", the same rule as the Mature line on Post a test and Edit.
  - "Payments section rewritten": every money rule is now in one place, section 4, How payments work, in short headed paragraphs with an example each.
  - "A call's length is measured by the app while both people are connected in the call room, up to 150% of the booked length and never past the $1,000 for that tester's calls. The developer pays by the hour at the call's rate; paying less needs a reason the tester reads, and the tester can dispute it once." Nobody lowers a call's length.
  - "A live call ends for both people when it reaches 150% of the booked length or its pay would pass what's left of the $1,000 for that tester's calls on the test, and when a block, a pause, a suspension, a leave, End early or a stop happens; the minutes before that are paid as usual." (Call page)
  - "Calls happen in the app's own call room, on the call page, straight between the two browsers (section 7)."
  - Agreeing again when the Terms change (section 8, and below).
  - "You must be 18 or older to use Playtest. Signing up (with email or Google) asks you to confirm it (section 1)."
  - "To decide a dispute, our team can read the chat between the tester and the developer (section 4, Paying for work)."
  - "A piece of work can be paid at most the part's maximum in all, listed or not: $1,000 for written answers or a recording, $200 an hour for a call's measured length. Pay, Pay more and a dispute decision all count toward it (section 4, Pay more)."
  - "A listed call the developer hasn't paid 7 days after it took place is charged to their card automatically; there's no Charge or Let it go for it (section 4, Calls)."
  - "Emails about payments are always sent the same evening, whatever your email setting, Off included (section 4, Bank checks, declines, holds)."
- **The sections:** 1. What this platform is, which opens its rules with "You must be 18 or older to use Playtest.", the sign-up tick's own words; 2. Posting a test (developers), which says posting is free, points to section 4 for what developers pay, and says "Every test takes at most 10 testers."; 3. Joining and sending work (testers); 4. How payments work; 5. Conduct; 6. Feedback, ratings and confidentiality (ratings go both ways, the tester rates first); 7. Calls and recordings (calls run in the app's own call room, video and sound going directly between the two browsers; only that call's developer and tester can be in it, from 10 minutes before the start until the call ends; when the two browsers were connected is recorded to measure the length, and the team can read it when handling a report; "Nothing in the call is recorded."); 8. The service and changes to these terms.
- **Section 4, How payments work** (its own anchor, `/terms#how-payments-work`, which the Charge tick on Post a test and Edit links to) opens with "Payment is for the work the tester sent, judged against the listing, not for whether the feedback is positive." It then gives every money rule as a short headed paragraph with a one-line example: Prices and the split; Days per tester; Paying for work; Pay more; Unreviewed work: Charge or Let it go; Close-out; Calls; Unlisted work; Bank checks, declines, holds; Payouts; Subscriptions; Taxes and refunds. They are Rules (Money, Close-out, Plans and the subscription) in plain words, not new rules; where wording differed, the app's rules were written. "Paying for work" says the tester can dispute a payment on each piece once, and the team's decision "can only raise the pay (up to the piece's cap, the same one Pay more has, less what that piece was already paid)", then "To decide a dispute, our team can read the chat between the tester and the developer." "Pay more" allows extra "within the piece's cap: the part's maximum in all, listed or not ($1,000 for written answers or a recording; $200 an hour for a call's measured length), counting the first payment, every Pay more and any dispute decision", adds "A listed price at the maximum leaves no room for Pay more.", says all of one tester's calls on a test stay within $1,000 together, and gives the example "A written part listed at $20 can be paid up to $1,000 in all." "Unlisted work" says such a part is paid any amount, $0 included, "at most $1,000 for written answers or a recording, and $200 an hour for a call". "Calls" ends with the automatic charge: "A listed call the developer hasn't paid 7 days after it took place is charged to their card automatically for its measured length at the call's rate (within the $1,000), with no Charge or Let it go." "Unreviewed work: Charge or Let it go" keeps Charge for listed written answers and recordings, says that work sent after the tester's own deadline never opens Charge or Let it go (the developer decides what to pay, any amount, $0 included, below the listing with a reason the tester reads, and the tester can still dispute once), and adds that "A listed call works differently: if the developer hasn't paid it 7 days after it took place, their card is charged automatically for its measured length at the call's rate (within the $1,000 for that tester's calls), with no Charge or Let it go. Pauses and outages move these dates later." "Bank checks, declines, holds" says a declined payment is owed by the developer, and so is a chargeback, plus the $15 fee ("A $10 payment reversed by the bank: $25 owed ($10 + the $15 chargeback fee)."); it counts a listed call's automatic charge among the charges made while the developer isn't there; after a chargeback "the tests stay paused until the developer verifies a card with 3D Secure and presses Pay what you owe, which reserves the amount on their card: it's taken when our team approves lifting the hold, and released if they don't approve or after about 7 days.", and ends "Emails about payments (receipts, payments, declines, holds, delays, payouts and chargebacks) are always sent the same evening, whatever the email setting, Off included." These changes kept version 2, as earlier Terms changes did (nothing has launched). The developer owes a chargeback; the platform takes the loss only if they never pay (Rules: Money). "Payouts" says a tester's pay waits for their payout setup "and it goes out on its own once the setup is done"; nothing about suspensions. "Taxes and refunds" says payments aren't refunded, except when the platform wins a chargeback the developer already paid.
  - Example (Days per tester): "Opened Nov 6 with Open until Nov 20: 14 days per tester, so a tester who joins Nov 19 is due Dec 3."
  - Example (Calls): "A call at $30 an hour where both people were connected for 50 minutes: $25.00."
- **Agreeing to updated Terms.** When the version goes up, an account on an older one is asked once, at its next Post the test or posting a draft (developer), or Join, Apply, Accept invitation or Ask to rejoin (tester): one more required tick above the button, in the same style as the Charge tick, shown only while the account hasn't agreed to the current version: "I agree to the updated Terms of Use. Read what changed" ("Read what changed" opens the note above in a new tab). Unticked, the press is refused with the red "Tick to agree to the updated Terms of Use." at the tick and in the mistake list; the server refuses the same. Ticked, the press goes through and records the current version. Age isn't asked here: it's confirmed only at sign-up (Every page: Sign-in and accounts).
- Nothing else is blocked: work in progress, paying, charging, disputes, chat and everything on running tests carry on under the terms they started with.

## Notifications

- "How you're notified": email Off, or On with Daily, Weekly or Monthly ("Your alert settings are saved."). Under it: "Payment updates are always emailed the same evening. That includes receipts, payments, failures, holds, retries, delays, payouts and chargebacks, even with email Off, Weekly or Monthly. Some account emails always send." (Every page: Emails).
- The bell list: newest first, stacked entries open to show their lines, each line opens its exact spot. Opening the page marks nothing read: a line is read when it's clicked, a stack when it's expanded or one of its lines is clicked; there's no "mark all as read" (Every page: The bell).
- The bell list stays open during a hold or suspension; a line whose page is closed opens the dashboard top.

## Messages and chat

- One chat per tester per test, only between the developer and testers who are or were in the test. Unread counts show on the Chat buttons.
- Chat is closed only while a person is restricted (their developer side or tester side suspended, or a developer on a payment or chargeback hold), never because a test is paused. For the restricted person, Messages and chat move them to the dashboard top; a Send from a page loaded earlier is refused the same way, and the typed text is kept.
- The other person can still open the chat and read everything. The message box is replaced by a neutral note, "Messages are paused for now. You can still read this chat." A Send from a page loaded before then shows "Messages are paused for now, so this wasn't sent." under Send, keeps the text and hides the box. When the restriction lifts, the box comes back live. This covers every chat where the restricted person is on the restricted side.

  [image: The team paused the test: the chat stays open for both.]
- After a block, or when an account is deleted: "This chat is closed."
- Each message reaches the other side's bell as "<name> sent you a message on “<test>”"; the message itself is in the chat and the email, never in the bell.
- A message sent twice by accident (the answer was lost and Send was pressed again) posts once; the same words sent again on purpose post again. At most 30 messages a minute per person ("You're sending messages very fast. Wait a minute, then send again.").

## Payments

A tester can join and work before finishing payout setup. The grey hint under the join deadline and above the session agreed scope says "You can join and work now; payments wait until you finish your payout setup.", with Finish setup. Waiting shares are totalled in one payment bell/email line: "$8.00 is waiting for you: finish your payout setup to receive it." The five-minute sweep sends the first reminder and repeats about every three days until setup is done; deleted accounts are excluded. Everything you sent shows the full amount paid, including Pay more and extras: Paid $1,000.00 / You get $800.00.

### What's on it

- **Developer:** "Your card" (Add a card, or Change card, goes to Stripe's page, which saves only a card or a card through Link; a card saved through Link works like any card) and "What you've paid" (receipts and history, paid so far per part). Pay is not here: it's in each test's Needs you, linked from the dashboard's Needs review. A payment the payment service never answered shows as being checked until it's finished or cleared (Every page).
  - **During a payment hold only:** "Pay what you owe" with the exact amount. It pays exactly what it shows. More declines during the hold add to it, and so do testers' charges and disputes decided for them during the hold. It updates live (about every 20 seconds) and disappears when nothing is owed. A chargeback's line reads "Your bank reversed a $X payment: $X + $15 chargeback fee". Automatic retries (Rules: Retries during a payment hold) show "Last try: <date> · next try: <date>", or "No more automatic tries: press Pay what you owe".
  - **During a chargeback hold:** a red box headed "Your bank reversed a payment, so your account is on a chargeback hold. Your tests are paused. To lift it:" (the same box on the test page), then the three steps, each ✓ or ✗, in this order: "A card verified with 3D Secure", "What you owe reserved on your card", "The team's approval". "Verify a card" starts the check. Pay what you owe stays disabled until a card is verified, with the line "Verify your card first (step 1): then you can pay what you owe." (the server refuses it too: "Verify your card first."). Here Pay what you owe **reserves** the money instead of charging it: the owed total is held on the card (Stripe shows it "Uncaptured"), with the bank's check inline if it asks. The box says "Pay what you owe reserves the money on your card: nothing is taken until the team approves lifting your hold, and your tests resume then." Nothing is retried on its own during a chargeback hold, so there's no "next try" line.
    - Once Pay what you owe has reserved, step 2 shows ✓ and the box reads "Reserved $X: it's taken when the team approves lifting your hold." The team's Approve takes it and lifts the hold in one step: the testers waiting on that money are paid then, and the developer gets one notice saying both, with no separate receipt line for the same money: "The team approved lifting your hold: the $X reserved on your card was taken, and your tests are running again." (with a suspension still on: "The team approved lifting your hold: the $X reserved on your card was taken. Your tests stay paused while your account is suspended."). Only when an Approve takes reserved money without lifting the hold does a receipt say it: "Receipt: you paid $X for what you owed (reserved on your card, taken on the team's approval)".
    - **Pay what you owe reserves once.** Anything added after the reservation (a tester's Charge meanwhile, a listed call's automatic charge, a dispute decided for a tester) is never reserved: it's charged right after the team's Approve lifts the hold, on the verified card, as an ordinary charge. The box says so, "Reserved $23.00: it's taken when the team approves lifting your hold. $5.00 added since will be charged right after.", with the added lines listed, and Pay what you owe stays disabled (the server refuses an old page's press with the same line). Approve is never held back by it. Right after the lift each added charge gets its own receipt naming it ("Receipt: you paid $5.00 for Ana Silva's written answers (charged by them after 7 days)"), and the tester is paid when it goes through. If the bank wants to confirm it, it waits in "Your bank wants you to confirm" (3 days); if it's declined, a normal payment hold starts (a reversed payment charged again that's declined reopens its chargeback instead). A new Pay what you owe is needed only if the reservation expired or was released, and it then reserves the full amount owed at that time.
    - If the team doesn't approve, the reservation is released and nothing is taken; the hold stays: "The team didn't approve lifting your hold: the $X reserved on your card was released, and nothing was taken."
    - A reservation lasts about 7 days. If the team hasn't approved by then, it's released (nothing taken) and the box asks again: "Your reservation expired: press Pay what you owe again so the team can approve it." (also a bell line).

    [image: A chargeback hold: step 1 not done, Pay what you owe disabled with "Verify your card first (step 1): then you can pay what you owe."]

    [image: Verified and reserved: "Reserved $X: it's taken when the team approves lifting your hold."]
  - **"Your bank wants you to confirm":** payments the bank wants confirmed (made while the developer wasn't there), e.g. "A payment $12.50 on “Harbor Run” · confirm by Mon, Nov 2, 3:10 PM" ("A team decision $5.00 on …" for a dispute decision, "A payment your bank had reversed $25.00 on …" for a retried chargeback, "Your subscription $7.00 · confirm by <date>" for a renewal), the time in the developer's own saved time zone. A charge on a test links to "Confirm with your bank →" under that piece on its test page (Developer test page), highlighted; a renewal, which belongs to no test, is confirmed right here with the same inline check (with no stop-once: there's no test page to have missed a change on; the plain refusals still apply). After 3 days one counts as declined. The same charges are listed at the very top of the developer dashboard, "Your bank wants you to confirm (N)", soonest deadline first, each with Confirm with your bank →: a charge on a test opens its piece on the test page, a renewal opens its row here, highlighted (Developer dashboard).
  - **Refunds:** a chargeback the platform won gives a receipt line, "Refund: your bank's chargeback of $X was reversed".
- **Subscription · $7/month** (developers; the plans are in Rules):
  - Not subscribed: the perks list and "Subscribe for $7/month", which needs a saved card. Subscribe charges the first month at once; if the bank wants to confirm it, its check opens inline under the button, like Pay's. Cancelled or failed, the new subscription is cancelled and nothing is charged, and the next Subscribe is a new attempt.
  - Subscribed: active since, renews on, and "Cancel subscription".
  - Cancelled: "You can use the perks until <date>; you won't be charged again." with "Keep my subscription".
  - Free during launch: "Free during launch until <date>: everything is included. Subscribe to keep the extras after that." With no date set: "Free during launch".
  - Each subscription charge gets a receipt line. During a hold or suspension billing pauses; Cancel and Keep my subscription still work, and Subscribe is refused: "You can subscribe once your account is active again."
- **Tester:** "Everything you sent" with statuses ("Payment may be delayed" when it is), "Where your money goes" (payout setup with Stripe; it needs a confirmed email: "Confirm your email first: payouts go only to a confirmed account. We sent you a link."), "Your earnings" (each payment's share; " · delayed until your payout account is ready" while it waits for payout setup).
  - **Pay that waited for payout setup goes out on its own** once the setup is done (when Stripe says the account can take payouts, or on coming back from Stripe's setup): every waiting share is sent, each once, and the tester gets "Your payout setup is done: $X on its way." Each share is sent from the developer's own charge, so it never needs money in the platform's balance and arrives as soon as that charge's money does; "on its way" means just that. A share whose sending failed is sent again by the daily run once the tester's setup is done. A suspended tester is paid like anyone (Rules: Money).
  - When a payout fails: "Your bank couldn't receive a payout. Update your payout details." with a button to Stripe; the money stays in their Stripe balance.
- Payments stays open during holds and suspensions. Change card works during a payment hold, a chargeback hold and a suspension, and a tester whose tester side is suspended can still set up payouts.

### Buttons and what can stop them

- **Pay what you owe:** pays every owed charge. It stops once for every change (another decline, a chargeback, a tester's Charge, a reversed chargeback, a payment in another tab), also during a hold: "What you owe changed: now $X. Press again to pay $X." It never charges an amount the developer didn't see. When nothing is owed, the server refuses it, so another tab's payment is never charged twice. During a chargeback hold it waits for a verified card, then reserves what is owed instead of charging it, once (above): while a reservation is in place it's disabled, and a press from an old page is refused with "Reserved $X: it's taken when the team approves lifting your hold. $Y added since will be charged right after." (or, when nothing was added, "There's nothing more to reserve: what you owe is already reserved on your card."). Payment holds keep charging at once, by this press and by the automatic retries. If the bank wants to confirm, its check opens inline under the button (Rules: Your bank wants to confirm); cancelled or failed: "Your bank didn't confirm the payment, so nothing was charged." (no hold). When everything is paid a payment hold lifts at once ("Your tests are running again"; testers: "<test> is running again"; while a suspension continues: "Your payment went through. Your tests stay paused while your account is suspended."), and payments that were waiting are charged; testers are paid the charges that were added for them. From the 4th hold on, admins must Approve first ("Hold lift waiting…"). Holds are in Rules.
- **Verify a card:** the 3D Secure check for a chargeback hold. The press shows Stripe's card form inline, in a white box under the steps, with **Verify this card** under it, disabled until the form is ready to take a card; Verify this card then checks it, and the bank's check opens inline under the button, like Subscribe's. Verified: "Card verified." and step 1 shows ✓. Not verified: "The card wasn't verified. Try again or use another card." A cancelled or failed check frees the press: the next Verify a card starts a new card form and check. The card form shows cards only: no Link bank tab and no wallets (Apple Pay, Google Pay), because the check needs a card with 3D Secure. Recordings with the fake payment service show the stand-in instead (Rules: Your bank wants to confirm).
- **Change card, Subscribe, Cancel subscription, Keep my subscription and payout setup** stop once after a change made in another tab or device (Settings, above).

### Bell lines that open this page

- Hold lines ("Your tests are paused…", "Another payment was declined…", "Your bank reversed…", "Your reservation expired…") → Pay what you owe or the chargeback steps; "The team didn't approve lifting your hold…" → the chargeback steps; "The team approved lifting your hold" (when it didn't lift the hold), receipts with no test and "We charged your card $X for what you owed: …" → the top; the payout problem → the payout section; "Your payout setup is done: $X on its way." → the top. The bell's one bank-confirmation line opens the dashboard's "Your bank wants you to confirm" box, whose rows open a renewal's own row here (highlighted) or a test's piece on its test page (Developer dashboard).

## Call page

Every call happens in the app's own call room, on this page: video and sound go straight between the two browsers, and the app only passes along the short messages that connect them. Nothing in the call is recorded. There's no outside video service and no link to share.

- **Who and when.** Only the booked tester and the test's developer can be in the room, from 10 minutes before the start until the call ends. Anyone else, and anyone after the call ended, reads "This call isn't available." The server checks every request the room makes, not only the page load. Like other pages, it's closed to a restricted account (it moves them to the dashboard top).
- **Getting in.** Join call (wherever the call is listed, from 10 minutes before the start to 30 minutes after the booked end) opens this page in a new tab, and the room starts at once. Opened earlier, the page reads "You can join from <time>." with "Check your call setup" under it, and Join appears when the room opens.
- **Top:** "Call with Ana", then "“<test>” · <time> · booked for 60 minutes (paid up to 90) · the length is measured while you're both connected."
- **The room.** Two pictures: the other person's, with their name and a muted mark ("Sharing their screen" while they share; without a picture: "Not here yet", "No camera", "Camera off" or "Connecting…"), and your own ("You", "You're sharing your screen"). The browser asks for the camera and microphone; a call works with the microphone alone. Buttons: **Mute** / **Unmute**, **Camera off** / **Camera on**, **Share screen** (the screen takes the camera's place until sharing stops) and **Leave**. The other person sees each change. When either person comes back into the room, a fresh connection starts.
- **The status line** under the pictures: "Starting your camera…", "Waiting for Ana…", "Connecting…", "Connected", "Ana lost connection…", "Ana left the call", or "Allow your camera or microphone to join (check the browser's permission prompt).", always followed by "· measured so far: N min".
- **Leave and Rejoin.** Leave (or closing the tab) hangs up, and the other person reads "<name> left the call". The page then shows "You left the call", "Measured so far: N minutes with both of you connected.", **Rejoin** and **Back to the test**. Rejoin works until the call ends.
- **Measuring.** The length is the time both browsers were actually connected: each browser tells the app when it connects and disconnects. A gap under 5 minutes (a reload, a brief drop) still counts as connected; being in the room without a connection counts nothing. It's counted in whole minutes, up to 150% of the booked length and up to the $1,000 for that tester's calls on this test (Rules: Money). It's final when the server ends the call, when both have left, or 30 minutes after the latest allowed end (the start plus 150% of the booked length). A call nobody joined by halfway is cancelled: "Nobody joined, so the call was cancelled."
- **Strict networks.** On a few strict networks (some offices, some mobile carriers) the two browsers can't connect directly. The room then says "Couldn't connect directly (a strict network?). Try another network, or reschedule." and nothing is measured. Once both have left, the call is cancelled with nothing charged, and both are told "It couldn't connect, so the call was cancelled." An optional relay fixes this: the server settings TURN_URL, TURN_USERNAME and TURN_CREDENTIAL (for example a free-tier TURN service); without them, calls connect only directly.
- **The server ends a call for real.** Both rooms hang up at once and show "Call ended: <reason>", and the room can't be rejoined (a later Join reads "This call is over."). The minutes measured up to that moment are kept and paid as usual (listed: Pay, or the automatic charge 7 days after the call; not listed: Pay), and calls ahead are cancelled as usual. The reasons, each person reading their own line:

  | What happened | Line |
  |---|---|
  | 150% of the booked length is reached | "Call ended: it reached 150% of the booked time." |
  | Its pay at the call's rate (the contract rate for a listed call, $200/h otherwise) reaches what's left of the $1,000 for that tester's calls | "Call ended: the $1,000 for calls with <other person> on this test is reached." |
  | The test is paused (by the team, the developer's payment or chargeback hold, or a suspension of the developer side) | "Call ended: the test is paused." |
  | The test is stopped (by the developer or the team) or closed | "Call ended: the test was stopped." |
  | A block, either way (it ends a call in progress between them at once, whether or not they were connected yet) | the person who blocked: "Call ended: you blocked <name>."; the other: "Call ended: the call was cancelled." (never that they were blocked) |
  | The tester leaves the test | tester: "Call ended: you left this test."; developer: "Call ended: they left the test." |
  | End early for that tester | tester: "Call ended: your session ended."; developer: "Call ended: their session ended." |
  | The tester side is suspended, or the tester deletes their account | tester: "Call ended: your session ended."; developer: "Call ended: they left the test." |

  The lines are neutral: a tester never reads hold, suspended or chargeback about a developer.
- **A warning before a planned end.** 2 minutes before the call reaches 150% or the $1,000, while both are connected, both rooms show "This call ends at 3:42 PM.", in each reader's own time.
- **After the call.** Once the length is final in any other way: "Call ended at <time>." (or "This call is over." when nothing was measured).
- **Beside Join call** on the test page and the session page: the call clock ("starts in N min", then "N of M covered minutes"), which says near the end "The call ends on its own once you've been connected for N minutes (150% of the booked time)."; and who is in the room, "Ana joined the call room at 3:01" or "Ana hasn't joined yet". After the call: "Call room: Ana joined 3:00 · Nora joined 3:05 · both in from 3:05".
- **Join call refusals**, under the button as "Your Join call didn't go through: …": the call was cancelled, "This call isn't available.", "This call was reported as a no-show.", "This test is paused.", "This test was stopped.", "You left this test." / "They left the test.", "Your session ended." / "Their session ended.", "You can join from <time>.", "This call is over."
- **Check your call setup.** From the moment a call is booked, on each call ahead in both Live session panels and both dashboards' Calls boxes, and on this page before Join: "Check your call setup" with the line "Preview your camera and microphone before the call. Nothing is sent or recorded." It opens a small panel in place: your camera's picture and a microphone level bar ("No camera found" / "No microphone found" when there's none), and Done. If the browser's permission is refused: "Allow your camera and microphone in the browser's permission prompt to check them." It never makes a call, never sends anything to the server and is never measured. The 15-minute reminder opens the call's row, next to it.
- Nobody enters or changes a length. The developer pays afterwards on Developer test page.

  [video: The developer (left) and Ana (right, phone) in the call room: both pictures, "Connected · measured so far: 3 min", Mute, Camera off, Share screen and Leave.]

## Administrator console

Developers and Testers are separate role-filtered lists reached from the admin bar; both search by name or email. Admin access checks protect both pages. Updated fields show Before and Now in separate scroll boxes of about eight lines; listing history also scrolls each before/after value separately.

### What it's for and who sees it

Admins see every account (deleted ones too), act on suspensions, tests, holds and disputes, and every action is recorded with the admin who did it. Admin views name that admin by email ("stopped by ana@…"), or "an admin (deleted)" once that account is gone. Only admins see it; developers and testers see nothing of it, and always read "the team".

From Every page: the bell (admin lines and the "Reported more than 3 times" label) and the admin summary email, which follows each admin's own email setting.

### What's on it (top to bottom)

An optional Opened from a link row shows the item a bell line or email named. The console has these twelve sections, in this order; there are no stat tiles or general Accounts, Tests, Latest payments, Decided disputes, payout-waiting or Needs the team today lists. Needs the team today remains in the team email.

- **Chargeback holds:** "Chargeback hold: card verified ✓/✗, owed reserved ✓/✗" with what is owed and reserved ("($28.00 owed, $0.00 reserved)" before Pay what you owe; then "($23.00 reserved)", or, when something was added after the reservation, "($23.00 reserved · $5.00 added since (charged after the hold lifts))"), **Approve** (enabled once both are ✓; something added since never holds it back), and, while something is reserved, **Don't approve: release the reservation** with an optional "Why (optional)" note. When the developer's last reservation lapsed: "Reservation expired".

- **Your actions:** tests this admin paused or stopped, and accounts they suspended (per side) or deleted, newest first.

- **Tests you paused:** only tests this admin paused that are still paused, with Lift the pause.

- **Suspensions:** active suspended sides, with Reinstate.

- **Outages:** first, each possible outage the app's own heartbeat found, oldest first, in amber, in the admin's own time: "Possible outage: no checks from 3:10 PM to 3:42 PM (32 min). Nothing has moved." with **Approve: move every timer** and **Dismiss** (Buttons, below); these never move anything by themselves (Every page: Outages and phones). Then, while the uptime service says the site is down, "The uptime service says the site has been down since <time>. The outage is recorded when it says the site is up again (if it lasted 15 minutes or more)."; then each recorded outage with its length (from the alerts, the up alert's own length, or an approved possible outage), overlapping reports shown as one outage. One over 24 hours, or the part of a report that would bring an applied outage over 24 hours, moves nothing until an admin presses "Approve: move every timer".

- **Open disputes**, newest first, under the line "Newest first. A decision can only raise the pay, up to the piece's cap (the same as for Pay more), less what the piece was already paid. The extra is charged to the developer. Stripe card disputes are handled by hand in the Stripe dashboard." Each open row shows the listing ("Listing $X" or "Not listed") and what the Pay was, then the range a decision can use: "$20.00 paid · can raise up to $990.00 more" (what the piece was paid, Pay and every Pay more, and what's left of its cap). Each row (open or decided) has a read-only fold, **"Their chat on this test (N)"**: every message between that tester and the developer on this test, with who sent it and when, newest last, in a scroll box ("No messages." when there are none), so an agreement made in chat can be checked. This is the only place an admin can read a chat: not from profiles, reports, the test page or anywhere else, and there's no read log. Each row also has a fold, "See the work", showing the disputed piece exactly as the developer's piece view shows it: written answers under the questions they answered (with "Sent for the questions until <date>" when relevant) in the scroll box, attachments to download, recordings that play, and for a call its time, length and rate. When the developer adds their side, the row updates live: "Developer's side", marked "added <time>".

  [image: See the work: the dispute row opens the piece as the developer sees it, the answers under their questions in the scroll box (a 10,000-character answer here).]

- **Payment holds:** current holds, including More than 3 holds where relevant; no Approve here.

- **Reports:** who reported whom, the date, reason, profiles and label.

- **Lifts:** from a developer's 4th payment hold on, each hold appears here once they have paid: the developer, "More than 3 holds", what was owed, when it was paid, a note box and Approve.

- **No-show reports:** reports from calls.

- **Contact us** (each message, up to 50,000 characters, in a scroll box about 8 lines tall, with "Mark replied" and then who replied and when).

  [image: The tests list names the admin by email ("stopped by <email>"); developers and testers still read "the team".]

  [image: A 50,000-character Contact us message in a scroll box about 8 lines tall, with Mark replied.]

- **Settings:** configuration problems with a plain fix (owner only, or all admins when no owner is configured); email failures and storage problems for all admins, only while present. Waiting emails show the count, oldest time and short cause, including the daily limit or missing key. Retries run after 5, 15 and 30 minutes, then hourly for 48 hours. Failed emails remain stored; Clear this list hides them from the box.

Every list has its total, including (0), with no None row. Some sections keep their explanatory guidance even when empty. Lists scroll and load another 25 near the end; live arrivals do not move a scrolled list. Names link to profiles, with email addresses; tests link to their admin listing. Stripe references open the appropriate live or test dashboard in a new tab. Piece names in payment and sent-part rows are currently plain text; disputed work opens in See the work.

Open disputes identify the test, part and amount paid at the top. The tester's reason and the developer's paying note name and link their authors. The existing chat and See the work folds remain.

- **On profiles,** admins see the totals line (money spent or paid out and counts of reports, disputes, chargebacks, holds and suspensions), then **Suspend** (per side, with Preview), **Reinstate** per suspended side, **Delete account** and, for a developer, **Close all open reviews for $0**, exactly as on the Accounts list (Buttons, below), then extra boxes, newest first: payments, Payment failures, payouts, disputes, chargebacks, holds and suspensions (with the acting admin), tests posted or joined, and reports against them. Testers' profiles also list their sent parts with totals.

A developer's Payment failures box is newest first, naming the amount, recipient, part, test, failure time and short reason, and whether it was paid later, is still owed or was settled by the team. Profile admin boxes and the console follow other admins' changes live without losing a typed Why or side choice.

- **On a test's public page,** admins see three scroll boxes, Timeline, Contract changes and Payments, and **Close this test's open reviews for $0** (Buttons, below). The timeline speaks about people, never to them: every line names who did it, in the third person, e.g. "Nora Lane paid Ana Silva $10.00 for the written answers", "Nora Lane's tests are running again", "Nora Lane's tests are paused until they pay what they owe", "Nora Lane's developer account was paused (by ana@…)", "ana@… paused this test: <reason>", "ana@… lifted the pause on this test", "The developer stopped the test: “<reason>”", "Call with Ana Silva cancelled by Nora Lane". Each dispute is one line, "Ana Silva disputed the pay for their written answers:" with the tester's reason after it in italics, and each decision is one line naming the admin, "ana@… decided Ana Silva's dispute: kept the pay at $5.00". They reach any test, drafts too, from **Tests** in the admin header (Find tests). Admins don't get the developer's test page: the dispute fold is their view of the work, and the file links for attachments and recordings open for admins.

### Buttons

Admin buttons have no confirm pop-up; each shows "Done." or the refusal.

- **Decide a dispute:** the row shows the listing, the Pay, and the allowed range, "$20.00 paid · can raise up to $990.00 more": from what the piece was already paid (its Pay and every Pay more) up to the piece's own cap, the same cap Pay and Pay more have (Rules: Extra pay cap: the part's max in all, listed or not). Enter the total pay; blank keeps it. Any part the developer paid can be disputed ($0 included), unlisted ones too. A call's decision is also capped by what's left of the $1,000 for that tester's calls on the test. The server refuses a total outside the range: "A decision can only raise pay: at least the $X paid for this piece.", "At most $Y: the most this piece can be paid." (or "At most $Y: what's left of the $1,000 for this tester's calls."). A decision's raise counts toward the piece's cap like a Pay more. The "Their chat on this test" fold above it is there to check what the two agreed. The developer's side is optional: an admin can decide without it. If the side arrived after the admin's page loaded, Decide stops once: "<dev> added their side at 3:12 PM: read it, then press Decide again."; the second press goes through. The decision and note (up to 1,000 characters) are recorded; both sides are told ("…was decided"); a raise is charged at once; during a payment or chargeback hold it is added to what the developer owes and paid to the tester when they pay; during a suspension with no hold it is charged at once, and a decline starts a payment hold.
- **Suspend** (a reason is required, up to 1,000 characters), on the Accounts list and on the profile. It suspends one side of an account. An account with both roles gets a choice, "Developer side" or "Tester side"; a one-role account just shows Suspend. To suspend both, the admin suspends each side. The controls run top to bottom, full width (stacked on phones too): **Preview suspension** first, and its effects as a list under it (which tests pause, which calls are cancelled, who is affected, money waiting), then the side choice, then the "Why" box, then the Suspend button. The Preview's time is what the race check below uses.
  - the developer side: all their tests pause; applications wait and open invites close; calls within 3 days are cancelled with no fault; billing pauses; payments don't lift it; they get "Your developer account is paused". Their tester side works as normal.
  - the tester side: removed from every test (developers get "<name> left <test>"); pay for their sent work reaches their Stripe account as for anyone (Rules: Money); they get "Your tester account is paused". Their developer side works as normal.
- **Reinstate**, per suspended side: "Your developer account is active again" or "Your tester account is active again". A developer's tests resume, deadlines move on by the pause, and testers get "<test> is running again". A tester isn't re-invited: the tests they were removed from wait on their dashboard with Ask to rejoin, and the developer can invite them again.
- **Delete** an account, with a reason (see Every page).
- **Pause a test** (a reason is required, up to 1,000 characters): the developer's test page shows the paused view, where only Stop the test, End early and Cancel call work (Developer test page); it closes after 7 days. Testers follow the same paused-test rules as during a developer's hold (Rules), and chat stays open. The developer gets "The team paused “<test>”" (the reason is on the banner and in the email). **Lift the pause** (only on a test the team paused): "The team lifted the pause on <test>"; testers' deadlines and the test's Open until move later by the length of the pause. Admins don't lift a pause that comes from the developer's hold or suspension.
- **Stop a test:** for good; testers get "<test> was stopped", the developer "The team stopped “<test>”". The admin's Stop and the developer's Stop can't both run: the second is refused with "This test was already stopped."
- **Extend** a paused test's closing date (1–90 days, this pause only): "<test> will close later". **Close now:** testers read "<test> ended"; the developer gets "<test> was closed. <reason>".
- **Approve** a lift or a chargeback hold. A lift's Approve: the developer gets "The team approved lifting your hold", or "Your tests are running again" if that lifts it.
  - **A chargeback hold's Approve** takes the money the developer's Pay what you owe reserved on their card and lifts the hold in one step: the first press claims it, so it's taken once; the testers waiting on that money are paid then (or their share waits for their payout setup), and get "<test> is running again" when it runs again. The developer gets one notice (bell and email, even with email Off), opening their dashboard top: "The team approved lifting your hold: the $X reserved on your card was taken, and your tests are running again." (the email adds "Every deadline moved on by the pause."); with a suspension still on, "The team approved lifting your hold: the $X reserved on your card was taken. Your tests stay paused while your account is suspended." There's no separate "Your tests are running again". If the Approve doesn't lift the hold, the line is "The team approved lifting your hold" (the email adds "The $X reserved on your card was taken."). Anything added to what is owed after the reservation (a tester's Charge meanwhile) never holds it back: the Approve takes the reservation and lifts the hold as usual, and what was added is charged right after the lift as an ordinary charge (Payments). It's refused only while a step is missing: "Not yet: the card isn't verified.", "Not yet: nothing is reserved ($28.00 is owed).", or both at once ("Not yet: the card isn't verified, and nothing is reserved ($28.00 is owed)."). A reservation that can't be taken any more is released, and the press reads "The reservation can't be taken any more (it expired): the developer needs to press Pay what you owe again."
  - **Don't approve: release the reservation** (only while something is reserved; an optional note): everything reserved is released, nothing is taken, and the hold stays. The developer gets "The team didn't approve lifting your hold: the $X reserved on your card was released, and nothing was taken." (the note is in the email as "Their note: “…”"). Refused when nothing is reserved ("Nothing is reserved on that account.") or after an Approve ("That hold was already approved.").
- **Allow 3 more** for a test: every limited field at 0 gets 3 more changes (Edit test).
- **Close open reviews for $0** (abuse cases: spam testers, a fraudulent test, an abandoned account): "Close all open reviews for $0" in a developer's admin box (profile and Accounts list) and "Close this test's open reviews for $0" in a test's admin boxes. Top to bottom like Suspend: **Preview** first ("This closes 7 pieces from 4 testers on 3 tests at $0:" and the list, or "There are no open reviews to close."), then a required reason (5–1,000 characters; testers read it), then the button. It pays $0, with the team's reason as the note, on every piece still open: sent written answers and recordings not paid yet, listed or not, and calls that took place and aren't paid or charged yet; pieces already closed unpaid at their closing date are left out. Their Charge, Let it go, automatic call charge, reminders and Needs you rows go; nothing is charged. The race check: anything paid, charged or let go since the Preview is left alone, "5 closed; 2 were settled meanwhile". It's final: no dispute, no Pay more, no update. Testers get a payment line per piece and the reason on the session page; the developer gets one line (The bell) and a stop-once line in the test page's live box; the developer's account history, this admin's actions and each test's timeline record it.
- **Approve: move every timer** on a possible outage (Outages): it becomes an outage and every running timer moves later by it, whatever its length (the admin decided), with the usual "The site was down…" bell line. **Dismiss** drops it: nothing moves. A second press reads "That possible outage was already approved or dismissed."; an Approve over a time already recorded as an outage reads "An outage is already recorded from that time."
- **Free during launch** (Settings, read only): "Free during launch: free for everyone until <date>" (or "…ended on <date>", or "free for everyone, no end date"), with "Set by FREE_UNTIL in the server configuration (at least 30 days ahead). Every developer is told 30 days and 7 days before (even with email Off)." Admins can't change it in the console.

### What can stop a press

- Any admin can act on anything. If another admin changed the state after the page or Preview, a still-possible opposite action stops once, naming who acted and when; a second press proceeds. An action that is no longer possible is refused. For final decisions the first decision wins. A second admin's press is refused, naming the first admin: "ben@… already decided this dispute at 3:10 PM.", "This lift was already approved by …", "That account's developer side is already suspended (by ana@…).", "That account's tester side was already reinstated (by ana@…).", "That test was already stopped."
- **Lift the pause from an old row** (another admin lifted it, two pressed at once, or the test closed or isn't team-paused any more) is refused, and the row refreshes: "That test's pause was already lifted (by ana@… at 3:10 PM).", "That test's pause was already lifted when it closed at <date, time>.", "That test isn't paused by the team: it's paused by the developer's account state.", or "That test isn't paused."
- **The Preview check** (Accounts list and profiles): Suspend and Reinstate send when the admin's Preview, or else the page, was loaded. If another admin suspended or reinstated that side since, a still-possible action stops once, naming who and when, "Since your Preview, bo@… reinstated that account's developer side at Oct 28, 3:10 PM." ("Since this page loaded, …" without a Preview), and the account's state shown there updates. A deleted account refuses both: delete wins ("That account was deleted.", or when it happened after the admin's view, "That account was deleted. bo@… deleted it at Oct 28, 3:10 PM." / "…Its owner deleted it at …").
- Other refusals: "Record why (it is kept with the suspension).", "You can't pause your own account.", "Use Settings to delete your own account.", "A decision can only raise pay…".

### The "Reported more than 3 times" label

- Counts profile reports plus call no-show and late reports. It shows on names in dispute, report and lift rows and in the admin bell lines about them. Never the number: the exact count is only in the totals line on the profile. A report count is never proof by itself.

### Admin accounts

- Proper sign-in. An email in the admin list gets admin access only once that account's email is confirmed, so registering an admin's address first gives nothing. Signing up never makes an admin: admin comes only from the admin list with a confirmed email (or the admin flag).
- **An admin account is only an admin**, never a developer or a tester; to test as one, the admin makes a separate account. The first sign-in's "Welcome to Playtest" asks only Name (required) and Pronoun, with no "I'm here to", and Continue goes to the console (/admin); from then on an admin always lands there. There's no side switch, no "Start posting tests too" / "Start testing too" in Settings, and every developer or tester page (the dashboards, Post a test, Edit, test and session pages, Payments, the profile editor, Messages, chats and calls) moves an admin to /admin. Admins keep the admin views of profiles, Find tests and the test pages it opens, Settings, the bell list and Contact us. A side an admin account already has (local test data) is ignored, and nothing is deleted. A press to add a side is refused: "An admin account is only for the admin console. To post or test, use a separate account."
- An admin with either side suspended loses admin access: they sign in like any suspended account, and the console and every admin action refuse them with "Your account is paused."
- Contact us messages arrive by email to every admin, who reply by email.

### Bell lines and emails that open this page

- New dispute → that dispute's row; new report → that report's row; "Hold lift waiting…" → that Lifts row; each marked. They stack per kind ("Disputes · 3 new", "Reports · 2 new", "Hold lifts waiting · 1"). "N suspended account sides to review" → Accounts.
- The chargeback email (sent at once, whatever the admin's setting) → Chargeback holds; the admin summary's items → their rows; "Emails aren't sending (since <time>)" (or "(since <day> at <time>)") → the red emails box.

Holds, chargebacks and suspensions in full: Rules. What the developer sees: Developer test page.

## Rules

The rules several pages share, with every number, cap and timer. How pages show them is in each page tab and in Every page.

### Test lifecycle and timers

- **States:** Draft (a next round not opened yet; it opens in Post a test, not as a test page), Open, Joining closed (Open until passed; testers who joined keep working), Paused (an admin pause, or the developer's payment or chargeback hold or developer-side suspension; nothing new can start; records kept), Stopped, Closed (after a long pause).
- **Open until** is required: the last day to join, counted in the developer's own time zone. On Post, at least 3 and at most 30 days after today. On Edit it can only move later, only before it passes, and at most 30 days after the test opened (posted, or its current Next round opened), so tester days never pass 30; a test already past that keeps its date. Pause and outage shifts can still push it later than the cap. A Next round opens a fresh 30 days. Open until stays one date: it sets both the last day to join and the days each tester gets; there's no separate "days per tester" field. After it, the test is closed to everyone who hasn't joined: it leaves Find tests, the join page says "This test closed to new testers on <date>.", and waiting applications and open invites close (as when it fills up).
- **Every tester gets the same number of days.** The tester days run from when the test opened (posted, or a Next round opened) to Open until, in whole days. Each tester's deadline is the day they join plus those days; accepted invites and approved applications count from the moment they become a tester.
  - Example: a test posted Nov 6 with Open until Nov 20 gives 14 days per tester. A tester joining Nov 6 is due Nov 20; one joining Nov 19 is due Dec 3.
  - When Edit moves Open until later by some days, the tester days grow by the same number, and every working tester's deadline moves that many days later. Pause and outage shifts work as before.
  - Tests posted before this rule keep the deadlines their testers already have; their tester days (Open until minus when they opened) apply to new joins only.
  - A deadline is a guide: late work is accepted and labelled "Sent late (deadline was <date>)", and work isn't closed at the deadline. Late listed work never opens Charge or Let it go: only the developer's own Pay settles it, like unlisted work, or it closes unpaid at its closing date (Close-out). An update of a late piece stays late; an update sent after the deadline makes the piece late. Listed calls are unchanged (charged automatically 7 days after them).
- **After Open until** the test is done when the last working tester's deadline passes, or when everyone has finished. Stop and End early work as usual. Once joining has closed, it no longer counts toward the open tests (DEFAULT).
- **The review date** of a piece of sent work is the later of that tester's own deadline and 7 days after sending, and it stays so after a stop, End early, Leave, a block or a removal: Charge doesn't open 7 days after the work ended (the owner said no, Oct 29). So the developer always has at least a week (DEFAULT: the tester's own deadline replaced the shared Open until here).
