A listed call has its own date instead: 7 days after the call, when it's charged automatically if it isn't paid (Money); it moves later with pauses and outages.
- **Review reminders** count down to the review date, the same for listed and unlisted parts: 1 month before (only if the review date was more than a month away when the work was sent), then 3 weeks, 2 weeks and 1 week before, 3 days before and 1 day before. Only marks still ahead when the work is sent are used, and a mark within 24 hours of sending is skipped, because the "<tester> sent work" line that day counts as the first.
  - Example: a review date 25 days away gives 3 weeks, 2 weeks, 1 week, 3 days and 1 day. A review date a week after sending gives the sent line, then 3 days, then 1 day.
  - After the review date a listed part opens Charge, and the developer's one daily "Pay now" line, across all their tests, covers it until it's paid, charged or let go: "Pay now: Ana's written answers on “Harbor Run” ($10.00): Ana can charge or let it go (until Nov 18)", or "Pay now: 3 pieces on 2 tests: their testers can charge or let them go (the first closes Nov 18)". A piece the tester can't Charge (they deleted their account, or their Charge closed) keeps its own daily line, "“Harbor Run”: Ana Silva's written answers weren't reviewed by Oct 30: pay it." (the bell table on Every page has every form). The tester gets their own one daily line, "Charge or let go: …", from the day Charge opens. An unlisted part doesn't open Charge: its reminders stop, and its Needs you row (Pay only) stays until it's paid, even at $0. Listed work sent late is the same: its reminders end "(it was sent late: any amount, $0 included)", stop at the review date, and it's never in "Pay now".
  - A listed call gets two reminders before its automatic charge, 3 days and 1 day before it (only those still ahead): "“Harbor Run”: pay Ana Silva's call on <time> within 3 days." / "…by tomorrow."
  - Calls that aren't listed never get these reminders. Instead the developer gets one weekly line, stacked across tests, "Pay your calls: 3 calls to pay on 2 tests.", until each is paid ($0 counts). They stay on the test until paid, with no end date and no Charge.
- **Charge opens** on unpaid contract work (written answers or a recording) sent by the tester's own deadline, at its review date. A stopped or closed test still waits for it. Rating, the "Charge or cancel" box and the refusals all use this same point. Nothing is charged automatically, except a listed call 7 days after it (Money: Listed calls are charged automatically). After a developer deletes their account, work still under review never opens Charge. Charge closes at the piece's close-out (below).
- **Paused tests close after 7 days**, whatever the cause, unless an admin presses Extend or Close now. A paused test has one closing clock: 7 days from when it was first paused. A second cause starting doesn't restart or extend it, and neither does one cause lifting while another remains. The card shows every reason, e.g. "Paused: payment hold · paused by the team", and the daily paused reminder uses that one date. Closing works like a Stop: testers read "ended" with no reason, and the developer gets the explanation by bell and a banner. Drafts never close.
- **Any pause is treated the same** on the developer's test page, whatever its cause (the team, a payment or chargeback hold, a developer-side suspension): only Stop the test, End early and Cancel call work, plus excerpts and the side of a dispute; they follow the same stop-once rules as on a running test, and a page opened before the pause stops once for it (Stopped tests on Every page).
- **Pausing and resuming** moves every deadline and timer on by the length of the pause. When any pause lifts (a hold is paid off, an account is reinstated, or the team lifts its pause), every working tester's deadline and the test's Open until move later by that length.
- **Outages:** with outage checks on, an outage that lasted 15 minutes or more moves every running timer later and cancels calls inside it with no fault. It's found by the uptime service's alerts (a down then an up, or an up alone, from the length it carries), with the alert's own span; reports of the same downtime merge, so it's counted once. A gap of 15 minutes or more with no server activity at all is never applied on its own: it's listed for the admins as a possible outage, with Approve or Dismiss (Every page: Outages and phones). Over 24 hours, a reported outage waits for an admin's Approve.
- **When timed jobs run.** The time-sensitive ones run every 5 minutes, from the uptime service's check of the site (with its key), and also when pages load and poll: first, the app's own heartbeat (any server activity counts: page loads, API calls, this run, the daily run and the server starting, checked at most once a minute; a gap of 15 minutes or more is listed for the admins as a possible outage and moves nothing by itself: Every page, Outages and phones), then emails that failed and are due another try (Every page: Emails) and chargeback reservations past their 7 days (Rules: Money); then the reminder 15 minutes before each call, calls the server ends (Call page) and calls nobody joined or that never connected, measured call lengths becoming final, Charge opening, the daily "Pay now" and "Charge or let go" lines, the automatic charge of listed calls 7 days after them, the closing-soon reminders, the developer's review reminders and the rating reminders. A run never overlaps another, and a problem in it never makes the site look down or records an outage. The daily run also sends again any tester's share still waiting once their payout setup is done (Payments). Without the key, the check only reports whether the site is up. The rest stays once a day (9:00 UTC): each tester's own deadline reminders, the Open until heads-ups, invite reminders, the paused-test reminder and closing tests paused for 7 days, the weekly "Pay your calls", bank confirmations expiring after 3 days, retries of what is owed during a payment hold, subscription renewals and the free-during-launch notices, the payout reminder, the admins' daily email, applications and invites closing after Open until, the next Forever calls, checking payments that never answered (also on page loads) and the summary emails. The daily run does the time-sensitive jobs too.

### Close-out

Each test gets one **close-out date: 14 days after the last tester's deadline has passed** (no earlier than 14 days after Open until, or after the stop for a stopped or closed test). Like deadlines, it moves later with pauses (the team's, or the developer's hold or suspension) and outages. Until the last deadline has passed, the date isn't known and nothing below applies.

- **Each piece gets at least 14 days of Charge.** A piece (a sent part, or a listed call that took place, though a listed call is charged automatically long before: Money) closes at the close-out, or 14 days after its own Charge opened (its review date) if that's later. On its closing date the tester's Charge and Let it go and the developer's Pay close. Until then the developer can still Pay it after its Charge opened (whichever comes first settles it). A tester whose tester side was suspended gets the suspended time added to their pieces' Charge windows, so a suspension never eats them.
- **A piece that closes unpaid stays unpaid.** The developer's row leaves Needs you (and Needs review), and the piece reads "Closed on <date>: not paid."; the tester's part reads "Not paid (closed on <date>)" and leaves Charge or cancel. A press after it: "Pay closed on <date>." / "Charge and Let it go closed on <date>."
- **Pay more closes together with the last Pay / Charge on the test:** on the close-out date, or, if a piece's Charge window runs later, on that later date. One date for the test (a Next round has its own). After it: "Pay more closed on <date>."
- **Testers can no longer rate** after the close-out: "Rating closed on <date>." A developer can rate a tester who never rated for 14 days from the close-out (or from when that tester's last open piece settled, if later), only if everything that tester sent was settled; if anything closed unpaid, there's no rating of that tester (Ratings, below).
- **Disputes are unchanged:** a dispute filed before its piece closed is still decided, and a decision that raises pay still charges the developer.
- **The last 7 days** (DEFAULT): the Charge box says "Charge closes on <date>.", Needs you rows and the dashboard's Needs review "Pay closes on <date>.", and the Pay more box "Pay more closes on <date>.", each by its own date.
- **Reminders** (owner, Oct 29): the developer gets "Closing soon" lines counting to each unpaid piece's closing date: 1 month before (only if that date was more than a month away when the piece's Charge opened), 1 week, 3 days and 1 day before, only the marks still ahead, never two for the same mark, moved with pauses and outages. One line a day covers every piece reaching a mark that day, across all their tests: "Closing soon: Ana's written answers on “Harbor Run” close on Nov 18" / "Closing soon: 2 pieces close by Nov 18 (Ana's written answers on “Harbor Run”, Ben's recording on “Sky Lab”)", opening Needs you on the dashboard. It's kept alongside "Pay now". The tester's side is their one daily "Charge or let go: …" line, every day they have a piece open to Charge or Let it go, which names the first closing date. Both are money lines, emailed whatever the setting. The developer's wait while the test is paused; the tester's waits while their tester side is suspended.

### Money

- **Pay:** one box per piece, with one green Pay button and no Reject. The developer pays any amount from $0 up. It starts at the listing (or, for a call, the rate × its measured length); for an unlisted part it starts empty. After a piece is paid, its row keeps the same box as "Pay more" until the cap is reached, and at most until Pay more closes (Close-out). Every Pay box has a note, "Note to <tester> (optional)", up to 1,000 characters. It becomes required at $0 ("Say why you're paying $0 (the tester reads it).") and when a listed part is paid less than the listing ("Say why you're paying less than the listing's $X (the tester reads it)."). The tester reads the note with the payment on their session page and in the email, never in the bell line. Beside the amount, the room left within the piece's cap ("up to $1,000.00 in all" / "up to $990.00 more ($1,000.00 in all)" / an unlisted call's "up to $X ($200/h × 29 min)" / what's left of the $1,000 for calls); an amount above it is refused in red at the box, live and again on the server. The card is charged at once and nothing shows success before the result. The split is 80% to the tester, 20% to the platform.
- **A $0 payment** closes the row like any payment, listed or unlisted. The tester reads "Paid $0: "<reason>"" and can dispute it once. For a listed part, a $0 payment is a review, so Charge and Let it go don't open for it.
- **An update after paying** (owner, Oct 29): the tester can send a paid part again (written answers or a recording). Below the piece's cap it's a Needs you row with Pay more on the same piece (any amount up to the cap; $0 closes it with an optional note); no new piece, no Charge, no review reminders; the row leaves once Pay more is pressed or closes. At the cap (or after Pay more closed) there's no row, only "Updated after you paid · already paid the most" on the tester's row and a quiet live-box line. An update never reopens a dispute.
- **The team's $0 closure** (owner, Oct 29): for abuse cases the team can pay every open piece of a developer, or of one test, at $0 with a reason the testers read (Administrator console). Nothing is charged, and it's final: no dispute, no Pay more, no update.
- **Old rejections:** Reject no longer exists. Work rejected before it was removed still shows "Rejected" with its reason and can still be disputed once.
- **When Pay works:** until the tester presses Charge or Let it go, or the piece closes (Close-out), including after Open until, after a Stop, after a pause closure, and after the tester deleted their account (Pay reaches their Stripe account if their payout account was connected; if not, those rows leave Needs you with "<name> deleted their account before setting up payouts, so this can't be paid.", a DEFAULT). Never during a hold or a developer-side suspension.
- **Over $200:** setting a part price over $200 needs "I confirm this part pays more than $200." Every Pay and Pay more above $0 needs the tick "I understand this payment of $X can't be refunded and is charged now.", naming the exact amount (a changed amount unticks it); over $200 the tick "I confirm this payment is over $200." is needed too. Paying $0 needs no tick. There is no 3D Secure check on Pay. A tester's Charge needs a tick only above $200 (Tester session page). Every amount is rounded to the cent (Every page).
- **Any declined payment starts a payment hold at once:** a part Pay, the tester's Charge, a call charge (a listed call's automatic charge too), extra pay, or something charged right after a chargeback hold lifted. Only a real card decline counts: any other problem with the payment service fails the press with "Payments are having a problem right now. Nothing was charged; try again shortly.", with no hold and nothing owed. There are no grace days, but what is owed is retried during a payment hold (below). A bank asking to confirm a payment isn't a decline (below). A declined subscription renewal is the one exception: it lapses with no hold (Plans and the subscription, below). More declines during the hold add to "Pay what you owe". Paying it all lifts the hold at once (from the 4th hold on, after an admin's Approve).
- **A payment that never answered** (the payment service didn't reply) is never a decline. After 15 minutes it's checked, by the daily job and whenever the developer's test page, Payments or the tester's session page loads: if it went through, it's finished as normal; if the bank is still confirming, it's left for the bank's timers; if it failed or can't be found, the payment is cleared and the piece is back in Needs you, with no bell. A new press on that piece within 24 hours reuses the same one-time key, so it's never charged twice.
- **Chargebacks are owed by the developer.** The chargeback's amount and its $15 fee are added to what they owe, and they pay it like any other debt; the platform takes the loss only if they never pay. A chargeback starts a chargeback hold with three steps, in this order: verify a card with 3D Secure, reserve what is owed (only by pressing Pay what you owe, refused until the card is verified), then an admin's Approve. During a chargeback hold Pay what you owe doesn't charge: it reserves the owed total on the card (an authorization the bank holds; Stripe shows it "Uncaptured"), with the bank's check inline if it asks. The Approve takes the reserved money and lifts the hold in one step, and the testers waiting on it are paid then. Pay what you owe reserves once: anything added to what is owed after the reservation (a tester's Charge meanwhile) is never reserved and never holds the Approve back; it's charged right after the lift on the verified card, as an ordinary charge with its own receipt (the bank asking: "Your bank wants you to confirm", 3 days; declined: a normal payment hold), and the tester is paid when it goes through. Before the Approve, Payments says "Reserved $X: it's taken when the team approves lifting your hold. $Y added since will be charged right after." and the admin's row "$X reserved · $Y added since (charged after the hold lifts)". The team's Don't approve releases the reservation: nothing is taken and the hold stays. A reservation lasts about 7 days: if the Approve hasn't come by then, it's released and the developer is asked to press Pay what you owe again ("Your reservation expired: press Pay what you owe again so the team can approve it."); the admin's row says "Reservation expired". Whatever is still reserved when a hold lifts another way, or stops being a chargeback hold, is released too. Nothing is retried on its own during a chargeback hold, and reserving alone never lifts it: the hold lifts only at the Approve (Payments). Payment holds are different: Pay what you owe and the automatic retries charge at once, and paying lifts them. A chargeback during a payment hold turns it into a chargeback hold, and its retries stop from that moment. Every further chargeback adds to what is owed; a new chargeback clears an earlier card check. Each chargeback also adds a $15 chargeback fee ("Your bank reversed a $X payment: $X + $15 chargeback fee"). When the platform wins a chargeback, its amount and fee come off what is owed, or are refunded to the card if already paid (receipt: "Refund: your bank's chargeback of $X was reversed"); if that was all that was owed, the hold lifts as usual (from the 4th hold on, after Approve).
- **Hold count:** the first 3 holds lift as soon as the developer pays. From the 4th hold on, an admin must Approve the lift (Lifts on Administrator console); the developer's box says the team will review after they pay.
- **Charge cap:** the listing minus what was already paid for that part (written answers and recordings; calls have no Charge).
- **Listed calls are charged automatically** (owner, Oct 29; the way hourly platforms bill). For the week after a listed call (from its end; the date moves later with pauses and outages) the developer can Pay it themselves, any amount: below the measured length × the rate needs a reason the tester reads, and the tester can dispute it once. Reminders come 3 days and 1 day before, and the call's Pay box says "If you don't pay by <date>, it's charged automatically: $X (<N> min × $Y/h)."; the tester's call row says "If <dev> doesn't pay by <date>, it's charged automatically." Not paid by then: the app charges the developer's card for the measured length × the contract rate, less what's paid, within what's left of the $1,000 for that tester's calls, once per call (a second run charges nothing), as a payment made while they're away. The developer's receipt: "Receipt: you paid $X for Ana Silva's call on Oct 30 (charged automatically after 7 days)"; the tester's line: "<dev> paid you $X for your call on <date>". The bank asking: "Your bank wants you to confirm" (3 days, then it counts as declined). Declined: a payment hold, as for any declined charge. During a payment or chargeback hold it's added to what is owed ("<tester>'s call on “<test>” was charged automatically ($X): it was added to what you owe"); during a developer-side suspension with no hold it's charged when the account is clear. Whenever the money waits (owed, waiting for the account to clear, waiting for the bank, declined), the tester's call reads "Payment for this call may be delayed." and their bell says "Payment for your call on “<test>” may be delayed", never failed or hold. The developer's test page records what really happened: "<tester>'s call was charged automatically: $X", "…: $X, added to what you owe", "…: $X, charged when your account is clear", "…: $X, waiting for your bank to confirm it", or "The automatic charge for <tester>'s call ($X) was declined: it was added to what you owe". The date on the developer's Pay box is in their own saved time zone, and the tester's line shows only while that date is ahead. A developer who deleted their account is charged too, on their saved card (declined: delayed for the tester, listed for the admins, no hold); with no saved card the call stays unpaid ("<dev> deleted their account, so this can't be paid."). On a test the team paused it waits. Nothing to charge (no measured time, or the $1,000 used): the call closes with nothing to pay. There's no Charge or Let it go for a listed call anywhere. Unlisted calls are unchanged: any amount ($0 included), the weekly "Pay your calls" line, no automatic charge and no end date. Listed calls whose Charge had opened before this rule switched to the automatic charge.
- **Charge during a pause:** during a payment or chargeback hold, a tester's Charge (and a dispute decided for the tester) goes through as their choice but isn't charged yet: it is added to the developer's "Pay what you owe", and the tester is paid when the developer pays (on a chargeback hold, at the team's Approve, which takes what Pay what you owe reserved, or, for what was added after the reservation, right after the lift). The tester sees "Charged $X. Payment may be delayed until the test resumes." A listed call's automatic charge during a hold is added to what is owed the same way. During a developer-side suspension with no hold, a Charge, or a dispute decided for the tester, is charged at once; if it's declined, a payment hold starts and the amount is added to what is owed.
- **Dispute range:** from what this piece was already paid (its Pay and every Pay more) up to the piece's own cap, the same cap Pay and Pay more have (Extra pay cap, below: the part's max in all, listed or not), and for a call also within what's left of the $1,000 for that tester's calls. So a recording listed at $10 and paid $20 can still be disputed, because a tester and developer may have agreed more in chat. A decision's raise counts toward the cap. An admin decision can only raise pay. To decide, the team can read that tester's and developer's chat on the test, in the dispute row only (Administrator console). Any part the developer paid can be disputed, $0 included, unlisted parts too, and older rejections; a part the tester settled with Charge or Let it go can't. Each part can be disputed once; the developer adds their side once, if they want (it's optional).
- **Extra pay cap: a piece's cap is the part's max in all** (owner, Oct 29), counted per piece across its Pay, every Pay more and any dispute raise, listed or not: $1,000 for written answers or a recording; $200 an hour × the measured length for a call, which also stays within what's left of the $1,000 for that tester's calls. So a $20 written listing can be paid up to $1,000 in all ($980 above the listing), a listing at the max leaves no Pay more room, an unlisted written part or recording is $1,000 in all, and a 30-minute call (listed or not) is at most $100 in all. Once a piece reaches its cap, the developer reads "Paid the most this piece can be paid ($X)." where Pay more was, and the tester "This part is already paid the most it can be ($X)." (Developer test page, Tester session page). Blocked: "This is more than this piece can be paid (up to $1,000)." / "This is more than this piece can be paid (up to $990 more, $1,000 in all)." (an unlisted call: "This is more than you can pay for this call (up to $X at $200 an hour).").
- **One $1,000 for all of a tester's calls on a test**, listed and unlisted together, with extra pay counted. Used = everything paid for that tester's calls on the test (Pay, Pay more, dispute decisions, Charge), plus each booked call not yet paid at its reference (rate × booked length; for calls that aren't listed, $200/h × booked length).
  - Booking: a request, an offer or an Accept that would take Used over $1,000 is refused on both sides: "Calls with Ana on this test can come to at most $1,000; $X is left, so this call can't be booked." Waiting requests over the limit can't be accepted. The longest call that can be booked is the smaller of 180 minutes and the whole minutes left at the call's rate (Developer test page, Request a time).
  - During a call: it ends when its pay at the call's rate reaches what's left, and the measured length is capped there (Call page).
  - Paying: every call's Pay and Pay more "up to" is also limited by what's left, and so are a listed call's automatic charge and dispute decisions.
  - A small grey line, never a stop, shows wherever calls are requested, offered, accepted or paid, once what's left is under one hour at that tester's listed rate (or under $200 for calls that aren't listed): "$X left of the $1,000 for calls with Ana on this test." (developer) / "$X left of the $1,000 for your calls on this test." (tester).
- **Calls that aren't a listed part:** either side can request a call even when a live call isn't a listed part of the test or isn't in that tester's contract. A call or any other part that isn't listed in the tester's contract carries the label "Not listed" on both sides. Pay for such a call is up to the developer: at most $200 an hour for its measured length, with no extra, within the $1,000 for that tester's calls. The Pay box and its rate start empty. Charge and Let it go never open for a call outside the tester's contract.
- **A call's length is measured** by the app: the time both browsers were connected, in whole minutes, up to 150% of the booked length and up to the moment its pay reaches what's left of the $1,000 (gaps under 5 minutes count as still connected; Call page). It's final when the server ends the call, when both have left, or 30 minutes after the latest allowed end. Nobody types or changes a length: the developer just pays, and paying less than the measured length × the rate needs the usual reason. Calls logged before measuring keep their logged minutes, and lengths lowered before this rule keep their recorded length. With no connected time at all, the call is cancelled with nothing charged ("nobody joined", or "couldn't connect" when both were in the room).
- **Paying for a call:** the call's Pay box has "Rate ($/h)" and the amount, which fill each other: a rate fills the amount (rate × length, to the cent), an amount fills the rate (amount ÷ length × 60, to the cent per hour). The one typed last wins, and the server works out the other and checks the caps (per hour and in total). A listed call's rate starts at the contract rate; an unlisted call's starts empty and can't go over $200/h. A listed call is paid and, if the developer hasn't paid it 7 days after it, charged automatically at the measured length × the contract rate. The tester sees the amount and, when one was typed, the rate.
- **Unlisted parts in Needs you** have Pay only, any amount, $0 included. Paying $0 closes the row; the tester reads "Paid $0: "<reason>"". Charge or Let it go never opens for them. They get the same review reminders as listed work until the review date, then no more; the row stays until it's paid, even $0.
- **Your bank wants to confirm:** a bank's request to confirm a payment isn't a decline. On the developer's own press (Pay and Pay more on the test page; Pay what you owe, Subscribe and "Verify a card" on Payments) the stop-once check runs first, then the bank's check opens **inline, directly under the button that was pressed**, not as a pop-up: confirmed, it goes through; cancelled or failed, "Your bank didn't confirm the payment, so nothing was charged." and no hold. The result shows under the button. A cancelled or failed check frees the press: the next Pay, Pay more, Pay what you owe, Subscribe or Verify a card on the same thing is a new attempt with its own one-time key (and its own bank check), so it goes through normally instead of failing; a double press of one attempt is still charged once. While the check is open the button stays disabled and the piece is locked: a tester's Charge or Let it go on it is refused ("A payment for this is being confirmed right now.") until the bank confirms, refuses or times out (the check is closed, or after 15 minutes). A charge made while the developer isn't there (a tester's Charge, a listed call's automatic charge, a dispute decision, an automatic retry, something charged right after a chargeback hold lifted, a renewal) waits as "needs your confirmation" for 3 days, or it counts as declined (a payment hold for a charge, a lapse for a renewal), and the tester sees "Payment may be delayed". One on a test is confirmed on that test page, under the affected piece, with **Confirm with your bank**, which runs the stop-once check first, exactly like Pay on that piece, and then opens the same inline check; its refusals stay plain ("You already confirmed this payment.", "This confirmation expired: nothing was charged.", "This payment was cancelled: nothing was charged."). Payments and the dashboard's box link straight to it; the bell's one line for every waiting charge opens that box. A renewal is confirmed on Payments: a renewal the bank wants confirmed isn't made past due meanwhile, it just waits for the Confirm, and Confirm with your bank fetches the bank's own page for it, shown inline like a press's. A Subscribe whose first payment the bank wants confirmed shows the check inline under Subscribe; cancelled or failed, the new subscription is cancelled and nothing is charged. A Confirm pressed twice counts once. A piece is never charged twice.
  - The fake payment service used in tests and recordings draws a clearly marked stand-in in the same inline place ("Stand-in for your bank's check", "Your bank's own page shows here when payments are live. Nothing is charged until it's confirmed.", Confirm / Cancel), so recordings show the step.
- **Retries during a payment hold:** while a payment hold lasts, what is owed is retried on the saved card once a day, at most 7 tries per hold, and at once when a new card is saved. A success pays the lines it covers (open pages stop once) and the developer gets one line, in Payments and holds, naming what was paid: "We charged your card $10.00 for what you owed: Ana Silva's call on Oct 30." ("…: Ana Silva's call on Oct 30 and Ben Okafor's written answers"; more than 3: the first 3, then "and 2 more"). There's no separate receipt line: the same line goes in the payment email, which is the receipt. A retry the bank wants confirmed sends no line until the bank confirms; then its one receipt names the items ("Receipt: you paid $10.00 for what you owed: Ana Silva's call on Oct 30"). With nothing left owed the hold lifts as if they had pressed Pay (from the 4th hold on, after Approve). A failed retry sends no bell; Payments shows "Last try: <date> · next try: <date>", or "No more automatic tries: press Pay what you owe". **Never during a chargeback hold**, including one that started as a payment hold: neither the daily job nor saving or verifying a card charges anything, and there's no "next try" line.
- **Paying while suspended:** a payment never lifts an admin suspension. When what is owed is paid (or a chargeback hold lifts at the team's Approve) while a developer-side suspension continues, the developer gets "Your payment went through. Your tests stay paused while your account is suspended." (bell and email, even with email Off); "Your tests are running again" goes out only when nothing pauses their tests any more.
- **Pay what you owe** updates live (about every 20 seconds), and the server refuses it when nothing is owed, so a payment from another tab is never charged twice. It has no note.
- **A deleted developer's card:** parts already open to Charge when they deleted stay open and charge their saved card, and so do a dispute decided for the tester and a listed call's automatic charge (with no saved card, that call can't be paid). A decline leaves the payment delayed for the tester and listed for admins; there's no account left to hold, and the platform never covers it. What they owe stays owed.
- **Prices:** written and recording $1–$1,000 each, calls $10–$200 an hour, or free. The smallest charge is $0.50; Stripe's largest is $999,999.99.
- **Cards only.** Add a card and Change card save only a card or a card through Link, and the developer's own presses (Pay, Pay more, Pay what you owe and its reservation) accept exactly those two, so a card saved through Link works like any other (on Stripe's current API this is the payment's allowed payment method types: Security and launch, Money).
- **A suspended tester is paid like anyone:** pay for work they sent (Pay, Pay more, a Charge they made before, a dispute decided for them) goes to their Stripe account, or waits for their payout setup like anyone's.
- **Payouts** go to a tester's Stripe account once it can receive them (payouts and transfers both switched on), and payout setup needs a confirmed email. Pay that came before the setup was done waits, and goes out on its own when it's done (Payments). A share sent after its charge (one that waited for payout setup, or one paid from a chargeback hold's reservation at the team's Approve) is sent from that charge itself, so it never needs money in the platform's balance and arrives when that charge's money does.
- **Wording for testers:** "paused" and "delayed" only.

### Plans and the subscription

- **Free plan (every developer):** one open test at a time. A test counts as open while it is Open, Full or Paused; drafts, stopped and closed tests, and tests whose joining has closed (DEFAULT), don't count.
- **Subscription, $7 a month,** adds: up to 10 open tests at once (an 11th is refused: "You have 10 open tests, the most you can have at once. Stop or close one to post another."; the count is checked on the server at the press), Approved-only testers (applications), Next round, and Invite testers.
- Testers never pay and never see any of this. The 20% platform share on payments is unchanged.
- **Billing:** monthly from the sign-up day; a sign-up on the 31st renews on the last day of shorter months. Cancel any time: the subscription lasts until the end of the paid month, with no refunds. Each charge gets a receipt line on Payments.
- **On the free plan** the perk buttons stay visible with a small "Subscribers" tag, and a press is refused with "<Perk> needs a subscription ($7/month)." and a Payments link (new tab). The perks are named "Inviting testers", "Next round", "Approved-only testers" and "Running several open tests" (Post a test, Edit test, Developer test page).
- **Losing the subscription** (a cancel reaches its end, or a renewal lapses): running tests carry on as they are (an Approved-only test stays Approved only, and invites already sent stay valid); only new uses of a perk are refused. Using a perk on an existing test is not a new use: an Approved-only test keeps working fully, and the developer can still accept or decline its applications. A page open at that moment gets it as a stop-once change: "Your subscription ended."
- **A declined renewal lapses, with no hold.** The payment is retried for about a week, and the perks continue meanwhile. After the last retry fails, the subscription ends and the developer is on the free plan. Testers are never affected. A chargeback on a subscription payment starts a chargeback hold like any chargeback, and the amount is added to what is owed.
- **During a payment hold, a chargeback hold or a developer-side suspension** billing pauses: no renewal is charged. The paid days left resume when it lifts, so the next renewal moves later by the time it was paused. The developer can still cancel from Payments, and Keep my subscription still works because it charges nothing; Subscribe is refused: "You can subscribe once your account is active again."
- **Deleting the account**, or the team deleting it, ends the subscription at once with no refund.
- **Free during launch:** until the date set in the server configuration (FREE_UNTIL, an ISO date; unset now, which means free with no end), every developer has the perks without paying. A value that isn't a real date stops the deploy, so the old version stays live until it's fixed; a past date is fine and means free has ended. It can be set, changed or cleared at any time after launch. The daily job tells every developer 30 days and 7 days before that date, by bell and by email even with email Off. A developer who subscribes during the free period is first charged on that date. When the free period ends, it works like a lapse: running tests carry on, and only new uses of a perk are refused.

### Ratings

- 1–5 stars and an optional comment (up to 500 characters), shown without names. Only real work counts: some work sent or a call held.
- **The tester rates first.** Their Rate box shows once they sent some work or had a call. Its button is disabled, not hidden, with one short grey line naming the first of these that applies, and updates live as things are decided:
  - a part they can still Charge or Let it go: "Charge or let go of your unreviewed work first.";
  - a dispute of theirs is open: "Rating opens once the team decides your dispute.";
  - work they sent is still inside its review window (sent, not reviewed, Charge not open yet): "Rating opens on <date>, or sooner if <dev> reviews everything you sent." (the latest review date among those pieces; a call still being measured: "Rating opens once <dev> reviews everything you sent."). A part that isn't listed, or was sent late, stops blocking at its review date, since it never opens Charge.
  - The server refuses an old page's press with the same line. The tester's Rate has no end date until the test's close-out ("Rating closed on <date>.": Close-out). Rating ends their part; Dispute stays, so a later dispute can only be about a payment already made.
- **The developer rates after the tester and settlement.** The developer's Rate for a tester opens only once everything that tester sent is settled (paid, charged or let go; any dispute decided) and the tester has rated them. Until then their Rate box is hidden, and an old page's press reads "You can rate <name> once they've rated you and everything they sent is settled." Their 14 days start then, with the bell line "<tester> finished <test>. You can rate them for 14 days.". A tester who never rates is never rated by that developer, except after close-out (Close-out). A dispute opened later disables the developer's Rate, with "Rating opens once the team decides <name>'s dispute.", until it's decided. After the 14 days: "The 14 days to rate <name> have passed."
- **Blind reveal.** The tester's rating stays hidden from the developer, from profiles and from averages until the developer rates or their 14 days end; then both show together. If the developer's window hadn't opened within 14 days of the tester's rating (work left unsettled that long), the tester's rating shows 14 days after it was given.
- **Ratings to give** on both dashboards lists every rating the person can give right now, following all of these rules (Developer dashboard, Tester dashboard).
- **No rating during your own suspension.** While the developer side or the tester side is suspended, that side can't rate: the Rate box is hidden on its pages (the suspended tester's limited view has no Rate; neither has the developer's paused view while they're held or suspended), and an old page's press is refused: "You can rate once your account is active again." A developer on a payment or chargeback hold can't rate either. The developer's 14 days and reminders pause whenever they can't rate and continue afterwards; the tester's Rate has no end date, so only its reminders pause.
- **The other side's state never matters.** The other side can still rate a suspended or held person, with no notice and nothing stopping for it: a tester is never told a developer is suspended or on a hold. On a test the team paused (nobody suspended or held), both sides rate, and the pause never stops Rate (only Rate's own short list does); the developer's paused view has "Rate your testers".
- **What stops Rate once:** only changes about that person and your shared work (Why a press stops: Rate).
- **Reminders** (owner, Oct 29), whatever the test's state (running, paused, stopped or closed): the tester's "Rate <dev> on <test>" once, when their Rate opens, then "Reminder: rate <dev> on “<test>”" every 3 days for 2 weeks from then while nothing blocks their Rate, not counting time their tester side was suspended. The developer's "<tester> finished <test>. You can rate them for 14 days." once, then "Reminder: rate <tester> for “<test>” (until <date>)" every 3 days inside their 14 days, waiting while they can't rate (a hold or their own suspension). Every rating line stacks in one "Ratings to give" entry a day across tests and opens the dashboard's Ratings to give.

### Holds and suspensions

| | Payment hold | Chargeback hold | Developer side suspended | Tester side suspended |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Starts when | any payment is declined | a chargeback arrives | an admin suspends, with a reason | an admin suspends, with a reason |
| Their tests | all paused | all paused | all paused | they are removed from every test |
| They can | Pay what you owe, Change card, Payments, Contact us; on their tests: Stop the test, End early, Cancel call, excerpts, their side of a dispute | the three steps, Change card, Payments, Contact us; on their tests the same as a payment hold | Change card, Payments, Contact us (appeal); on their tests the same as a payment hold; their tester side works as normal | Payments (payout setup), Contact us (appeal), Dispute in the limited view of the tests they were removed from; their developer side works as normal |
| Testers in those tests can | save drafts, cancel booked calls, dispute, Charge or Let it go, Rate, Leave; read the chat | the same | the same | (not in any test) |
| Waits until it lifts | sending work, sending chat messages, new calls, joins, applications, Pay, the developer's Rate and its 14 days (open invites close; the developer can invite again after); testers' charges are paid when the developer pays what they owe | the same, except that testers' charges are paid at the team's Approve, when the reserved money is taken (what was added after the reservation: right after the lift) | the same; testers' charges and disputes decided for them are charged at once | their Charge and Let it go (the suspended time is added to their Charge windows); their Rate. Pay for work they sent still reaches them as usual. |
| Lifts when | everything owed is paid, by a press or an automatic retry (4th hold on: plus an admin's Approve) | card verified, then everything owed reserved on the card with Pay what you owe (never retried on its own; a reservation lasts about 7 days), then the admin's Approve, which takes the reserved money and lifts it in one step (what was added after the reservation never holds it back and is charged right after; reserving alone never lifts it; Don't approve releases the money and the hold stays) | an admin reinstates that side | an admin reinstates that side |
| On lifting | tests resume; deadlines move on; testers get "<test> is running again" | the same | the same | no automatic re-invite: the tests they were removed from stay at their dashboard top ("You were removed from this test", with Ask to rejoin while the test is open with a spot) until they ask or dismiss; the developer can invite them again |

- **Calls:** a call in progress ends at that moment (Call page). Calls starting within 3 days of a hold or suspension starting are cancelled with no fault, and both sides are told. Later calls stay booked; each is cancelled 3 days before it starts if the hold or suspension is still on.
- **Testers in a paused test** (the developer is on any hold or a developer-side suspension, or the team paused that one test): Send, the call-request tools and Join call are hidden (the server still refuses them), under a blue banner listing what still works; drafts can be written and saved. They can still dispute, Charge or Let it go, rate and leave. A paused test never closes the chat: it stays open both ways on a team pause; while the developer is restricted, the tester can read it but not send ("Messages are paused for now. You can still read this chat."). Applications wait, and work again after the lift unless something else closed them; open invites close, and nothing reopens by itself. The 7-day close still applies; after it, Charge, Let it go, dispute and rate carry on until the close-out, and charges added to what is owed stay owed.
- **What the developer can do on a paused test** is the same for every cause: Stop the test and End early (both still need their reasons), Cancel call, excerpts, their side of a dispute and Confirm with your bank, with the same stop-once as on a running test (excerpts and the side of a dispute never stop); on a team pause with nobody held or suspended, Rate too. During a hold or suspension they reach the test's paused view from its dashboard card; Calls can also be cancelled from the dashboard's Calls box.
- **Daily paused reminder** for the developer: "<test> is paused and closes on <date> unless your account is cleared" (for a hold: "…unless you pay what you owe"). It stacks across tests and replaces their "paused" and "closes tomorrow" lines; testers keep theirs.
- **Per side.** A hold limits only the developer side of an account. A suspension covers one side: the developer side or the tester side; to suspend both, an admin suspends each. While it lasts, every signed-in page of that side except the dashboard, Payments, Settings, the bell list (and, for the developer side, the paused view of their test pages) moves them to the dashboard top (details in Every page). Presses are judged by the side they belong to. A tester whose developer is suspended or on hold isn't restricted.

### When account states change or combine

One row per change. A test is paused while its developer has any hold or suspension, so it runs again only when none is left. The lines in the last column are quoted from the bell and email tables on Every page; "bell" and "email" say which.

| What happened | What changes for the developer | What changes for testers | Who is told |
|---|---|---|---|
| A developer-side suspension starts, with no hold | All their tests pause. Every developer page except the dashboard, Payments, Settings, the bell list and the paused view of their test pages moves to the dashboard top, where the blue box says how to appeal. On their tests only Stop the test, End early, Cancel call, excerpts and their side of a dispute work. Calls within 3 days are cancelled with no fault. Their tester side works as normal. | The paused-test rules: Send is refused (drafts save), the chat can be read but not sent to, applications wait and open invites close; dispute, Charge, Let it go, Rate, Leave and cancelling calls work. A Charge, or a dispute decided for them, is charged at once. | Developer: "Your developer account is paused" (bell and email), then daily "<test> is paused and closes on <date> unless your account is cleared" (bell). Testers: the call line for a call cancelled because the test is paused (bell). |
| A tester's Charge, or a dispute decided for them, is declined during a suspension | A payment hold starts on top of the suspension, and the amount goes into "Pay what you owe". Pay can't be pressed during a suspension, so a Pay can't be declined then. | Their payment is delayed; they are paid when the developer pays. | Developer: "Your tests are paused" (bell and email). Testers: "Payment for your work on <test> may be delayed" (bell). |
| A payment hold becomes a chargeback hold | The three chargeback steps on Payments replace Pay what you owe: verify a card with 3D Secure, reserve what is owed with Pay what you owe (it no longer charges at once), then an admin's Approve, which takes it. The chargeback adds its amount plus the $15 fee to what is owed; paying alone never lifts it. Automatic retries stop from that moment. | Nothing new: the test stays paused under the same rules, and Charges and disputes decided for them are still added to what is owed. | Developer: "…paused after a chargeback" (bell) and "…your payment hold is now a chargeback hold" (email). Admins: "Playtest: new chargeback of $X" (email), then the hold in their daily email. |
| Another decline, or another chargeback, during a hold | A decline adds to "Pay what you owe". A chargeback adds its amount plus the $15 fee to what is owed, turns a payment hold into a chargeback hold and clears an earlier card check. A Pay what you owe press for the old amount stops once ("What you owe changed: now $X. Press again to pay $X."). | The payment is delayed and paid when the developer pays. | Developer: "Another payment was declined: $X … was added to what you owe" or "Your bank reversed a $X payment …" (bell). Admins: "Playtest: new chargeback of $X" (email). Testers: "<dev>'s payment for your <part> ($X) is delayed. You'll be told when it arrives." (bell). |
| The hold is paid while the suspension continues | The hold lifts (from the 4th hold on, after an admin's Approve). The tests stay paused and the pages stay closed while the suspension lasts. | The test stays paused. Charges added for them are paid. | Developer: "Receipt: you paid $X for …" (bell). Testers: "<dev> paid $X for your <part>" (bell). Instead of "Your tests are running again": "Your payment went through. Your tests stay paused while your account is suspended." (bell and email). |
| The developer-side suspension lifts while a hold continues | The developer side stays as in a hold: tests paused, pages closed except the paused view, Pay what you owe or the chargeback steps on Payments. | The test stays paused. | Developer: "Your developer account is active again" (bell and email). Testers: nothing until the test runs again. |
| Both have lifted | Tests resume as normal and the pages reopen. Every working tester's deadline and the test's Open until move later by the length of the pause. | Send, chat, call requests and waiting applications work again, unless something else closed them; the developer can invite them again. Their deadline moves later by the length of the pause. | Developer: the line for whichever lifted last, "Your tests are running again" or "Your developer account is active again" (bell and email). Testers: "<test> is running again" (bell). |
| A paused test reaches its 7-day close during a hold or suspension | The test closes like a Stop: the card says "Closed after 7 days paused" and a banner gives the reason. The hold or suspension goes on, and money owed stays owed. Pay opens once it lifts. | "This test ended on <date>." with no reason. Charge, Let it go, dispute and rate carry on; charges added to what is owed stay owed. | Developer: the daily "<test> is paused and closes on <date> unless …" before it, then "<test> was closed. <reason>" (bell and email). Testers: "<test> ended" (bell). |
| The team pauses one test during a hold | The test is paused by both, so it runs again only when the hold has lifted and the team has lifted its pause. It keeps its one closing date, 7 days from when it was first paused, and the card shows both reasons: "Paused: payment hold · paused by the team". | The same paused-test rules. | Developer: "The team paused <test>" (bell and email); later "The team lifted the pause on <test>" (bell). |
| A tester's tester side is suspended while their developer is on hold | The tester leaves every test ("Left this test"). Their sent work stays, waiting for Pay once the hold lifts; pay for it reaches the tester as for anyone. | The suspended tester is removed from every test, and every tester page except the dashboard, Payments, Settings, the bell list and the Dispute-only limited view of those tests closes. Charges already added to what the developer owes are paid to them when the developer pays. | Developer: "<tester> left <test>" (bell). Tester: "Your tester account is paused" (bell and email). |
| The developer deletes their account during a hold | It goes through: a developer can always delete. Deleting never clears a hold, a chargeback or money owed. Parts already open to Charge stay open and charge the saved card; work still under review never opens Charge. | Their tests stop. Parts already open to Charge keep Charge and Let it go; work still under review reads "<dev> deleted their account, so this can't be paid."; open disputes are still decided. | As for any deletion; there is no hold line. |
| During a chargeback hold, the developer presses Pay what you owe, then the team approves | The press reserves the owed total on the card (nothing taken yet): "Reserved $X: it's taken when the team approves lifting your hold." It reserves once: a tester's Charge after that is never reserved and never holds the Approve back ("Reserved $X: … $Y added since will be charged right after."; the admin's row: "$X reserved · $Y added since (charged after the hold lifts)"). The Approve takes the reserved money and lifts the hold in one step; what was added is charged right after the lift as an ordinary charge (bank asks: the confirm box; declined: a payment hold); a suspension stays. | The test stays paused until the Approve. Then the charges added for them are paid (those added after the reservation once their charge goes through), and the test runs again when nothing else pauses it. | Developer: one notice for the Approve and the lift together, "The team approved lifting your hold: the $X reserved on your card was taken, and your tests are running again." (bell and email, even with email Off; with a suspension still on: "The team approved lifting your hold: the $X reserved on your card was taken. Your tests stay paused while your account is suspended."), with no separate lift line or receipt for the reserved money; then a receipt for each charge added after the reservation ("Receipt: you paid $5.00 for Ana Silva's written answers (charged by them after 7 days)"). Testers: "<dev> paid $X for your <part>" (bell). |
| A chargeback hold's reservation isn't approved: the team presses Don't approve, or about 7 days pass | The reservation is released and nothing is taken; the hold stays. The developer presses Pay what you owe again so the team can approve it. | Nothing changes: the test stays paused. | Developer: "The team didn't approve lifting your hold: the $X reserved on your card was released, and nothing was taken." (bell and email, with the team's note in the email), or "Your reservation expired: press Pay what you owe again so the team can approve it." (bell). Admins: "Reservation expired" on the Chargeback holds row. |
| The 4th hold or later is paid | The hold stays until an admin presses Approve (Lifts on the Administrator console); the blue box says the team will review the account after they pay. The tests stay paused until then. | The test stays paused until the Approve. Charges added for them are paid when the developer pays. | Admins: "Hold lift waiting: <dev> paid what they owe (More than 3 holds)" (bell) and the admin daily email. Developer: "The team approved lifting your hold", or "Your tests are running again" when the Approve lifts it (bell; "Your tests are running again" is also emailed). Testers: "<test> is running again" (bell). |
| A subscription renewal is declined | No hold starts. The payment is retried for about a week and the perks continue. After the last retry fails, the subscription ends and they are on the free plan: running tests carry on, new uses of a perk are refused, and an open page gets "Your subscription ended." as a stop-once change. | Nothing changes. | Developer: "Your $7 subscription payment didn't go through. Update your card by <date> to keep it." (bell and email), then "Your subscription ended because the payment didn't go through." (bell and email). |
| A chargeback arrives on a subscription payment | A chargeback hold starts, like any chargeback, and the amount is added to what is owed. | As for any chargeback hold: the developer's tests pause. | As for any chargeback hold (above). |
| A hold or suspension starts while subscribed | Billing pauses: no renewal is charged while it lasts, and the paid days left are kept. They can still cancel or keep it from Payments; Subscribe is refused ("You can subscribe once your account is active again."). | Only the hold or suspension rules above. | The hold or suspension lines above. |
| That hold or suspension lifts | The paid days left resume, so the next renewal moves later by the time it was paused. | Only the lifting rules above. | The lift lines above. |
| A subscriber deletes their account, or the team deletes it | The subscription ends at once, with no refund. The delete warning says: "Your subscription ends now, with no refund for the rest of the month." | As for any deleted developer (Every page). | As for any deletion; there is no subscription line. |
| An automatic retry during a payment hold goes through (there are none during a chargeback hold) | The lines it covers are paid. With nothing left owed, the hold lifts (from the 4th hold on, after Approve); a suspension stays. | Payments for those lines are no longer delayed; the test runs again only when nothing pauses it. | Developer: one line naming what was paid, "We charged your card $10.00 for what you owed: Ana Silva's call on Oct 30." (bell, and the payment email as its receipt; no separate receipt line), then the lift lines above. |
| The platform wins a chargeback | Its amount and the $15 fee come off what is owed, or are refunded to the card if already paid ("Refund: your bank's chargeback of $X was reversed"). If no chargeback is left open, a chargeback hold becomes a payment hold again: anything reserved for the team's approval is released (nothing taken), Pay what you owe charges at once again, and if that was all that was owed, it lifts as usual (from the 4th hold on, after Approve). Open test pages and Edit stop once: "Your bank's chargeback of $X was reversed: $Y refunded to your card." | Nothing changes, unless the hold lifts (above). | Developer: "Your bank's chargeback of $X was reversed in your favour." (bell and email). |
| The bank wants a charge confirmed while the developer isn't there | The payment waits as "needs your confirmation", with Confirm with your bank under the piece on its test page (on Payments for a renewal), and a row in "Your bank wants you to confirm" at the top of the developer dashboard. After 3 days without a confirmation it counts as declined: a payment hold for a charge, a lapse for a renewal. | "Payment may be delayed". | Developer: the one line for every charge waiting for the bank, "Your bank wants you to confirm a $10.00 payment on “Harbor Run” by Nov 2" or "Your bank wants you to confirm 3 payments ($24.50 in all): the first by Nov 2", opening the dashboard's box (bell and email, even with email Off; at most one a day). |
| A tester deletes their account with sent work waiting | Their sent work stays in Needs you with Pay and the usual reminders. Pay reaches their Stripe account if their payout account was connected; if not, those rows leave Needs you with "<name> deleted their account before setting up payouts, so this can't be paid." (DEFAULT). | Unsent work is gone, and so are Charge and Dispute. Their excerpt requests and shown excerpts are removed. | Developer: "<tester> left <test>" (bell). |

### Deletion

- Deleting stops the account's tests, cancels invites and applications, keeps all history, and never clears holds, chargebacks or money owed. A developer can always delete: parts already open to Charge stay open and charge their saved card, and so do listed calls not paid yet, at their automatic charge (a decline leaves the payment delayed for the tester and listed for admins; the platform doesn't cover it; with no saved card such a call can't be paid); work still under review never opens Charge; open disputes are still decided; pending excerpt requests on their tests close, and shown excerpts stay. A tester who deletes leaves every test (calls ahead, invites and applications cancelled, open disputes withdrawn); work they already sent can still be paid by the developer, to their Stripe account if their payout account was connected (if not, those rows leave Needs you, a DEFAULT), but they can't charge for it or dispute it; their excerpt requests and shown excerpts are removed.

### Limits

| What | Limit |
|---|---|
| Spots | 1–10 per test; a test that already had more keeps them (Edit can lower them, never raise them); its Next round starts at 10 |
| Open tests per developer | 1 at a time on the free plan (Open, Full and Paused count; not once joining has closed, DEFAULT); up to 10 with the $7/month subscription |
| Tester deadline | the day they join + the test's tester days (from when the test opened to Open until, in whole days) |
| Open until | required; the last day to join; on Post 3–30 days after today, in the developer's own time zone; on Edit extend only, at most 30 days after the test opened (pause and outage shifts may pass it) |
| Close-out | 14 days after the last tester's deadline (moved by pauses and outages); each piece keeps Charge, Let it go and Pay at least 14 days after its Charge opened (plus any time the tester side was suspended) |
| Pay more | until the piece's cap, and at most until the test's last Pay / Charge closes; a grey "Pay more closes on <date>." in its last 7 days |
| Closing-soon reminders | developer: 1 month (if it was more than a month away when Charge opened), 1 week, 3 days and 1 day before a piece closes, one line a day across tests; tester: one "Charge or let go" line a day while anything is open; both emailed whatever the setting |
| Product, test name | 60 characters each; the test name at least 10 |
| What should testers do | 10–20,000 characters |
| NDA | 1,000 characters (an Edit Save that changes a longer old NDA must fit) |
| Written part | 50,000 characters for all its answers together; each answer to a question at least 50 ("Anything else" has no minimum); up to 5 attachments, 500 MB each (images, PDF, plain text, zip), plus recordings |
| Excerpts | 10–500 characters; at most 3 shown or pending per tester; one pending request per part |
| Categories | at least 1 |
| Written questions | 1–20, each up to 20,000 characters; "At most 20 questions." shows at 20 |
| Part price | written and recording $1–$1,000, or free; over $200 needs a tick |
| Call rate | $10–$200 an hour, or free |
| Any charge | $0.50 to $999,999.99 |
| Pay | any amount from $0; a note is required at $0 and below the listing |
| A piece's cap | the part's max in all, listed or not, Pay, Pay more and dispute raises together: $1,000 for written answers or a recording; $200 an hour × the measured length for a call, within the $1,000 for that tester's calls |
| Dispute decisions | from what the piece was paid up to its cap (the same as Pay and Pay more) |
| All of one tester's calls on a test | $1,000 together, listed and unlisted, extra pay counted (paid plus booked calls at their reference); a booked length can't pass what's left; a call ends when its pay reaches what's left |
| Changes to the steps, questions, languages, devices, build link, and parts and prices | 3 per field, counted from the first join, application or invite; one Save that changes two fields uses one change of each (an admin's Allow 3 more gives 3 more to every field at 0); access keys and the NDA aren't limited (a changed NDA is accepted again) |
| Call length | booked 10–180 min, and no more than the whole minutes left of the $1,000 at the call's rate; measured as the time both browsers were connected (gaps under 5 minutes count), up to 150% of booked and up to the $1,000; nobody changes it |
| Call reminder | 15 minutes before each booked call, to both (none for a call booked less than 15 minutes ahead) |
| Call room | only the booked tester and the test's developer, from 10 minutes before the start until the call ends; a warning 2 minutes before a planned end (150% or the $1,000) |
| Call requests | 3 time rows; repeats 2–8 or Forever; per tester 3 booked times and 3 waiting requests; each time at least 5 minutes ahead (1 minute outside production, or CALL_MIN_AHEAD_MINUTES) |
| Joining a call | 10 minutes before the start to 30 minutes after the booked end; cancelled if nobody joins by halfway; a pause, a stop, a block, a suspension, a leave or End early ends a call in progress, and nobody can join or rejoin after the server ends a call |
| Cancelling a call | a reason is required under 24 hours; not after it starts |
| Tests per tester | 10 at once |
| Recordings | 500 MB each; up to 5 on the Recording part, sent together; a written part's attachments count separately |
| Uploads per account | 3 GB a day ("You've uploaded a lot today. Try again tomorrow."); an upload not finished within 3 hours is removed |
| Names | at least 1 character |
| Age | 18 or older, confirmed at sign-up (email or Google) |
| Reasons and notes | 1,000 characters (the team's reasons and decision notes, Pay notes, the reason for paying $0 or less than the listing) |
| End early's and Stop's reasons | required, 5–10,000 characters |
| Text | why a test is Mature 5–500, rating comment 500, dispute 2,000, the developer's side of a dispute 2,000, bio 1,000, Contact us 10–50,000 characters; report reason at least 5 |
| Contact us | 5 messages per account a day |
| Chat | 30 messages a minute per person |
| Sign-in | 5 failed tries per email or 20 per network in 15 minutes pause sign-in for 15 minutes |
| New accounts | 10 per network an hour |
| Confirmation and reset emails | 3 an hour each; reset requests 10 per network an hour |
| Kept drafts | 50 forms per person (a new one drops the oldest) |
| Reports | one per person about another, ever |
| Disputes | one per part |
| Paused test | closes after 7 days; an admin can extend by 1–90 days |
| Review date (Charge opens) | the later of the tester's own deadline and 7 days after sending (written answers and recordings sent by that deadline; late work never opens Charge) |
| Listed calls | charged automatically 7 days after the call if not paid (moved by pauses and outages); reminders 3 days and 1 day before; no Charge or Let it go |
| Developer review reminders | 1 month (if it was more than a month away), 3, 2 and 1 weeks, 3 days and 1 day before the review date, none within 24 hours of sending; then daily for listed work, none for unlisted |
| Tester deadline reminders | 1 month (if it was more than a month away at joining), 1 week, 3 days and 1 day before each tester's own deadline, none within 24 hours of joining |
| Unlisted calls | one weekly "Pay your calls" line until paid |
| Tester Charge line | one a day across all their tests while any piece is open to Charge or Let it go |
| Developer "Pay now" line | one a day across all their tests while any listed piece is past its review date and open to the tester's Charge |
| Bank-confirmation line | one line for every charge waiting for the bank; at most one a day |
| Invite reminders | daily until answered |
| Rating | tester: no end date until the close-out; developer: 14 days from when their Rate opens (the tester rated and everything is settled, or the close-out for a tester who never rated), paused while they can't rate; reminders every 3 days whatever the test's state, for 2 weeks from when their Rate opened (tester) or inside the 14 days (developer) |
| Payout problem reminders | every 3 days until fixed |
| Hold lifts | the 4th hold on needs an admin's Approve |
| Retries during a payment hold | once a day, at most 7 per hold, and at once when a new card is saved; none during a chargeback hold |
| Bank confirmation | 3 days to confirm a charge made while the developer wasn't there; a pending press locks the piece for up to 15 minutes |
| A payment with no answer | checked after 15 minutes; the same one-time key for 24 hours |
| Chargeback fee | $15 per chargeback, added to what is owed |
| Calls near a hold or suspension | cancelled within 3 days |
| Outage | a reported one of 15 minutes or more moves timers; over 24 hours needs an admin's Approve; a heartbeat gap of 15 minutes or more only waits for an admin's Approve or Dismiss |
| Long lists | blue boxes: 3 lines, then "Show N more" (scroll box about 8 lines); dashboard and profile lists: plain scroll boxes about 4 rows tall; a part's payment lines: a scroll box about 4 lines tall past 4; questions and past calls: more than 3 in a scroll box about 3 tall; admin lists: scroll boxes about 10 rows tall that load 25 more as you near the end |
| Search | name searches from 1 character (1–2 characters: the start of a word or the whole name; 3 or more: any part); Find tests: up to 100 characters; admin Find tests lists the newest 500; admin Accounts and Tests: up to 200 characters |
| Long text | steps and each question: scroll box about 6 lines; reasons and notes: about 4 lines; written answers: about 16 lines; NDA: about 12 lines |
| Big numbers | in full under 10,000, then k, M, B, T, × 10^N |

Open questions about these rules are in QA and open questions.

## Security and launch

What the pre-launch security fixes mean for the people using Playtest, in plain words. How each page shows them is in its own tab.

### Signing in

- A wrong sign-in always says "Wrong email or password", whatever was wrong, so nobody can find out who has an account. "Forgot your password?" always answers the same way too.
- Too many wrong tries pause sign-in for 15 minutes for that email or that network, even with the right password: "Too many tries. Wait 15 minutes or reset your password." New accounts (10 an hour) and reset requests (10 an hour) are limited per network as well.
- **Google into an unconfirmed account.** If someone made an account with an email they don't own, the real owner signing in with Google takes it over safely: the password is cleared, every device is signed out, and any payout setup is dropped before the email counts as confirmed.
- **Sign out of all devices** in Settings ends every session of the account, this device included.

### Admins

- An address on the admin list gets admin access only once its email is confirmed. Registering an admin's address first gives nothing.
- An admin with either side of their account suspended loses admin access ("Your account is paused.").
- Every admin action is kept with the admin who did it; other admins see who, developers and testers only ever see "the team".

### Money

- **A card decline, a missing saved card or a card/customer no longer known to Stripe follows the payment-hold path.** NO_CARD_SAVED identifies the missing-card case for the developer; the tester sees only a payment delay. Any other problem with the payment service fails the press with "Payments are having a problem right now. Nothing was charged; try again shortly.": no hold starts, nothing is owed, and the payment service's own error text is never shown. A subscription renewal that fails for such a reason is simply tried again the next day. Money steps on the database may wait up to 10 seconds to start and run up to 20; a Pay or Pay more that still runs out of time is rolled back with nothing charged and gets the same line, never a server error.
- **A payment that never answered** is checked after 15 minutes and either finished or cleared, never charged twice (Rules).
- **No press counts twice.** Each press carries its own one-time key, made by the app for that person, so a repeated or replayed press never charges twice, and one person's press can never block another person's Charge or the team's decision. A payment event delivered twice by the payment service, or a Confirm pressed twice, counts once. Pay more is checked and saved in one step, so two quick presses can't both go over the cap.
- **Payouts** need a confirmed email, and a tester's payout account counts as ready only once it can receive transfers and pay out.
- **Money ends on a known date.** Every piece closes for Pay and Charge at the test's close-out, never sooner than 14 days after its Charge opened, and both sides are reminded 3 days and 1 day before, by email too (Rules: Close-out).
- **Stripe's current API (2026-09-30).** Every call to the payment service and every field it sends back was checked against this version, and against Stripe's test mode, so no press fails on real Stripe while it works on the fake one:
  - The developer's presses name the card types they accept with `allowed_payment_method_types` (the older `payment_method_types` is refused on this version). The fake payment service used in tests refuses the removed parameter too, so it can't come back unnoticed.
  - An invoice no longer carries its payment or its subscription: the app reads the subscription from the invoice's parent and finds the payment in the invoice's `payments`. Renewals, "invoice paid", "payment failed" and "the bank wants to confirm" all work this way, a renewal's payment is never recorded twice, and a renewal waiting for the bank's check isn't made past due by the failed-payment notice that comes with it.
  - **Subscribe confirms the first payment itself,** with a return address, so when the bank asks, its check shows inline under Subscribe like Pay's; a cancelled check cancels the new subscription (Stripe won't cancel an invoice's payment), and nothing is charged. Confirm with your bank on a renewal (or another charge made while away) fetches the bank's own page the same way.
  - **A tester's share sent after its charge** (one that waited for payout setup, one paid from a chargeback hold's reservation) is a transfer made from that charge (`source_transaction`), one per share: it needs no available balance on the platform (money from a charge is "pending" for days, so a plain transfer would fail) and arrives when that charge's money does. The fake refuses a transfer without it.

### Uploads

- Each file must be exactly the size the browser said it was, and the app checks it once it's uploaded. A file that's missing, the wrong size or too big is deleted: "That upload didn't finish properly. Try again."
- Files are always served as the type they were uploaded as: images and videos open in the page, everything else downloads.
- Removing a recording deletes its file. An upload that fails in the browser is abandoned at once (its row and any partial file go), so a retry starts clean. Uploads not finished within 3 hours are removed by the daily job, and so are files left without their row.
- Each account can upload up to 3 GB a day: "You've uploaded a lot today. Try again tomorrow."

### The live site

- **Settings are checked loudly.** A missing or wrong setting that would make the live site quietly unsafe (payments recorded but never charged, uploads on the server's own disk, emails written to a test outbox, test payment fakes, placeholder admin addresses or keys, a missing site address) stops the deploy, so the old version stays live until it's fixed. Smaller problems show in the red "Settings to fix" box at the top of the admin console, for the owner only.
- **Emails are really sent, and never lost.** On the live site an email is never written to a test outbox, and the test outbox only opens for admins. A missing email key stops the deploy. Every email is stored before it's sent, so one that fails waits and is tried again for 48 hours, then is kept as failed for good (Every page: Emails).
- **The owner hears when emails stop, even while Resend is down.** Besides the red emails box and the bell line on the admin console, the uptime check with the site's key, `/api/health?key=<HEALTH_KEY>`, answers "degraded: emails" once emails have been failing for 15 minutes (the oldest waiting email is that old), and "sending" otherwise. It's still HTTP 200, because the site is up. A second UptimeRobot monitor, of type "keyword", watches that address for "degraded: emails" and alerts the owner through UptimeRobot's own emails and texts. Without the key the answer says nothing about emails.
- **Outages can't be faked.** They come only from the uptime service's own alerts (with the site's key), timed by the app's own clock (an up alert with no down before it counts back the length it carries from when it arrived); alerts more than 10 minutes off are refused, the key can be checked safely, and an outage over 24 hours moves nothing until an admin approves it. The app's own heartbeat never moves anything: a gap only waits for an admin's Approve or Dismiss.
- **Pages are protected in the browser:** they can't be shown inside other sites, and they only load scripts, pictures, videos and the bank's inline check from Playtest, the payment service and the file storage. The only Playtest address that may be shown in a frame is the bank check's return page, and only inside Playtest itself. Forms send only to Playtest, and on to Stripe's own Checkout and Connect pages (checkout.stripe.com, connect.stripe.com), where Add a card and payout setup redirect (the browser applies the form rule to that redirect too).
- **Calls stay between the two people.** The call room is the app's own: video and sound go directly between the two browsers, nothing in the call is recorded, and the server lets only the booked tester and the test's developer in, checking every request (Call page). The browser allows the camera, microphone and screen sharing for Playtest's own pages only, and no outside call service is allowed in a frame.
- **Chat** is limited to 30 messages a minute per person, and kept drafts to 50 forms per person.
- **The uptime monitor must stay on.** Its 5-minute check (with the site's key) also drives the reminders and timers (Rules: When timed jobs run). If it stops, they wait for page loads, polls and the daily run, so a call reminder can come late or not at all. Pausing it no longer moves any timer: with outage checks on, a quiet spell of 15 minutes or more with no server activity at all only shows in the console as a possible outage for an admin to Approve or Dismiss. The webhook alert contact needs UptimeRobot's alertType, alertDateTime and alertDuration variables (HANDOVER), so an up alert can record an outage whose down alert never arrived.
- **Launch steps and at-home checks** are in HANDOVER (the launch checklist) and docs/media/proof/AT-HOME.md: setting up the second, keyword monitor and testing it on staging with a wrong email key; the Stripe test cards (the bank's check, a decline, a chargeback, with a reserved chargeback payment shown "Uncaptured" until Approve); and, right after going live, one real $1 payment between two of the owner's own accounts, refunded afterwards.

## QA and open questions

Local admin QA uses a throwaway account with both sides and nonempty examples for every admin box, including two pending lifts, made through the real server functions. This is local test data, not a product feature; its presence is not implied by this document.

### Edge cases to test before launch

- **Payments:** a declined Pay, Charge, call charge (an automatic one too) or extra pay starting a hold at once; another decline during the hold adding to what is owed; two tabs paying one part; a chargeback with work waiting; a chargeback during a payment hold; a tester or developer suspended mid-payment; a deleted developer with valid charges; a payout failure; an admin raising pay in a dispute; a suspended tester's work paid (Pay, Pay more, a Charge made before, a dispute decision: the share goes out as for anyone); a tester paid before payout setup (waiting, then sent once when the setup is done, with "Your payout setup is done: $X on its way.", a second webhook sending nothing, the admin list read-only); the extra-pay cap with parts outside the contract and unlisted parts;
